Then the uniform bound (
3.5
) follows from

|D\varphi|^{2}<c^{2}(|D\varphi|,\varphi)=\frac{2}{\gamma+1}\big(1+(\gamma-1)(B-\varphi)\big)\,,

where we have used Definition
2.11
(
iii
) and (
3.2
).

3
. The upper bounds in (
3.6
) follow directly from the expression of density (
2.2
) along with (
3.5
).
For the lower bound in
\Omega
, we combine the Bernoulli law (
2.2
) with Definition
2.11
(
iv
) to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}|D\varphi|^{2}+h(\rho)=\frac{1}{2}|D\varphi_{2}|^{2}+(\varphi_{2}-\varphi)+h(\rho_{2})\geq\frac{1}{2}|D\varphi_{2}|^{2}\geq 0\,.

Then, from Definition
2.11
(
iii
), we have

\frac{1}{\gamma-1}\big(\frac{1}{2}(\gamma+1)\rho^{\gamma-1}-1\big)=\frac{1}{2}c^{2}+h(\rho)\geq\frac{1}{2}|D\varphi|^{2}+h(\rho)\geq 0\,,

so that the lower bound of
\rho
in
\Omega
is obtained.
For the lower bound of
\rho
on
\Gamma_{\rm shock}
,
we combine Problem
2.10
(
ii
) and Remark
2.2
(
iii
)
to obtain

\displaystyle\rho=\frac{D\varphi_{2}\cdot\bm{\nu}}{D\varphi\cdot\bm{\nu}}>1\qquad\mbox{on $\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}$}\,.

\displaystyle\hfill\square

3.1.2.
Uniform positive lower bound of
{\rm dist}(\Gamma_{\rm{shock}},\partial B_{1}(O_{2}))

We now obtain a uniform estimate for the positive lower bound of

{\rm{dist}}(\Gamma_{\rm{shock}},\partial B_{1}(O_{2}))
for any admissible solution.
This allows us to make the uniform estimate for the ellipticity of equation (
2.1
) within the pseudo-subsonic domain of admissible solutions.

Proposition 3.4
.

Fix
\gamma\geq 1
and
v_{2}\in(v_{\min},0)
.
There exists a constant
C_{\rm sh}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
such that any admissible solution corresponding to parameters
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta
satisfies

(3.7)

{\rm{dist}}\big(\Gamma_{\rm{shock}},\,\partial B_{1}(O_{2})\big)\geq C_{\rm sh}^{-1}\,.

Proof.

The proof of (
3.7
) follows the same argument
as for
[
2
, Proposition 3.7]
except the case that
v_{2}\leq-1
.
In the following, we focus on the case that
v_{2}\leq-1
and give the proof
in three steps.

1
. We first rewrite equation (
2.1
) as

\mbox{div}\mathcal{A}(D\varphi,\varphi)+\mathcal{B}(D\varphi,\varphi)=0\,,

where

\mathcal{A}(\bm{p},z)\coloneqq\rho(|\bm{p}|,z)\bm{p}
and

\mathcal{B}(|\bm{p}|,z)\coloneqq 2\rho(|\bm{p}|,z)

with
\bm{p}=(p_{1},p_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
z\in\mathbb{R}
, and

(3.8)

\rho(|\bm{p}|,z)=\begin{cases}\big(1+(\gamma-1)(\frac{1}{2}v_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}|\bm{p}|^{2}-z)\big)^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}&\quad\text{if}\;\gamma>1\,,\\
\,\exp(\frac{1}{2}v_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}|\bm{p}|^{2}-z)&\quad\text{if}\;\gamma=1\,.\\
\end{cases}

We denote the sonic speed as
c(|\bm{p}|,z)\coloneqq\rho^{(\gamma-1)/2}
for
\gamma\geq 1
.
For a constant
R>1
, define

(3.9)

\mathcal{K}_{R}\coloneqq\Big\{(\bm{p},z)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}\,:\,|\bm{p}|+|z|\leq R,\,\rho(|\bm{p}|,z)\geq R^{-1},\,\frac{|\bm{p}|^{2}}{c^{2}(|\bm{p}|,z)}\leq 1-R^{-1}\Big\}\,.

For each
R>1
, there exists a constant
\lambda_{R}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},R)
such that,
for any
\bm{\kappa}=(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,

\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\partial_{p_{j}}\mathcal{A}_{i}(\bm{p},z)\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}\geq\lambda_{R}|\bm{\kappa}|^{2}\qquad\,\text{for any $(\bm{p},z)\in\mathcal{K}_{R}$}\,.

The next two lemmas follow directly from
[
2
, Lemmas 3.8–3.9]
.

Lemma 3.5
.

For
R>2,
let
\mathcal{K}_{R}
be given by (
3.9
).
Then there exist functions
(\tilde{\mathcal{A}},\tilde{\mathcal{B}})(\bm{p},z)
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}
satisfying the following properties
:

(i)

For any
(\bm{p}_{0},z_{0})\in\mathcal{K}_{R},

\,(\tilde{\mathcal{A}},\tilde{\mathcal{B}})(\bm{p},z)=(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})(\bm{p},z)

for all
(\bm{p},z)\in B_{\varepsilon}((\bm{p}_{0},z_{0}))
;

(ii)

For any
(\bm{p},z)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}
and
\bm{\kappa}=(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2},

\,\,\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\partial_{p_{j}}\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{i}(\bm{p},z)\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}\geq\lambda_{R}|\bm{\kappa}|^{2}
;

(iii)

|\tilde{\mathcal{B}}(\bm{p},z)|\leq C_{0}

and

|D^{m}_{(\bm{p},z)}(\tilde{\mathcal{A}},\tilde{\mathcal{B}})(\bm{p},z)|\leq C_{m}

in
\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}
for each
m=1,2,\cdots,

where the positive constants

\varepsilon
and
\lambda_{R}
depend only on
(\gamma,v_{2},R)
,

and
C_{m}
depends on
(\gamma,v_{2},R,m)
.

Lemma 3.6
.

Fix
\gamma\geq 1
and
v_{2}\in(v_{\min},0)
.
For any given constants
\alpha\in(0,1),

m\in\mathbb{N},
and
r>0,
there exist constants
C,C_{m}>0

depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\alpha,r),
with
C_{m}
depending additionally on
m,

such that any admissible solution
\varphi
corresponding to parameters

{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta
satisfies the following estimates
:

(i)

For any
B_{4r}(P)\subseteq\Omega,

\displaystyle\|\varphi\|_{2,\alpha,\overline{B_{2r}(P)}}\leq C\,,\qquad\|\varphi\|_{m,\overline{B_{r}(P)}}\leq C_{m}\,;

(ii)

If
P\in\Gamma_{\rm{sym}},
and
B_{4r}(P)\cap\Omega
is the half-ball
B^{+}_{4r}(P)\coloneqq B_{4r}(P)\cap\{\eta>0\},
then

\displaystyle\|\varphi\|_{2,\alpha,\overline{B_{2r}(P)}\cap\Omega}\leq C\,,\qquad\|\varphi\|_{m,\overline{B_{r}(P)}\cap\Omega}\leq C_{m}\,.

2.

Fix
\varepsilon>0
small enough such that
2\xi^{P_{3}}<\xi^{P_{0}}
for all
\theta_{2}\in[0,\varepsilon)
,
where
\xi^{P_{0}}<0
is the
\xi
–coordinate of
P_{0}\coloneqq\lim\limits_{\theta_{2}\to 0^{+}}P_{3}
, which depends only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
.
We define

(3.10)

d_{\rm sep}\coloneqq\min{\big\{{-}\frac{\xi^{P_{0}}}{2},\,\inf\limits_{\theta_{2}\in[\varepsilon,\theta^{\rm d}]}|u_{6}|\big\}}\,.

It is direct to verify that
d_{\rm sep}>0
by using
u_{6}=v_{2}\tan\theta_{26}

and property (
2.38
), and
d_{\rm sep}
depends only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
.
Then, for any
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta
,
{\rm dist}(P_{2},P_{3})\geq 2d_{\rm sep}
.
We also define

(3.11)

r_{1}\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}\inf_{{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta}\big\{|D\varphi_{5}(P_{0}^{1})|,|D\varphi_{6}(P_{0}^{2})|\big\}\,.

By (
2.25
) and Lemma
2.8
,
it is direct to verify that
r_{1}>0
depends only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
.

For any
r\in(0,r_{1})
and for
i=1,2
, we define two sets:

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}\coloneqq\big\{\theta_{i}\in(0,\theta^{\rm d}]\,:\,|P_{0}^{i}-P_{i+1}|\geq\frac{r}{20}\big\}\cup\{0\}\,,\quad\mathscr{B}\coloneqq\big\{\theta_{i}\in[0,\theta^{\rm d}]\,:\,|P_{3i-2}-P_{i+1}|\geq\frac{r}{20}\big\}\,.

From the continuous dependence of
P_{0}^{i},P_{3i-2}
, and
P_{i+1}
on
\theta_{i}\in(0,\theta^{\rm d}]
,
we know that
\mathscr{A}
and
\mathscr{B}
both are closed sets.
Then there exists a constant
C_{1}>0
such that

{\rm dist}(P_{i+1},\Gamma_{\rm{sym}})\geq\frac{2}{C_{1}}\qquad\text{for all $\theta_{i}\in\mathscr{A}\cup\mathscr{B}$}\,.

If
{\rm dist}(P_{i+1},\Gamma_{\rm{sym}})\leq\frac{1}{C_{1}}
,
then
\theta_{i}\notin\mathscr{A}\cup\mathscr{B}
, which implies that

\max\{|P_{0}^{i}-P_{i+1}|,|P_{3i-2}-P_{i+1}|\}<\frac{r}{20}\,.

We now seek a constant
C_{r}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},r)
such that,
for any admissible solution of Problem
2.10
corresponding to parameters
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta
,

(3.12)

\sup\limits_{P\in\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}\cap B_{r/2}(P_{3i-2})}{\rm{dist}}(P,\Gamma_{\rm{sym}})>C_{r}^{-1}\qquad\text{if $|P_{3i-2}-P_{i+1}|\leq\frac{r}{10}$}\,.

To obtain the above result, we follow the proof as in
[
10
, Lemma 9.4.2]
,
in which we need to take a limit of parameters
{\bm{\theta}}^{(n)}
.

In order to take the limit, we use a
compactness lemma below.
For concreteness, in the following, we use the notation defined in Definition
2.9
,
and append superscripts
(n)
and
*
to indicate that the corresponding quantities are related
to parameters
{\bm{\theta}}^{(n)}
and
{\bm{\theta}}^{*}
, respectively.

Lemma 3.7
.

Fix
\gamma\geq 1
and
v_{2}\in(v_{\min},0)
.
Let
\big\{{\bm{\theta}}^{(n)}\big\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subseteq\Theta
be any sequence of parameters satisfying

\,\lim_{n\to\infty}{\bm{\theta}}^{(n)}={\bm{\theta}}^{*}

for some
{\bm{\theta}}^{*}\in\overline{\Theta}
.
For each
n\in\mathbb{N},
let

\varphi^{(n)}
be an admissible solution corresponding to parameters
{\bm{\theta}}^{(n)}
.
Then there exists a subsequence
\{\varphi^{(n_{j})}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
converging uniformly on
any compact subset of
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
to a function
\varphi^{*}\in C^{0,1}_{\rm loc}(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}})
.
Moreover,
\varphi^{*}
is a weak solution of equation (
2.1
)
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
and satisfies the following properties
:

(i)

For the corner points
P_{m},

m=1,2,3,4,
and the reflection points
P_{0}^{i},

i=1,2,

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}P_{m}^{(n_{j})}=P_{m}^{*}\,,\qquad\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}P_{0}^{i,(n_{j})}=P_{0}^{i,*}\,.

Note that
\xi^{P_{0}^{1,*}}=\infty
in the case that
\theta^{*}_{1}=0,
and
\xi^{P_{0}^{2,*}}=-\infty

in the case that
\theta^{*}_{2}=0
.

(ii)

Let
\eta=f_{2l}^{(n_{j})}(\xi),

l=5,6,
and
\eta=f^{(n_{j})}_{\rm{sh}}(\xi)
be the expressions
of
S^{(n_{j})}_{2l}
and
\Gamma^{(n_{j})}_{\rm shock}

from
Definition
2.7
and
Lemma
3.2
,
respectively.
Extend
f_{\rm{sh}}^{(n_{j})}
by

f^{(n_{j})}_{\rm{sh}}(\xi)=\begin{cases}\,f_{25}^{(n_{j})}(\xi)&\quad\text{for $\xi^{P^{(n_{j})}_{2}}<\xi<\xi^{P_{0}^{1,(n_{j})}}$}\,,\\
\,f_{26}^{(n_{j})}(\xi)&\quad\text{for $\xi^{P_{0}^{2,(n_{j})}}<\xi<\xi^{P_{3}^{(n_{j})}}$}\,.\end{cases}

Then

\{f^{(n_{j})}_{\rm{sh}}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
converges uniformly
to a function
f^{*}_{\rm{sh}}\in C^{0,1}([\xi^{P_{0}^{2,*}},\xi^{P_{0}^{1,*}}])
.

(iii)

Let smooth functions
\eta=g^{(n_{j})}_{l,\rm{so}}(\xi)

be the expressions of
\Gamma^{l,(n_{j})}_{\rm{sonic}}
for
l=5,6
.
Then
\{g^{(n_{j})}_{5,\rm{so}}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
converges pointwise
to
g^{*}_{5,\rm{so}}
on
(\xi^{P^{*}_{2}},\xi^{P^{*}_{1}}),
and
\{g^{(n_{j})}_{6,\rm{so}}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}

converges pointwise to
g^{*}_{6,\rm{so}}
on
(\xi^{P^{*}_{4}},\xi^{P^{*}_{3}})
.

(iv)

Denote

\widehat{\Omega^{*}}\coloneqq\big\{\bm{\xi}\in[\xi^{P^{*}_{4}},\xi^{P^{*}_{1}}]\times\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\,:\,0\leq\eta\leq f^{*}_{\rm{bd}}(\xi)\big\},

where
f^{*}_{\rm{bd}}(\cdot)
is given by

f^{*}_{\rm{bd}}(\xi)=\begin{cases}\,g^{*}_{6,\rm{so}}(\xi)&\quad\text{for $\xi^{P^{*}_{4}}\leq\xi\leq\xi^{P^{*}_{3}}$}\,,\\
\,f^{*}_{\rm{sh}}(\xi)\qquad&\quad\text{for $\xi^{P^{*}_{3}}<\xi\leq\xi^{P_{2}}$}\,,\\
\,g^{*}_{5,\rm{so}}(\xi)\qquad&\quad\text{for $\xi^{P^{*}_{2}}<\xi\leq\xi^{P^{*}_{1}}$}\,.\end{cases}

Denote by
\Omega^{*}
the interior of
\widehat{\Omega^{*}},

\Gamma^{*}_{\rm{shock}}\coloneqq\{(\xi,f^{*}_{\rm{sh}}(\xi))\,:\,\xi\in(\xi^{P^{*}_{3}},\xi^{P^{*}_{2}})\},

and
\Gamma^{*}_{\rm{sym}}\coloneqq\{(\xi,0)\,:\,\xi\in(\xi^{P^{*}_{4}},\xi^{P^{*}_{1}})\},

and denote by
\Gamma^{*,0}_{\rm{sym}}
the relative interior of
\Gamma^{*}_{\rm{sym}}\setminus\Gamma^{*}_{\rm{shock}}
.
Then
\varphi^{*}\in C^{\infty}(\Omega^{*}\cup\Gamma^{*,0}_{\rm{sym}}),
and

(
iv
.1)

\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\|\varphi^{(n_{j})}-\varphi^{*}\|_{C^{2}(K)}=0\,
for any
K\Subset\Omega^{*}\cup\Gamma^{*,0}_{\rm{sym}}
,

(
iv
.2)

D(\varphi_{2}-\varphi^{*})\cdot(\cos\theta,\sin\theta)\leq 0\,
for all
\theta\in(\theta^{*}_{26},\theta^{*}_{25})
,

(
iv
.3)

Equation (
2.1
) is strictly elliptic in
\Omega^{*}\cup\Gamma^{*,0}_{\rm{sym}}
,

(
iv
.4)

In
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\setminus\Omega^{*},

\varphi^{*}
is equal to the constant states
\bar{\varphi}^{*},

\varphi^{*}_{5},

and
\varphi^{*}_{6}
in their respective domains as given
in
Definition
2.11
(
ii
),

where
\bar{\varphi}^{*}
is given by (
2.20
) with
{\bm{\theta}}={\bm{\theta}}^{*}
.

(v)

\Gamma_{\rm{sym}}^{*,0}=\{\varphi_{2}>\varphi^{*}\}\cap\Gamma_{\rm{sym}}^{*}
is dense in
\Gamma_{\rm{sym}}^{*}
.

Using Lemmas
3.3
and
3.6
,
properties (
i
)–(
iv
) in Lemma
3.7

can be proved by
similar arguments
as for
[
2
, Corollary 3.10]
.

For property (
v
), if it is not true,
we can choose
\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\in(\xi^{P_{3}^{*}},\xi^{P^{*}_{2}})
such that

\{(\xi,0)\,:\,\xi_{1}<\xi<\xi_{2}\}\subseteq\{\varphi_{2}\leq\varphi^{*}\}\cap\Gamma_{\rm{sym}}^{*}
.
By Definition
2.11
(
iv
),

\varphi^{(n_{j})}\leq\varphi_{2}
in
\Omega^{(n_{j})}
so that

\varphi^{*}\leq\varphi_{2}
in
\overline{\Omega^{*}}
.
Combining with property (iv.4), we obtain that
\varphi^{*}(\bm{\xi})=\varphi_{2}(\bm{\xi})
on
\{\bm{\xi}\in\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}:\xi_{1}<\xi_{2}\}
.
We take any non-negative function
\zeta\in C^{\infty}_{\rm c}(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}})

satisfying that
{\rm supp}\,\zeta\subseteq\{\bm{\xi}\in\overline{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\,:\,\xi_{1}<\xi<\xi_{2}\}

and
\zeta>0
on some subset of
\{(\xi,0)\,:\,\xi_{1}<\xi<\xi_{2}\}
with positive measure.
Then

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}(\rho(|D\varphi^{*}|,\varphi^{*})D\varphi^{*}\cdot D\zeta(\bm{\xi})-2\rho(|D\varphi^{*}|,\varphi^{*})\zeta(\bm{\xi}))\,{\rm d}\bm{\xi}

\displaystyle\;\;=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}(\rho_{2}D\varphi_{2}\cdot D\zeta(\bm{\xi})-2\rho_{2}\zeta(\bm{\xi}))\,{\rm d}\bm{\xi}=-v_{2}\int_{\xi_{1}}^{\xi_{2}}\zeta(\xi,0)\,{\rm d}\xi>0\,,

which contradicts the fact that
\varphi^{*}
is a weak solution of
equation (
2.1
) in
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
.
Then Lemma
3.7
(
v
) is verified.

We prove (
3.12
) by
contradiction. If it is not true, we can choose a sequence of parameters
\{{\bm{\theta}}^{(n)}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that

\sup\limits_{P\in\Gamma^{(n)}_{\rm{shock}}\cap B_{r/2}(P^{(n)}_{3i-2})}{\rm{dist}}(P,\Gamma^{(n)}_{\rm{sym}})\leq\frac{1}{n}\qquad\text{if $|P^{(n)}_{3i-2}-P^{(n)}_{i+1}|\leq\frac{r}{10}$}\,.

Then, by Lemma
3.7
, we can further choose
a subsequence
\{{\bm{\theta}}^{(n_{j})}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
and take a limit
to some
{\bm{\theta}}^{*}\in\overline{\Theta}
, which provides a contradiction
to Lemma
3.7
(
v
).

From (
3.12
), we can choose
\hat{Q}_{i}\in\overline{\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}\cap B_{r/2}(P_{3i-2})}

with
{\rm dist}(\hat{Q}_{i},\Gamma_{\rm{sym}})\geq C_{r}^{-1}
,
so that

\hat{Q}_{i}\in\overline{\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap B_{3r/4}(P_{0}^{i})}
.
For
i=1,2
, we take
Q_{i}=P_{i+1}\in\overline{\Gamma_{\rm shock}}

if
{\rm dist}(P_{i+1},\Gamma_{\rm sym})>C_{1}^{-1}
;
otherwise, we take
Q_{i}=\hat{Q}_{i}\in\overline{\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap B_{3r/4}(P_{0}^{i})}
as chosen above.
Then it follows that

\min\{{\rm dist}(Q_{1},Q_{2}),\,{\rm dist}(Q_{1},\Gamma_{\rm sym}),\,{\rm dist}(Q_{2},\Gamma_{\rm sym})\}\geq\min\{d_{\rm sep},\,C_{1}^{-1},\,C_{r}^{-1}\}\eqqcolon a\,.

Combining with Lemma
3.7
and following a similar proof
as for
[
10
, Lemmas 9.4.1 and 15.4.1]
, we obtain that,
for

a>0
defined above, there exists a constant
C_{2}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},a)

such that, for any admissible solution
corresponding to parameters
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta
,

{\rm dist}(\Gamma_{\rm shock}[Q_{1},Q_{2}],\,\Gamma_{\rm sym})\geq C_{2}^{-1}\,,

where
\Gamma_{\rm shock}[Q_{1},Q_{2}]
represents the segment on
\Gamma_{\rm shock}
between points
Q_{1}
and
Q_{2}
.
Therefore,

(3.13)

{\rm dist}(\Gamma_{\rm shock}\setminus(B_{r}(P_{0}^{1})\cup B_{r}(P_{0}^{2})),\,\Gamma_{\rm{sym}})>C_{2}^{-1}\,.

3.
Since
v_{2}\leq-1
,
B_{1}(O_{2})\subseteq\mathbb{R}\times(-\infty,0)
.
By Lemma
2.3
and property (
ii
) of Proposition
2.6
,

d_{\rm ref}\coloneqq|D\varphi_{2}(P_{0}^{1}|_{\theta_{1}=\theta^{\rm d}})|-1>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
satisfies

\displaystyle{\rm dist}(P_{0}^{1},\,B_{1}(O_{2}))=|D\varphi_{2}(P_{0}^{1})|-1\geq d_{\rm ref}\,.

By symmetry, we also see that
{\rm dist}(P_{0}^{2},\,B_{1}(O_{2}))\geq d_{\rm ref}
.
Denote
\bar{r}\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}\min\{r_{1},d_{\rm ref}\}>0

with
r_{1}
defined by (
3.11
). Then

{\rm dist}(\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap(B_{\bar{r}}(P_{0}^{1})\cup B_{\bar{r}}(P_{0}^{2})),\,B_{1}(O_{2}))\geq\bar{r}\,.

Meanwhile, from the argument in Step
2
, there exists a constant
C_{2}>0

depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\bar{r})
such that (
3.13
) holds
with
r
replaced by
\bar{r}
.
Thus, for any admissible solution corresponding to parameters
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta
, we have

\displaystyle{\rm dist}(\Gamma_{\rm shock},\,B_{1}(O_{2}))\geq\min\{\bar{r},\,C_{2}^{-1}\}\eqqcolon C_{\rm sh}^{-1}\,.

This completes the proof of (
3.7
).

\square

3.1.3.
Uniform estimate for the ellipticity of equation (
3.1
)

We now show a uniform estimate for the ellipticity of equation (
3.1
),
which may be degenerate near
\Gamma_{\rm{sonic}}^{5}\cup\Gamma_{\rm{sonic}}^{6}
.
Fix a function
h^{\ast}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
such that

h^{\ast}(s)=\begin{cases}\,s&\quad\text{for $s\leq\frac{1}{2}$}\,,\\[2.84526pt]
\,1&\quad\text{for $s\geq 1$}\,,\end{cases}

satisfying
0\leq(h^{\ast})^{\prime}(\cdot)\leq 2
on
\mathbb{R}
.
For each
{\bm{\theta}}\in\overline{\Theta}
, define

(3.14)

\hat{c}_{j}\coloneqq\min{\{c_{j},\,|D\varphi_{j}(P_{0}^{j-4})|\}}\,,\quad q_{j}\coloneqq{\rm dist}(O_{j},\,S_{2j})\qquad\,\mbox{for $j=5,6$}\,.

By Lemma
2.8
and
[
10
, Lemma 6.1.2]
,
we see that
\hat{c}_{j}>q_{j}
for every
{\bm{\theta}}\in\overline{\Theta}
, so that
there exists a constant
\hat{\delta}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
such that

(3.15)

\inf_{{\bm{\theta}}\in\overline{\Theta}}\min\{\hat{c}_{5}-q_{5},\,\hat{c}_{6}-q_{6}\}\geq\hat{\delta}\,.

For
j=5,6
, define
g^{\ast}_{j}:\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
by

g^{\ast}_{j}(\bm{\xi})\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}(\hat{c}_{j}-q_{j})h^{\ast}(\frac{{\rm{dist}}(\bm{\xi},\,\partial B_{\hat{c}_{j}}(O_{j}))}{\hat{c}_{j}-q_{j}})+\big(1-\frac{\hat{c}_{j}^{2}}{c^{2}_{j}}\big)\,.

We choose a function
\chi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
such that

\chi(\xi)=\begin{cases}\,1&\quad\text{for}\;\xi\leq-\tfrac{d_{\rm sep}}{4}\,,\\
\,0&\quad\text{for}\;\xi\geq\tfrac{d_{\rm sep}}{4}\,,\end{cases}

satisfying
-\frac{4}{d_{\rm sep}}\leq\chi^{\prime}(\cdot)\leq 0
on
\mathbb{R}
,
where
d_{\rm sep}>0
is given by (
3.10
) depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
.
Finally, define a function
g:\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
by

(3.16)

g(\bm{\xi})\coloneqq\chi(\xi)g_{6}^{\ast}(\bm{\xi})+(1-\chi(\xi))g_{5}^{\ast}(\bm{\xi})\,.

By Definition
2.11
and Lemma
3.3
,
there exist constants
d>0
and
C>1
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
such that,
if
\varphi
is an admissible solution corresponding to
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta

with pseudo-subsonic region
\Omega
,
then
g
given by (
3.16
) satisfies the following properties:

(a)

For
\bm{\xi}\in\Omega
satisfying
{\rm{dist}}(\bm{\xi},\Gamma^{j}_{\rm{sonic}})<d
,

C^{-1}{\rm{dist}}_{j}(\bm{\xi},\,\Gamma_{\rm{sonic}}^{j})\leq g(\bm{\xi})\leq C\,{\rm{dist}}_{j}(\bm{\xi},\,\Gamma_{\rm{sonic}}^{j})
,
where
{\rm{dist}}_{j}(\bm{\xi},\,\Gamma_{\rm{sonic}}^{j})\coloneqq{\rm{dist}}(\bm{\xi},\,\Gamma_{\rm{sonic}}^{j})+(c_{j}-\hat{c}_{j})
for
j=5,6
.

(b)

For each
\varepsilon>0
, there exists a constant
C_{\varepsilon}>1
depending only
on
(\gamma,v_{2},\varepsilon)
such that, if a point
\bm{\xi}\in\overline{\Omega}

satisfies
{\rm{dist}}(\bm{\xi},\,\Gamma_{\rm{sonic}}^{5}\cup\Gamma_{\rm{sonic}}^{6})>\varepsilon
,
then
C_{\varepsilon}^{-1}\leq g(\bm{\xi})\leq C_{\varepsilon}
.

In fact, for property (
a
) above, it suffices
to choose
d\coloneqq\min\{\tfrac{d_{\rm sep}}{2},\hat{\delta}\}
,
with constants
d_{\rm sep}
and
\hat{\delta}
given by (
3.10
) and (
3.15
)
respectively.
Then, choosing
\varepsilon\coloneqq\tfrac{d}{2}
in property (
b
),
we deduce that there exists a constant
{C}_{\flat}>1
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
such that,
for any
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta
,

(3.17)

{C}_{\flat}^{-1}{\rm{dist}}^{\flat}(\bm{\xi},\,\Gamma^{5}_{\rm{sonic}}\cup\Gamma^{6}_{\rm{sonic}})\leq g(\bm{\xi})\leq{C}_{\flat}\,{\rm{dist}}^{\flat}(\bm{\xi},\,\Gamma^{5}_{\rm{sonic}}\cup\Gamma^{6}_{\rm{sonic}})\qquad\text{for all $\bm{\xi}\in\overline{\Omega}$}\,,

where

(3.18)

{\rm{dist}}^{\flat}(\bm{\xi},\,\Gamma^{5}_{\rm{sonic}}\cup\Gamma^{6}_{\rm{sonic}})\coloneqq\min\big\{\frac{d}{2},\,{\rm{dist}}_{5}(\bm{\xi},\,\Gamma_{\rm{sonic}}^{5}),\,{\rm{dist}}_{6}(\bm{\xi},\,\Gamma_{\rm{sonic}}^{6})\big\}\,.

Proposition 3.8
.

There exists a constant
\mu>0
such that, if
\varphi
is an admissible solution corresponding
to
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta
and
\Omega
is its pseudo-subsonic region, then the pseudo-Mach number

M(\bm{\xi})\coloneqq\frac{|D\varphi(\bm{\xi})|}{c(|D\varphi(\bm{\xi})|,\varphi(\bm{\xi}))}

satisfies

(3.19)

M^{2}(\bm{\xi})\leq 1-\mu g(\bm{\xi})\qquad\text{in $\overline{\Omega}$}\,,

and there exists a constant
C>1
such that

(3.20)

C^{-1}{\rm{dist}}^{\flat}(\bm{\xi},\,\Gamma^{5}_{\rm{sonic}}\cup\Gamma^{6}_{\rm{sonic}})|\bm{\kappa}|^{2}\leq\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\mathcal{A}^{i}_{p_{j}}(D\varphi(\bm{\xi}),\varphi(\bm{\xi}))\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}\leq C|\bm{\kappa}|^{2}

for all
\bm{\xi}\in\overline{\Omega}
and
\bm{\kappa}=(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
where
\mathcal{A}(\bm{p},z)=\rho(|\bm{p}|,z)\bm{p},

{\rm{dist}}^{\flat}(\mathrel{\,\cdot\,},\mathrel{\,\cdot\,})
is given by (
3.18
)
,
and
constants
\mu
and
C
depend only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
.

Proof.

Let
\varphi
be any admissible solution corresponding to
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta

with pseudo-subsonic region
\Omega
and curved transonic shock
\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}
.

1.

From Lemma
3.3
, there exists a constant
R>1

depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})

such that

\Omega\subseteq B_{R}(\bm{0})\,,\qquad\|c(|D\varphi|,\varphi)\|_{C^{0}(\overline{\Omega})}+\|g\|_{C^{2}(\overline{\Omega})}\leq R\,,

where
g(\cdot)
is given by (
3.16
).
Since
O_{5},O_{6}\in\Gamma_{\rm sym}
,

\partial_{\eta}g=0
on
\Gamma_{\rm{sym}}
.
Then, by
[
10
, Theorem 5.3.1]
, we can choose
constants
C_{0}>1
,
\delta\in(0,\frac{3}{4C_{0}})
, and
\mu_{1}\in(0,1)

depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
such that, for any
\mu\in(0,\mu_{1}]
,
either inequality
M^{2}+\mu g\leq C_{0}\delta<1
holds in
\Omega

or the maximum of
M^{2}+\mu g
over
\overline{\Omega}
cannot
be attained in
\Omega\cup\Gamma_{\rm{sym}}
.

In the first case, (
3.19
) follows immediately.
Thus, we focus on the second case that the maximum of
M^{2}+\mu g
must
be attained on
\partial\Omega\setminus\Gamma_{\rm{sym}}
.

2.

We claim that there exists a constant
\mu_{2}\in(0,\mu_{1}]
depending only
on
(\gamma,v_{2})
such that, whenever
\mu\in(0,\mu_{2}]
,

(M^{2}+\mu g)\leq 1
holds in
\Omega
, from which (
3.19
) follows.

Let the maximum of
M^{2}+\mu g
be attained
at
P_{\rm{max}}\in\partial\Omega\setminus\Gamma_{\rm{sym}}
,
and let
(M^{2}+\mu g)(P_{\rm{max}})>1
.
Then, using Proposition
3.4
, we may proceed as in Steps
2
–
3

of the proof of
[
2
, Proposition 3.15]

to find
\mu_{2}\in(0,\mu_{1}]
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})

such that
P_{\rm max}\notin\Gamma_{\rm shock}
when

\mu\in(0,\mu_{2}]
.
Therefore,
P_{\rm max}\in\Gamma^{5}_{\rm sonic}\cup\Gamma^{6}_{\rm sonic}
.
However, reducing
\mu_{2}
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
if necessary, we have

(M^{2}+\mu g)(P_{\rm max})=\sup_{\bm{\xi}\in\Gamma^{5}_{\rm sonic}\cup\Gamma^{6}_{\rm sonic}}(M^{2}+\mu g)(\bm{\xi})\leq 1\,,

which is a contradiction.
In the final inequality above,
we have used that
g|_{\Gamma^{j}_{\rm sonic}}=0
for
\theta_{j-4}\in[0,\theta^{\rm s})
,
whilst
g|_{\Gamma^{j}_{\rm sonic}}\leq C_{\flat}(c_{j}-|D\varphi_{j}(P_{0}^{j-4})|)

for
\theta_{j-4}\in[\theta^{\rm s},\theta^{\rm d})
, for
j=5,6
.

\square

3.2.
Interior Hölder estimates away from the sonic boundaries

For any set
U\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{2}
and any constant
\varepsilon>0
, define
the
\varepsilon
-neighbourhood of
U
as

(3.21)

\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}(U)\coloneqq\{\bm{\xi}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\,:\,{\rm dist}(\bm{\xi},\,U)<\varepsilon\}\,.

From Proposition
3.4
,
there exists a constant
C_{\rm sh}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
such that

{\rm dist}(\Gamma_{\rm shock},\,\partial B_{1}(O_{2}))\geq C_{\rm sh}^{-1}

for any admissible solution and parameters
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta
.
It follows that

|D\varphi_{2}(\bm{\xi})|^{2}\geq 1+d_{0}\qquad\text{for any $\bm{\xi}\in\mathcal{N}_{\tfrac{1}{2C_{\rm sh}}}(\Gamma_{\rm{shock}})$}

for some constant
d_{0}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
.
Subsequently, we claim that there exist constants
d_{0}^{\prime}\in(0,d_{0})
and
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{2C_{\rm sh}})

depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
such that, for any admissible solution
\varphi
,

(3.22)

|D\varphi_{2}(\bm{\xi})|-|D\varphi(\bm{\xi})|\geq d_{0}^{\prime}\qquad\,\text{for any $\bm{\xi}\in\overline{\Omega}\cap\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}(\Gamma_{\rm shock})$}\,.

Indeed, in the case:
\gamma>1
, from Definition
2.11
(
iii
)–(
iv
)
and the Bernoulli law:

\frac{1}{2}|D\varphi|^{2}+\varphi+\frac{\rho^{\gamma-1}-1}{\gamma-1}=B=\frac{1}{2}|D\varphi_{2}|^{2}+\varphi_{2}\,,

we have

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma+1}{2}\left(\rho^{\gamma-1}-1\right)\geq

\displaystyle\left(\rho^{\gamma-1}-1\right)+\frac{\gamma-1}{2}\left(|D\varphi|^{2}-1\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\gamma-1)(\varphi_{2}-\varphi)+\frac{\gamma-1}{2}\left(|D\varphi_{2}|^{2}-1\right)\geq\frac{\gamma-1}{2}d_{0}\,,

and

|D\varphi_{2}|^{2}-|D\varphi|^{2}=\frac{2\left(\rho^{\gamma-1}-1\right)}{\gamma-1}+(\varphi-\varphi_{2})\geq\frac{2d_{0}}{\gamma+1}+(\varphi-\varphi_{2})\,.

Since
\varphi
is Lipschitz continuous in
\overline{\Omega}
from Lemma
3.3
,
and
\varphi=\varphi_{2}
on
\Gamma_{\rm shock}
, we obtain

|D\varphi_{2}(\bm{\xi})|^{2}-|D\varphi(\bm{\xi})|^{2}\geq\frac{d_{0}}{\gamma+1}\qquad\text{for any $\bm{\xi}\in\overline{\Omega}\cap\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}(\Gamma_{\rm shock})$}\,,

with some
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{2C_{\rm sh}})
sufficiently small.
Then (
3.22
) follows from the above inequality and the uniform boundedness property in Lemma
3.3
.
In the case:
\gamma=1
, it is straightforward to obtain (
3.22
)
by using Definition
2.11
(
iii
):
for any admissible solution
\varphi
,

|D\varphi|^{2}\leq c^{2}(|D\varphi|,\varphi)\equiv 1
in
\overline{\Omega}
, since
the sonic speed is a constant.

For
\bm{\xi}=(\xi,\eta)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\{O_{2}\}
, define the polar coordinates
(r,\theta)
centred at
O_{2}=(u_{2},v_{2})
by

\displaystyle r(\cos\theta,\sin\theta)\coloneqq(\xi,\eta)-O_{2}\,.

It follows from (
3.22
) that

(3.23)

\partial_{r}(\varphi_{2}-\varphi)\leq-(|D\varphi_{2}|-|D\varphi|)\leq-d_{0}^{\prime}\qquad\text{in $\overline{\Omega}\cap\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}(\Gamma_{\rm shock})$}\,.

Therefore, we may apply the implicit function theorem to
express
\Gamma_{\rm shock}
under these polar coordinates as

\Gamma_{\rm shock}=\{\bm{\xi}(r,\theta)\,:\,r=f_{O_{2},\rm{sh}}(\theta),\,\theta_{P_{2}}<\theta<\theta_{P_{3}}\}\,,

where
(f_{O_{2},\rm{sh}}(\theta_{P_{i}}),\theta_{P_{i}})
are the polar coordinates of points
P_{i}
, for
i=2,3
.
By Lemma
3.3
and (
3.23
),
there exists a constant
C_{1}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
such that

\|f_{O_{2},\rm{sh}}\|_{C^{0,1}\left([\theta_{P_{2}},\theta_{P_{3}}]\right)}\leq C_{1}\,.

From the above, with the help of Lemma
3.5
,
we deduce the following properties; the proof is similar to that of
[
2
, Lemmas 3.17–3.18]
.

Lemma 3.9
.

Fix
\gamma\geq 1
and
v_{2}\in(v_{\min},0)
.
Let
\varphi
be an admissible solution corresponding to parameters
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta
.

(i)

There exists a constant
\delta^{\prime}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
such that

\partial_{\bm{\nu}}\varphi_{2}-\delta^{\prime}>\partial_{\bm{\nu}}\varphi\geq\delta^{\prime}\qquad\text{on $\overline{\Gamma_{\rm shock}}$}\,,

where
\bm{\nu}=\frac{D(\varphi_{2}-\varphi)}{|D(\varphi_{2}-\varphi)|}

is the unit normal vector on
\Gamma_{\rm shock}
pointing into the interior of
\Omega
.

(ii)

For each
d>0
and
m=2,3,\cdots,
there exist positive constants
s
and
C_{m}

depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},d)
such that,
whenever
P=\bm{\xi}(r_{P},\theta_{P})\in\Gamma_{\rm shock}
satisfies

{\rm{dist}}(P,\Gamma_{\rm sonic}^{5}\cup\Gamma_{\rm sonic}^{6})\geq d,

\displaystyle|f^{(m)}_{O_{2},\rm{sh}}(\theta_{P})|+\sup_{B_{s}(P)\cap\Omega}|D^{m}\varphi|\leq C_{m}\,.

From Lemmas
3.6
,
3.8
, and
3.9
(
ii
),
we have the following
a priori
interior estimates.

Corollary 3.10
.

Fix
\gamma\geq 1
and
v_{2}\in(v_{\min},0)
.
For each
d>0
and
m=2,3,\cdots,

there exists a constant
C_{m,d}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},m,d)
such that
any admissible solution
\varphi
corresponding to
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta
satisfies

\displaystyle\|\varphi\|_{m,\overline{\Omega\cap\{{\rm dist}(\bm{\xi},\Gamma_{\rm sonic}^{5}\cup\Gamma_{\rm{sonic}}^{6})>d\}}}\leq C_{m,d}\,.

3.3.
Weighted Hölder estimates near the sonic boundaries

From Definition
2.9
, the sonic boundary
\Gamma_{\rm sonic}^{5}
depends continuously
on
\theta_{1}\in[0,\theta^{\rm d})
and shrinks into a single point
P_{0}^{1}=P_{1}=P_{2}

when
\theta_{1}\in[0,\theta^{\rm s})
tends to
\theta^{\rm s}
from below (if additionally
v_{2}\in[v_{2}^{\rm s},0)
).
Moreover, from Proposition
3.8
,
the ellipticity of equation (
3.1
) degenerates on the sonic boundary

\Gamma_{\rm sonic}^{5}
for
\theta_{1}\in[0,\theta^{\rm s})
,
while equation (
3.1
) is uniformly elliptic in the case that

\theta_{1}\in(\theta^{\rm s},\theta^{\rm d})
up to
\Gamma_{\rm sonic}^{5}
away from
\Gamma_{\rm sonic}^{6}
.

The above reasons lead us to consider the following four cases separately
for the weighted Hölder estimates of the admissible solutions near
\Gamma^{5}_{\rm sonic}
.
Since
\theta^{\rm s}
can be equal to
\theta^{\rm cr}
depending on
(\gamma,v_{2})
,
some of the following cases may not
occur (
cf.
Remark
2.1
(
i
)):

(1)

Case 1.

{\theta}_{1}\in[0,{\theta}^{\rm{s}})
away from
{\theta}^{\rm{s}}
;

(2)

Case 2.

{\theta}_{1}\in[0,{\theta}^{\rm{s}})
close to
{\theta}^{\rm{s}}
;

(3)

Case 3.

{\theta}_{1}\in[{\theta}^{\rm{s}},{\theta}^{\rm{d}})
close to
{\theta}^{\rm{s}}
;

(4)

Case 4.

{\theta}_{1}\in[{\theta}^{\rm{s}},{\theta}^{\rm{d}})
away from
{\theta}^{\rm{s}}
.

Corresponding weighted
C^{2,\alpha}
–estimates near
\Gamma^{6}_{\rm sonic}
for the four cases with respect
to
\theta_{2}\in[0,\theta^{\rm d})
are obtained immediately due to the symmetry of our problem.

For any
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta
, define an open set
\Omega^{5}\subseteq\Omega

by

\Omega^{5}\coloneqq\big(\Omega\cap\{\bm{\xi}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\,:\,\xi>u_{5}+q_{5}\sin\theta_{25}\}\big)\setminus\overline{B_{c_{5}^{*}}(O_{5})}\,,

where
q_{5}={\rm dist}(O_{5},S_{25})
is given by (
3.14
), and

c_{5}^{*}\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}\big(|O_{5}P_{2}|+{\rm dist}(O_{5},S_{25})\big)
.
For
\bm{\xi}\in\overline{\Omega^{5}}
, we introduce the modified polar coordinates
(x,y)
centred at
O_{5}
defined by

(3.24)

(x,y)\coloneqq\mathcal{R}(\xi,\eta)\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad(\xi,\eta)-O_{5}=(c_{5}-x)(\cos y,\sin y)\qquad\text{with $y\in[0,\tfrac{\pi}{2}]$}\,.

It is clear that
\mathcal{R}(\Omega^{5})\subseteq\{(x,y)\,:\,0<x<c_{5}-c_{5}^{*}\}
.
For the pseudo-potential
\varphi_{5}
given by (
2.33
), we have

\varphi_{5}\circ\mathcal{R}^{-1}(x,y)=-\frac{1}{2}(c_{5}-x)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}u_{5}^{2}-u_{5}\xi^{P_{0}^{1}}\qquad\text{for $(x,y)\in\mathcal{R}(\Omega^{5})$}\,.

Note that
u_{5}\xi^{P_{0}^{1}}\to-v_{2}\eta_{0}
as
\theta_{1}\to 0^{+}
, where
\eta_{0}>0

is defined in §
2.1.3
.

For any admissible solution
\varphi
corresponding to parameters
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta
, let
\psi
be given by

\psi(x,y)\coloneqq(\varphi-\varphi_{5})\circ\mathcal{R}^{-1}(x,y)\qquad\text{for $(x,y)\in\mathcal{R}(\Omega^{5})$}\,.

Then
\psi
satisfies a free boundary problem for an elliptic equation:

(i)

Equation for
\psi
in
\mathcal{R}(\Omega^{5})
:
Direct calculation gives

(3.25)

\mathcal{N}(\psi)=\big(2x-(\gamma+1)\psi_{x}+\mathcal{O}_{1}\big)\psi_{xx}+\mathcal{O}_{2}\psi_{xy}+\big(\frac{1}{c_{5}}+\mathcal{O}_{3}\big)\psi_{yy}-\big(1+\mathcal{O}_{4}\big)\psi_{x}+\mathcal{O}_{5}\psi_{y}=0\,,

with
\mathcal{O}_{j}=\mathcal{O}_{j}(\psi_{x},\psi_{y},\psi,x,c_{5})
for
j=1,\cdots,5
, defined as follows:

(3.26)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{1}(p_{x},p_{y},z,x,c)

\displaystyle=-\frac{x^{2}}{c}+\frac{\gamma+1}{2c}(2x-p_{x})p_{x}-\frac{\gamma-1}{c}\Big(z+\frac{p_{y}^{2}}{2(c-x)^{2}}\Big)\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{2}(p_{x},p_{y},z,x,c)

\displaystyle=-\frac{2(p_{x}+c-x)p_{y}}{c(c-x)^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{3}(p_{x},p_{y},z,x,c)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{c(c-x)^{2}}\Big((2c-x)x-(\gamma-1)\big(z+(c-x)p_{x}-\frac{p_{x}^{2}}{2}\big)+\frac{(\gamma-1)p_{y}^{2}}{2(c-x)^{2}}\Big)\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{4}(p_{x},p_{y},z,x,c)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{c-x}\Big(x-\frac{\gamma-1}{c}\big(z+(c-x)p_{x}+\frac{p_{x}^{2}}{2}+\frac{(\gamma+1)p_{y}^{2}}{2(\gamma-1)(c-x)^{2}}\big)\Big)\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{5}(p_{x},p_{y},z,x,c)

\displaystyle=-\frac{2(p_{x}+c-x)p_{y}}{c(c-x)^{3}}\,.

(ii)

Boundary condition for
\psi
on
\mathcal{R}(\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap\partial\Omega^{5})
:
Denote the Rankine–Hugoniot condition for
\varphi
on
\Gamma_{\rm shock}
as

(3.27)

g^{\rm sh}(D\varphi,\varphi,\bm{\xi})\coloneqq\rho(|D\varphi|,\varphi)D\varphi\cdot\bm{\nu}-D\varphi_{2}\cdot\bm{\nu}=(\mathcal{A}(D\varphi,\varphi)-D\varphi_{2})\cdot\bm{\nu}=0\,,

where
\mathcal{A}(\bm{p},z)=\rho(|\bm{p}|,z)\bm{p}
,
and
\bm{\nu}=\frac{D(\varphi_{2}-\varphi)}{|D(\varphi_{2}-\varphi)|}

is the unit interior normal vector on
\Gamma_{\rm shock}
.
For constant
\delta^{\prime}>0
given in Lemma
3.9
, we define a
function
\zeta\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
by

\zeta(t)=\begin{cases}t&\quad\text{for}\;t\geq\tfrac{3\delta^{\prime}}{4}\,,\\
\tfrac{\delta^{\prime}}{2}&\quad\text{for}\;t<\tfrac{\delta^{\prime}}{2}\,,\end{cases}

satisfying
\zeta^{\prime}(t)\geq 0
on
\mathbb{R}
. Also, we define an extension of

g^{\rm sh}(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})
onto
\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}\times\overline{\Omega^{5}}
:

(3.28)

g_{\rm mod}^{\rm sh}(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})\coloneqq\big(\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(\bm{p},z)-D\varphi_{2}(\bm{\xi})\big)\cdot\frac{D\varphi_{2}(\bm{\xi})-\bm{p}}{\zeta(|D\varphi_{2}(\bm{\xi})-\bm{p}|)}\,,

where
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(\bm{p},z)
is given by Lemma
3.5
, and

g_{\rm mod}^{\rm sh}(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})=g^{\rm sh}(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})
if
|D\varphi_{2}(\bm{\xi})-\bm{p}|\geq\frac{3\delta_{1}}{4}
.
Then we define

M^{(\theta_{1})}(\bm{p},z,\xi)\coloneqq g_{\rm mod}^{\rm sh}(\bm{p}+D\varphi_{5},z+\varphi_{5},\xi,\xi\tan\theta_{25}+a_{25}+\frac{z}{v_{2}})

with
(D\varphi_{5},\varphi_{5})
evaluated at
\bm{\xi}=(\xi,\,\xi\tan\theta_{25}+a_{25}+\frac{z}{v_{2}})
.
Note that, by Proposition
2.6
(
i
) and
Definition
2.7
,

(\theta_{25},\,a_{25})

depend continuously on
\theta_{1}\in[0,\theta^{\rm d}]

with the limit:

\lim\limits_{\theta_{1}\to 0^{+}}(\theta_{25},a_{25})=(\pi,\eta_{0})
,
where
\eta_{0}>0
is given by (
2.23
).

We prescribe the free boundary condition for
\psi
to be

\mathcal{B}^{(\theta_{1})}(\psi_{x},\psi_{y},\psi,x,y)=0\qquad\text{on $\mathcal{R}(\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap\partial\Omega^{5})$}\,,

where
\mathcal{B}^{(\theta_{1})}
is given by

(3.29)

\mathcal{B}^{(\theta_{1})}(p_{x},p_{y},z,x,y)\coloneqq|D\bar{\phi}_{25}-(p_{1},p_{2})|M^{(\theta_{1})}(p_{1},p_{2},z,\xi)\,,

with function
\bar{\phi}_{25}(\bm{\xi})\coloneqq(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{5})(\bm{\xi})=v_{2}(\eta-\xi\tan\theta_{25}-a_{25})
,
and

\xi=v_{2}\tan\theta_{25}+(c_{5}-x)\cos y\,,\qquad\begin{pmatrix}p_{1}\\
p_{2}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\cos y&\sin y\\
\sin y&-\cos y\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}-p_{x}\\
-\frac{p_{y}}{c_{5}-x}\end{pmatrix}.

(iii)

Other conditions for
\psi
: From Problem
2.10
(
iii
)–(
iv
)
and Definition
2.11
(
iv
),
\psi
satisfies

\psi\geq 0\,\,\,\,\text{in $\mathcal{R}(\Omega^{5})$}\,,\qquad\;\;\psi=0\,\,\,\,\text{on $\mathcal{R}(\Gamma_{\rm sonic}^{5})$}\,,\qquad\;\;\psi_{y}=0\,\,\,\,\text{on $\mathcal{R}(\Gamma_{\rm sym}\cap\partial\Omega^{5})$}\,.

Definition 3.11

(Parabolic norm)
.

Fix
\alpha\in(0,1)
.
Define the
\alpha
-th parabolic distance between points

\bm{x}=(x,y),\;\tilde{\bm{x}}=(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\in(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}
to be

\delta^{({\rm par})}_{\alpha}(\bm{x},\tilde{\bm{x}})\coloneqq\left(|x-\tilde{x}|^{2}+\max\{x,\tilde{x}\}|y-\tilde{y}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}.

Then, given constants
\sigma>0
and
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{+},
the parabolic norms are defined as follows
:

(i)

For any open set
\mathcal{D}\subseteq(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}

and any function
u\in C^{2}(\mathcal{D})
in the
(x,y)
–coordinates, define

\displaystyle\|u\|^{(\sigma),({\rm par})}_{m,0,\mathcal{D}}\coloneqq\sum\limits_{0\leq k+l\leq m}\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{D}}\Big(x^{k+\frac{l}{2}-\sigma}|\partial^{k}_{x}\partial^{l}_{y}u(\bm{x})|\Big)\,,

\displaystyle[u]^{(\sigma),({\rm par})}_{m,\alpha,\mathcal{D}}\coloneqq\sum\limits_{k+l=m}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{x},\tilde{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{D}\\
\bm{x}\neq\tilde{\bm{x}}\end{subarray}}\Big(\min\big\{x^{\alpha+k+\frac{l}{2}-\sigma},\tilde{x}^{\alpha+k+\frac{l}{2}-\sigma}\big\}\frac{|\partial^{k}_{x}\partial^{l}_{y}u(\bm{x})-\partial^{k}_{x}\partial^{l}_{y}u(\tilde{\bm{x}})|}{\delta^{({\rm par})}_{\alpha}(\bm{x},\tilde{\bm{x}})}\Big)\,,

\displaystyle\|u\|^{(\sigma),({\rm par})}_{m,\alpha,\mathcal{D}}\coloneqq\|u\|^{(\sigma),({\rm par})}_{m,0,\mathcal{D}}+[u]^{(\sigma),({\rm par})}_{m,\alpha,\mathcal{D}}\,.

(ii)

For any
a>0,
fix an open interval
I\coloneqq(0,a)
. For a function
f\in C^{2}(I),
define

\displaystyle\|f\|^{(\sigma),({\rm par})}_{m,0,I}\coloneqq\sum\limits_{k=0}^{m}\sup_{x\in I}\Big(x^{k-\sigma}|\partial^{k}_{x}f(x)|\Big)\,,

\displaystyle[f]^{(\sigma),({\rm par})}_{m,\alpha,I}\coloneqq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,\tilde{x}\in I\\
x\neq\tilde{x}\end{subarray}}\Big(\min\big\{x^{\alpha+m-\sigma},\tilde{x}^{\alpha+m-\sigma}\big\}\frac{|\partial^{m}_{x}f(x)-\partial^{m}_{x}f(\tilde{x})|}{|x-\tilde{x}|^{\alpha}}\Big)\,,

\displaystyle\|f\|^{(\sigma),({\rm par})}_{m,\alpha,I}\coloneqq\|f\|^{(\sigma),({\rm par})}_{m,0,I}+[f]^{(\sigma),({\rm par})}_{m,\alpha,I}\,.

(iii)

In the case
\sigma=2,
write
\|u\|^{({\rm par})}_{2,\alpha,\mathcal{D}}\coloneqq\|u\|^{(2),({\rm par})}_{2,\alpha,\mathcal{D}}
and

\|f\|^{({\rm par})}_{2,\alpha,I}\coloneqq\|f\|^{(2),({\rm par})}_{2,\alpha,I}
.

Case 1. Admissible solutions for
{\theta}_{1}<\theta^{\rm s}
away from
\theta^{\rm s}
.

Proposition 3.12
.

Fix
\gamma\geq 1
and
v_{2}\in(v_{\min},0)
.
For some constant
\bar{\varepsilon}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2}),
any
\sigma\in(0,\theta^{\rm s}),

and any
\alpha\in(0,1),
there exist both
\varepsilon\in(0,\bar{\varepsilon}]
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\sigma)

and
C>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\alpha)

such that any admissible solution
\varphi=\psi\circ\mathcal{R}+\varphi_{5}
corresponding to

{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap\{0\leq\theta_{1}\leq\theta^{\rm s}-\sigma\}
satisfies

(3.30)

\|\psi\|_{2,\alpha,\mathcal{R}(\Omega^{5}_{\varepsilon})}^{(\mathrm{par})}+\|f_{5,\mathrm{sh}}-f_{5,0}\|_{2,\alpha,(0,\varepsilon)}^{(\rm par)}\leq C\,,

where
\Omega^{5}_{\varepsilon}\subseteq\Omega
is a neighbourhood of
\Gamma^{5}_{\rm sonic},

and functions
{f}_{5,\mathrm{sh}}
and
{f}_{5,0}
represent
\Gamma_{\rm shock}
and
S_{25}
respectively
to be defined in
Step
1
below.

The proof of this proposition is similar to that of
[
2
, Propositions 3.26 and 3.30]
.
We sketch the main steps of the proof, while omitting most
details.

1.

The depiction and the properties of the reflected shock
S_{25}
and
the free boundary
\Gamma_{\rm shock}
in a small neighbourhood of the sonic boundary
\Gamma^{5}_{\rm sonic}
.

It follows from the geometric properties of
\overline{\Omega^{5}}
that
there exist positive constants
(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{0},\omega_{0})
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
,
with
\varepsilon_{0}<\varepsilon_{1}
and
\omega_{0}\in(0,1)
,
and a unique function
{f}_{5,0}\in C^{\infty}([-\varepsilon_{0},\varepsilon_{0}])
satisfying

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}\big(S_{25}\cap\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\Gamma^{5}_{\rm sonic})\big)\cap\{|x|<\varepsilon_{0}\}=\big\{(x,y)\,:\,|x|<\varepsilon_{0},\,y={f}_{5,0}(x)\big\}\,,

where
\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\Gamma^{5}_{\rm sonic})
is defined by (
3.21
),
and
2\omega_{0}\leq{f}^{\prime}_{5,0}(x)\leq\omega_{0}^{-1}
for
x\in(-\varepsilon_{0},\varepsilon_{0})
.
Then, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{1}]
, we define a set
\Omega^{5}_{\varepsilon}\subseteq\Omega
by

(3.31)

\Omega^{5}_{\varepsilon}\coloneqq\Omega\cap\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\Gamma^{5}_{\rm sonic})\cap\mathcal{R}^{-1}\big(\{x<\varepsilon\}\big).

Using (
3.20
) and (
3.25
),
we can show that there exist
\bar{\varepsilon}\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
,
L\geq 1
,
\delta\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
,
and
\omega\in(0,\omega_{0})
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
such that,
for all
(x,y)\in\mathcal{R}(\Omega^{5}_{\bar{\varepsilon}})
,

(3.32)

0\leq\psi(x,y)\leq Lx^{2}\,,\qquad 0\leq\psi_{x}\leq\frac{2-\delta}{1+\gamma}x\leq Lx\,,\qquad|\psi_{y}(x,y)|\leq Lx\,,

and there exists a unique function
{f}_{5,{\rm sh}}\in C^{1}([0,\bar{\varepsilon}])
such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(\Omega^{5}_{\bar{\varepsilon}})=\big\{(x,y)\,:\,0<x<\bar{\varepsilon},\,0<y<{f}_{5,{\rm sh}}(x)\big\}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap\partial\Omega^{5}_{\bar{\varepsilon}})=\big\{(x,y)\,:\,0<x<\bar{\varepsilon},\,y={f}_{5,{\rm sh}}(x)\big\}\,,

and
\omega\leq{f}^{\prime}_{5,{\rm sh}}(x)\leq L
for any
x\in(0,\bar{\varepsilon})
.

2.

Ellipticity for the equation and the free boundary condition.

Equation (
3.25
) for
\psi

in
\mathcal{R}(\Omega^{5})
can be written as

(3.33)

\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\hat{A}_{ij}^{(\theta_{1})}(\psi_{x},\psi_{y},\psi,x)\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\psi+\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\hat{A}_{i}^{(\theta_{1})}\partial_{i}\psi=0\,,

with
(\partial_{1},\partial_{2})=(\partial_{x},\partial_{y})

and
\hat{A}^{(\theta_{1})}_{12}=\hat{A}^{(\theta_{1})}_{21}
.
Then there exist
\varepsilon_{5}\in(0,\frac{\bar{\varepsilon}}{4}]
and
\lambda_{5}>0

depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
such that, for all
(x,y)\in\mathcal{R}(\overline{\Omega^{5}_{4\varepsilon_{5}}})
,

\frac{\lambda_{5}}{2}|\bm{\kappa}|^{2}\leq\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\hat{A}^{(\theta_{1})}_{ij}(\psi_{x}(x,y),\psi_{y}(x,y),\psi(x,y),x)\frac{\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}}{x^{2-\frac{i+j}{2}}}\leq\frac{2}{\lambda_{5}}|\kappa|^{2}\qquad\text{for all $\bm{\kappa}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}$}\,.

Moreover,
\mathcal{B}^{(\theta_{1})}
defined by (
3.29
)
satisfies
\mathcal{B}^{(\theta_{1})}(\mathbf{0},0,x,y)=0
for all
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
.
Let
y_{P_{2}}
be the
y
–coordinate of point
P_{2}
defined in Definition
2.9
.
For each
m=2,3,\cdots
, there exist constants
\delta_{\rm bc}>0
and
C>1
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},m)

such that, whenever
|(p_{x},p_{y},z,x)|\leq\delta_{\rm bc}
and
|y-y_{P_{2}}|\leq\delta_{\rm bc}
,

\big|D^{m}\mathcal{B}^{(\theta_{1})}(p_{x},p_{y},z,x,y)\big|\leq C\,.

Furthermore, there exist constants
\hat{\delta}_{\rm bc}>0
,
C>1
,
and
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
such that,
whenever
|(p_{x},p_{y},z,x)|\leq\hat{\delta}_{\rm bc}
and
|y-y_{P_{2}}|\leq\hat{\delta}_{\rm bc}
,

\displaystyle\partial_{a}\mathcal{B}^{(\theta_{1})}(p_{x},p_{y},z,x,y)\leq-C^{-1}

\displaystyle\text{for $\partial_{a}=\partial_{p_{x}},\partial_{p_{y}}$, or $\partial_{z}$}\,,

\displaystyle D_{(p_{x},p_{y})}\mathcal{B}^{(\theta_{1})}(p_{x},p_{y},z,x,y)\cdot\bm{\nu}^{(x,y)}_{\rm sh}\geq C^{-1}

\displaystyle\text{on $\mathcal{R}(\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap\partial\Omega_{\varepsilon^{\prime}}^{5})$}\,,

where
the vector field
\bm{\nu}^{(x,y)}_{\rm sh}
is the unit normal vector to
\mathcal{R}(\Gamma_{\rm shock})

expressed in the
(x,y)
–coordinates and oriented towards the interior of
\mathcal{R}(\Omega)
.

3.

Extension of the domain for the coefficients to equation (
3.33
).

Let
\varepsilon_{0}>0
and
L\geq 1
be the constants defined as above.
Then there exist constants
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{2}]

and
C>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
such that,
for any admissible solution
\varphi

corresponding to parameters
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta
, function
\psi=(\varphi-\varphi_{5})\circ\mathcal{R}^{-1}
satisfies

(3.34)

\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{2}\hat{A}_{ij}^{(\rm mod)}(\psi_{x},\psi_{y},\psi,x)\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\psi+\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{2}\hat{A}_{i}^{(\rm mod)}(\psi_{x},\psi_{y},\psi,x)\partial_{i}\psi=0\qquad\text{in $\mathcal{R}(\Omega^{5}_{\varepsilon})$}\,,

with
(\partial_{1},\partial_{2})\coloneqq(\partial_{x},\partial_{y})
and
coefficients
(\hat{A}_{ij}^{(\rm mod)},\hat{A}_{i}^{(\rm mod)})
satisfying

(\hat{A}_{ij}^{(\rm mod)},\hat{A}_{i}^{(\rm mod)})=(\hat{A}_{ij}^{(\theta_{1})},\hat{A}_{i}^{(\theta_{1})})\quad\,\,\text{in $\{(p_{x},p_{y},z,x)\,:\,|(p_{x},p_{y})|\leq Lx,\,|z|\leq Lx^{2},\,0<x<\varepsilon\}$}\,.

Moreover, the modified coefficients satisfy

\displaystyle\big|(\hat{A}_{11}^{(\rm mod)},\hat{A}_{12}^{(\rm mod)},\hat{A}_{2}^{(\rm mod)})(p_{x},p_{y},z,x)\big|\leq Cx\qquad\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}\times(0,\varepsilon)$}\,,

\displaystyle\big\|(\hat{A}_{22}^{(\rm mod)},\hat{A}_{1}^{(\rm mod)})\big\|_{0,\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}\times(0,\varepsilon)}+\big\|D_{(p_{x},p_{y},z,x)}(\hat{A}_{ij}^{(\rm mod)},\hat{A}_{i}^{(\rm mod)})\big\|_{0,\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}\times(0,\varepsilon)}\leq C\,.

4.

Rescaling method in the local domain to obtain the weighted Hölder estimate.

Using the strictly decreasing property of
\eta^{P_{2}}
with respect
to
\theta_{1}\in[0,\theta^{\rm s}]
which is shown in Lemma
A.2
(
ii
),
we obtain a constant
l_{\rm so}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\sigma)
such that,
for any admissible solution
\varphi
corresponding to

{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap\{0\leq\theta_{1}\leq\theta^{\rm s}-\sigma\}
,

y_{P}={f}_{5,{\rm sh}}(x_{P})\geq l_{\rm so}\qquad\text{for any $x_{P}\in[0,\bar{\varepsilon}]$}\,.

Subsequently, we define
\varepsilon_{*}\coloneqq\min\{\tfrac{\bar{\varepsilon}}{2},\,l^{2}_{\rm so}\}
.

For fixed
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{*}]

and any
z_{0}=(x_{0},y_{0})\in\mathcal{R}(\overline{\Omega^{5}_{\varepsilon}}\setminus\overline{\Gamma_{\rm sonic}^{5}})
,
define the rescaled coordinates
(S,T)
by
(x,y)\eqqcolon z_{0}+\frac{1}{4}(x_{0}S,\sqrt{x_{0}}T)
, which take values in a local domain

Q_{r}^{(z_{0})}\coloneqq\big\{(S,T)\in(-1,1)^{2}\,:\,z=z_{0}+\frac{r}{4}(x_{0}S,\sqrt{x_{0}}T)\in\mathcal{R}(\Omega_{2\varepsilon}^{5})\big\}\qquad\text{for any $r\in(0,1]$}\,.

Considering property (
3.32
), we define a rescaled function
\psi^{(z_{0})}
by

\psi^{(z_{0})}(S,T)\coloneqq\frac{1}{x_{0}^{2}}\psi(x_{0}+\frac{x_{0}}{4}S,y_{0}+\frac{\sqrt{x_{0}}}{4}T)\qquad\text{for $(S,T)\in Q_{1}^{(z_{0})}$}\,.

With a suitable rescaling on the coefficients of the modified equation (
3.34
),
we obtain a uniformly elliptic equation for
\psi^{(z_{0})}

under the
(S,T)
–coordinates.

Note that, for
z_{0}\in\mathcal{R}(\overline{\Omega^{5}_{\varepsilon}}\cap\Gamma_{\rm shock})
,
we can express
\Gamma_{\rm shock}
locally under the rescaled coordinates
as
\Gamma^{(z_{0})}_{\rm shock}\coloneqq\big\{(S,T)\in(-1,1)^{2}\,:\,T=F^{(z_{0})}(S)\big\}\subseteq\partial Q^{(z_{0})}_{1}

with
F^{(z_{0})}(S)
defined by

F^{(z_{0})}(S)\coloneqq\frac{4}{\sqrt{x_{0}}}\big({f}_{5,{\rm sh}}(x_{0}+\frac{x_{0}}{4}S)-{f}_{5,{\rm sh}}(x_{0})\big)\qquad\text{for $S\in(-1,1)$}\,.

Then, in the local domain
Q_{1}^{(z_{0})}
,
we apply
[
10
, Theorem 4.2.3]
for
z_{0}\in\mathcal{R}(\Omega_{\varepsilon}^{5})
,

[
10
, Theorem 4.2.8]
for

z_{0}\in\mathcal{R}(\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap\partial\Omega_{\varepsilon}^{5})
,
and
[
10
, Theorem 4.2.10]

for
z_{0}\in\mathcal{R}(\Gamma_{\rm sym}\cap\partial\Omega_{\varepsilon}^{5})
, respectively,
to obtain the uniform bound:

\sup\limits_{z_{0}\in\mathcal{R}(\Omega_{\varepsilon}^{5})}\big\|\psi^{(z_{0})}\big\|_{2,\alpha,\overline{Q^{(z_{0})}_{1/4}}}\,+\sup\limits_{z_{0}\in\mathcal{R}(\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap\partial\Omega_{\varepsilon}^{5})}\frac{1}{\sqrt{x_{0}}}\big\|F^{(z_{0})}\big\|_{2,\alpha,[-\frac{1}{4},\frac{1}{4}]}\leq C\,.

Scaling back into the
(x,y)
–coordinates,
we obtain the weighted Hölder estimates (
3.30
).

Case 2. Admissible solutions for
{\theta}_{1}<\theta^{\rm s}
close to
\theta^{\rm s}
.

Proposition 3.13
.

Fix
\gamma\geq 1
and
v_{2}\in[v_{2}^{\rm s},0)
.
Let
\bar{\varepsilon}>0
be the constant from
Proposition
3.12
.
For any
\alpha\in(0,1),
there exist constants
\varepsilon\in(0,\bar{\varepsilon}]

and
\sigma_{1}\in(0,{\theta}^{\rm{s}})
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2}),
and
C>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\alpha)

such that any admissible solution
\varphi=\psi\circ\mathcal{R}+\varphi_{5}

corresponding
to
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap\{\theta^{\rm s}-\sigma_{1}\leq\theta_{1}<\theta^{\rm s}\}
satisfies

\|\psi\|_{2,\alpha,\mathcal{R}(\Omega^{5}_{\varepsilon})}^{(\mathrm{par})}+\|{f}_{5,\mathrm{sh}}-{f}_{5,0}\|_{2,\alpha,(0,\varepsilon)}^{(\operatorname{par})}\leq C\,,

where
\Omega_{\varepsilon}^{5}\subseteq\Omega,

and functions
{f}_{5,\mathrm{sh}}
and
{f}_{5,0}
are defined in
Step

1
of
Case 1
above.

The proof is similar to that of
[
2
, Proposition 3.32]
.
The admissible solutions for Case 2 share the same properties as
in Steps
1
–
3
of Case 1. We omit the details of the proof
and only draw the main differences compared with the proof
of Proposition
3.12
here.

1.

Weighted Hölder estimate near
\mathcal{R}(P_{0}^{1})
.

Because

v_{2}\in[v_{2}^{\rm s},0)
,

y_{P_{2}}>0
tends to zero as
\theta_{1}\rightarrow\theta^{\rm s-}
.
Therefore, for the same rescaled coordinates
(S,T)

and local domain
Q_{r}^{(z_{0})}
with
z_{0}=(x_{0},y_{0})\in\mathcal{R}(\Omega^{5}_{\varepsilon})
as given in
Step
4
of Case 1 above,
it is possible that “
Q_{r}^{(z_{0})}
does not fit into
\Omega^{5}_{\varepsilon}
”,
meaning that the image of
Q_{r}^{(z_{0})}
under
transform
(S,T)\mapsto(x,y)
intersects the boundaries:

\mathcal{R}(\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap\partial\Omega_{\varepsilon}^{5})

and
\mathcal{R}(\Gamma_{\rm sym}\cap\partial\Omega_{\varepsilon}^{5})
simultaneously.

For that reason, we define
\Omega^{5}_{\rm fc}\coloneqq\Omega^{5}_{\bar{\varepsilon}}\cap\mathcal{R}^{-1}(\{x<y^{2}_{P_{2}}\})
.
It follows that
Q_{r}^{(z_{0})}
fits into
\Omega^{5}_{\rm fc}
for any
z_{0}\in\mathcal{R}(\Omega^{5}_{\rm fc})

due to similar considerations as the choice of
\varepsilon_{*}
above.
In this case, we follow the same idea as for Proposition
3.12

to obtain that there exists a constant
C>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\alpha)
such that,
for
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap\{\theta^{\rm s}-\sigma^{*}\leq\theta_{1}<\theta^{\rm s}\},

\|\psi\|^{({\rm par})}_{2,\alpha,\mathcal{R}(\Omega^{5}_{\rm fc})}\leq C

for some small constant
\sigma^{*}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
.

2.

Weighted Hölder estimate away from
\mathcal{R}(P_{0}^{1})
.

Note that there exists sufficiently large
k>1
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
such that the
following geometric relations hold:

\{0<x<2\bar{\varepsilon},\,0<y<y_{P_{2}}+\frac{x}{k}\}\;\subseteq\;\mathcal{R}(\Omega^{5}_{2\bar{\varepsilon}})\;\subseteq\;\{0<x<2\bar{\varepsilon},\,0<y<y_{P_{2}}+kx\}\,.

In the rest of the domain, there exist constants
\hat{\varepsilon}\in(0,\frac{\bar{\varepsilon}}{2}]
,

\sigma^{\prime}\in(0,\sigma^{*}]
, and
C^{*}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
such that,
for
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap\{\theta^{\rm s}-\sigma^{\prime}\leq\theta_{1}<\theta^{\rm s}\}
,

(3.35)

0\leq\psi(x,y)\leq C^{*}x^{4}\qquad\text{in $\mathcal{R}(\Omega^{5}_{2\hat{\varepsilon}})\cap\{x>\tfrac{1}{10}{y^{2}_{P_{2}}}\}$}\,.

Then, for any
z_{0}=(x_{0},y_{0})\in\mathcal{R}(\overline{\Omega^{5}_{\hat{\varepsilon}}})\cap\{x>\frac{1}{5}y^{2}_{P_{2}}\}
,
we introduce the rescaled coordinates
(S,T)
as
(x,y)\eqqcolon z_{0}+\frac{\sqrt{x_{0}}}{10k}(x_{0}S,\sqrt{x_{0}}T)
,
which take values in a local domain
Q_{r}^{(z_{0})}
defined by

Q_{r}^{(z_{0})}\coloneqq\big\{(S,T)\in(-1,1)^{2}\,:\,z=z_{0}+\frac{r\sqrt{x_{0}}}{10k}(x_{0}S,\sqrt{x_{0}}T)\in\mathcal{R}(\Omega_{2\hat{\varepsilon}}^{5})\big\}\qquad\text{for any $r\in(0,1]$}\,.

Thus, the local domain
Q_{r}^{(z_{0})}
fits into
\Omega_{2\hat{\varepsilon}}^{5}
, meaning that the image of

Q_{r}^{(z_{0})}
under transform
(S,T)\mapsto(x,y)
does not intersect both
\mathcal{R}(\Gamma_{\rm shock})

and
\mathcal{R}(\Gamma_{\rm sym})
simultaneously.
Considering property (
3.35
), we define a rescaled function
\psi^{(z_{0})}
by

\psi^{(z_{0})}(S,T)\coloneqq\frac{1}{x_{0}^{4}}\psi(x_{0}+\frac{x^{3/2}_{0}}{10k}S,y_{0}+\frac{x_{0}}{10k}T)\qquad\text{for $(S,T)\in Q_{1}^{(z_{0})}$}\,.

The rescaled function
\psi^{(z_{0})}
satisfies a uniformly elliptic equation
in the
(S,T)
–coordinates after a suitable rescaling on the coefficients of the modified equation (
3.34
).
The rest of the proof follows the same idea as in Step
4
of the proof of Proposition
3.12
.

Case 3. Admissible solutions
for
{\theta}_{1}\geq\theta^{\rm s}
close to
\theta^{\rm s}
.

Proposition 3.14
.

Fix
\gamma\geq 1
and
v_{2}\in(v_{2}^{\rm s},0)
.
Let
\bar{\varepsilon}>0
be the constant
from
Proposition
3.12
.
For each
\alpha\in(0,1),
there exist constants
\varepsilon\in(0,\bar{\varepsilon}]

and
\sigma_{3}\in(0,\theta^{\rm d}-\theta^{\rm s})
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
,
and a constant
C>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\alpha)
such that
any admissible solution
\varphi

corresponding to

{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap\{{\theta}^{\rm{s}}\leq\theta_{1}<{\theta}^{\rm{s}}+\sigma_{3}\}
satisfies

(3.36)

\displaystyle\|\varphi-\varphi_{5}\|_{2,\alpha,\overline{\Omega_{\varepsilon}^{5}}}+\|{f}_{5,\mathrm{sh}}-{f}_{5,0}\|_{2,\alpha,(0,\varepsilon)}\leq C\,,

\displaystyle|D^{m}_{\bm{\xi}}(\varphi-\varphi_{5})(P_{0}^{1})|=\frac{{\rm d}^{m}}{{\rm d}x^{m}}\left({f}_{5,\mathrm{sh}}-{f}_{5,0}\right)(0)=0\qquad\text{for $m=0,1,2$}\,,

where
\Omega_{\varepsilon}^{5}\subseteq\Omega,
and functions
{f}_{5,\mathrm{sh}}
and
{f}_{5,0}
are defined in
Step

1
below.

\square

The proof is similar to that of
[
2
, Proposition 3.39]
,
so we omit most details here and only emphasize the difference compared with the previous two cases.

1.

The depiction and the properties of the reflected shock
S_{25}
and
the free boundary
\Gamma_{\rm shock}
in a small neighbourhood of the sonic boundary
\Gamma^{5}_{\rm sonic}
.

Let
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{0},\omega_{0}>0
be chosen as
in Step
1
of the proof of Proposition
3.12
.
For the coordinates:
(x,y)=\mathcal{R}(\bm{\xi})
given by (
3.24
),
we define
\hat{x}\coloneqq x-x_{P_{2}}
.
Note that
x_{P_{2}}=0
if
\theta_{1}\in[0,\theta^{\rm s}]
and
x_{P_{2}}>0
if
\theta_{1}\in(\theta^{\rm s},\theta^{\rm s}+\sigma_{2}]
.
Then there exists a unique function
{f}_{5,0}\in C^{\infty}([-\varepsilon_{0},\varepsilon_{0}])
satisfying

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}\big(S_{25}\cap\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\Gamma^{5}_{\rm sonic})\big)\cap\{(x,y)\,:\,|\hat{x}|<\varepsilon_{0}\}=\big\{(x,y)\,:\,|\hat{x}|<\varepsilon_{0},\,y={f}_{5,0}(\hat{x})\big\}\,,

and
2\omega_{0}\leq{f}^{\prime}_{5,0}(\hat{x})\leq\omega_{0}^{-1}
for
\hat{x}\in(-\varepsilon_{0},\varepsilon_{0})
.

Reducing
\bar{\varepsilon}\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
further,
we can choose
\sigma_{2}\in(0,\theta^{\rm d}-\theta^{\rm s})
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\bar{\varepsilon})
such that,
for any admissible solution
\varphi
corresponding to

{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap\{0\leq\theta_{1}\leq\theta^{\rm s}+\sigma_{2}\}
,

\Omega^{5}_{\bar{\varepsilon}}
defined by (
3.31
) is nonempty
due to the monotonicity of the pseudo-Mach number in Lemma
2.3
, from which we can generalize the definition of
\Omega^{5}_{\bar{\varepsilon}}
to

(3.37)

\Omega^{5}_{\bar{\varepsilon}}\coloneqq\Omega\cap\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\Gamma^{5}_{\rm sonic})\cap\mathcal{R}^{-1}\big(\{(x,y)\,:\,0<\hat{x}<\bar{\varepsilon}\}\big)\,.

There exist
L\geq 1
,
\delta\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
,
and
\omega\in(0,\omega_{0})

depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
such that,
for all
(x,y)\in\mathcal{R}(\Omega^{5}_{\bar{\varepsilon}})
,

(3.38)

0\leq\psi(x,y)\leq Lx^{2}\,,\qquad 0\leq\psi_{x}\leq\frac{2-\delta}{1+\gamma}x\leq Lx\,,\qquad|\psi_{y}(x,y)|\leq Lx\,,

for
\psi\coloneqq(\varphi-\varphi_{5})\circ\mathcal{R}^{-1}
,
and there exists a unique function
{f}_{5,{\rm sh}}\in C^{1}([0,\bar{\varepsilon}])
such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(\Omega^{5}_{\bar{\varepsilon}})=\{(x,y)\,:\,0<\hat{x}<\bar{\varepsilon},\,0<y<{f}_{5,{\rm sh}}(\hat{x})\}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap\partial\Omega^{5}_{\bar{\varepsilon}})=\{(x,y)\,:\,0<\hat{x}<\bar{\varepsilon},\,y={f}_{5,{\rm sh}}(\hat{x})\}\,,

and
\omega\leq{f}^{\prime}_{5,{\rm sh}}(\hat{x})\leq L
for any
\hat{x}\in(0,\bar{\varepsilon})
.

2.

The free boundary condition and the modification on equation (
3.33
).

Similar to
[
2
, Lemma 3.37 and Corollary 3.38]
,
it follows that, for
\sigma_{2}\in(0,\theta^{\rm d}-\theta^{\rm s})
defined in Step
1
,
there exists a constant
\mu_{0}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\sigma_{2})
such that,
for any
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap\{\theta^{\rm s}\leq\theta_{1}\leq\theta^{\rm d}-\sigma_{2}\}
,

g^{\rm sh}_{\rm mod}
defined by (
3.28
) satisfies the following properties:

\partial_{a}g^{\rm sh}_{\rm mod}(D\varphi_{5}(P_{2}),\varphi_{5}(P_{2}),P_{2})\leq-\mu_{0}\,,

where
\partial_{a}=\partial_{p_{1}},\partial_{p_{2}}
, or
\partial_{z}
.
From these properties above, we can show that
\mathcal{B}^{(\theta_{1})}
defined by (
3.29
)
satisfies the same properties as in Step
2
of Case 1.
The extension of the domain for the coefficients of equation (
3.33
) is the same as Step
3

of Case 1.

3.

Rescaling method in the local domain to obtain the weighted Hölder estimate.

Note that, from the properties of
{f}_{5,0}
and
{f}_{5,{\rm sh}}
in Step
1
,
there exists
k>1
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
such that

\{0<\hat{x}<\bar{\varepsilon},\,0<y<\frac{\hat{x}}{k}\}\;\subseteq\;\mathcal{R}(\Omega^{5}_{\bar{\varepsilon}})\;\subseteq\;\{0<\hat{x}<\bar{\varepsilon},\,0<y<k\hat{x}\}\,.

Moreover, there exist constants
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{\bar{\varepsilon}}{2}],\sigma_{3}\in(0,\sigma_{2}]
,
and
C>1
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
such that,
for
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap\{{\theta}^{\rm{s}}\leq\theta_{1}\leq{\theta}^{\rm{s}}+\sigma_{3}\}
,

(3.39)

x_{P_{2}}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{10}\,,\qquad\,0\leq\psi(x,y)\leq C(x-x_{P_{2}})^{5}\quad\text{in $\mathcal{R}(\Omega^{5}_{2\varepsilon})$}\,.

Then, for any
z_{0}=(x_{0},y_{0})\in\mathcal{R}(\overline{\Omega^{5}_{\varepsilon}}\setminus\{P_{2}\})
,
we introduce the rescaled coordinates
(S,T)
as
(x,y)\eqqcolon z_{0}+\frac{x_{0}-x_{P_{2}}}{10k}(\sqrt{x_{0}}S,T)
,
taking values in a local domain
Q_{r}^{(z_{0})}
defined by

Q_{r}^{(z_{0})}\coloneqq\big\{(S,T)\in(-1,1)^{2}\,:\,z=z_{0}+\frac{r(x_{0}-x_{P_{2}})}{10k}(\sqrt{x_{0}}S,T)\in\mathcal{R}(\Omega_{2\varepsilon}^{5})\big\}\qquad\text{for any $r\in(0,1]$}\,.

Then the local domain
Q_{r}^{(z_{0})}
fits into
\Omega_{2\varepsilon}^{5}
, meaning that the image of
Q_{r}^{(z_{0})}

under transform
(S,T)\mapsto(x,y)
does not intersect both
\mathcal{R}(\Gamma_{\rm shock})
and
\mathcal{R}(\Gamma_{\rm sym})
simultaneously.
Considering property (
3.39
), we define a rescaled function
\psi^{(z_{0})}
by

\psi^{(z_{0})}(S,T)\coloneqq\frac{1}{(x_{0}-x_{P_{2}})^{5}}\psi(x_{0}+\frac{x_{0}-x_{P_{2}}}{10k}\sqrt{x_{0}}S,\,y_{0}+\frac{x_{0}-x_{P_{2}}}{10k}T)\qquad\text{for $(S,T)\in Q_{1}^{(z_{0})}$}.

The rescaled function
\psi^{(z_{0})}
satisfies a uniformly elliptic equation in the
(S,T)
–coordinates
after a suitable rescaling of the coefficients of the modified equation (
3.34
).
The rest of the proof follows
the same idea as Step
4
of Case 1.

Case 4.
Admissible solutions for
{\theta}_{1}\geq\theta^{\rm s}
away from
\theta^{\rm s}
.

abcs

Let
\sigma_{3}>0
be from Proposition
3.14
.
By Proposition
3.8
, there exists a constant
\delta\in(0,1)
depending
only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
such that any admissible solution
\varphi
corresponding to

{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap\{\theta^{\rm s}+\frac{\sigma_{3}}{2}\leq\theta_{1}<\theta^{\rm d}\}
satisfies

(3.40)

\frac{|D\varphi|^{2}}{c^{2}(|D\varphi|,\varphi)}\leq 1-\delta\qquad\,\text{in $\overline{\Omega}\cap\{\xi\geq 0\}$}\,,

where
c(|D\varphi|,\varphi)
is defined by (
3.2
).
By Lemma
3.3
and (
3.40
),

(D\varphi(\bm{\xi}),\varphi(\bm{\xi}))\in\mathcal{K}_{R_{*}}
for some constant
R_{*}\geq 2

depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
,
where
\mathcal{K}_{R}
is defined by (
3.9
).
In particular, there exist
\lambda_{*}>0
and
r_{*}>0
, depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
,
such that any admissible solution
\varphi
corresponding to

{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap\{\theta^{\rm s}+\frac{\sigma_{3}}{2}\leq\theta_{1}<\theta^{\rm d}\}
satisfies

(3.41)

\lambda_{*}|\bm{\kappa}|^{2}\leq\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\partial_{p_{j}}\mathcal{A}_{i}(D\varphi(\bm{\xi}),\varphi(\bm{\xi}))\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}\leq\lambda^{-1}_{*}|\bm{\kappa}|^{2}

for any
\bm{\xi}\in\overline{\Omega}\cap B_{2r_{*}}(P_{0}^{1})
and any
\bm{\kappa}=(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

Set
\bar{\phi}(\bm{\xi})\coloneqq\varphi_{2}(\bm{\xi})-\varphi(\bm{\xi})
.
Recalling equation (
3.1
) for function
\phi=\varphi-\varphi_{5}
, the free boundary condition (
3.27
)
on
\Gamma_{\rm shock}
, and the slip boundary condition
defined in Problem
2.10
(
iv
) on
\Gamma_{\rm sym}
,
it follows that
\bar{\phi}
satisfies the system:

(3.42)

\begin{cases}\,\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{2}\bar{A}_{ij}(D\bar{\phi},\bar{\phi},\bm{\xi})\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\bar{\phi}=0&\quad\text{in}\;\Omega\cap B_{r_{*}}(P_{0}^{1})\,,\\
\,\bar{g}^{\rm sym}(D\bar{\phi},\bar{\phi},\bm{\xi})=0&\quad\text{on}\;\Gamma_{\rm sym}\,,\\
\,\bar{g}^{\rm sh}(D\bar{\phi},\bar{\phi},\bm{\xi})=0&\quad\text{on}\;\Gamma_{\rm shock}\,,\end{cases}

with coefficients
\bar{A}_{ij}
and the boundary functions
\bar{g}^{\rm sym}
and
\bar{g}^{\rm sh}
defined by

(3.43)

\displaystyle\bar{A}_{ij}(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})\coloneqq c^{2}(|D\varphi_{2}-\bm{p}|,\varphi_{2}-z)\delta_{ij}-(\partial_{i}\varphi_{2}-p_{i})(\partial_{j}\varphi_{2}-p_{j})\,,

\displaystyle\bar{g}^{\rm sym}(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})\coloneqq v_{2}-p_{2}-\eta\,,

\displaystyle\bar{g}^{\rm sh}(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})\coloneqq-g^{\rm sh}(D\varphi_{2}-\bm{p},\varphi_{2}-z,\bm{\xi})\,,

for
g^{\rm sh}
given by (
3.27
), where
(\partial_{1},\partial_{2})=(\partial_{\xi},\partial_{\eta})
.

We define a vector
\bm{e}
as

\bm{e}\coloneqq\frac{D\varphi_{2}(P_{0}^{1})}{|D\varphi_{2}(P_{0}^{1})|}=\frac{O_{2}-P_{0}^{1}}{|O_{2}-P_{0}^{1}|}\,,

and choose
\bm{e}^{\perp}
as the clockwise rotation of
\bm{e}
by
\frac{\pi}{2}
.
Note that
\bm{e}
depends only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{1})
and satisfies that,
for any
\bm{\xi}\in\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap B_{r_{*}}(P_{0}^{1})
,

(3.44)

\partial_{\bm{e}}(\varphi_{2}-\varphi)(\bm{\xi})=-\partial_{r}(\varphi_{2}-\varphi)+\big(\bm{e}-\frac{D\varphi(\bm{\xi})}{|D\varphi(\bm{\xi})|}\big)\cdot D(\varphi_{2}-\varphi)\geq\frac{d^{\prime}_{0}}{2}\,,

where
d^{\prime}_{0}>0
is the constant from (
3.23
), depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
.
Then we define the new coordinates
(S,T)
by

(3.45)

\bm{\xi}\eqqcolon P_{0}^{1}+S\bm{e}+T\bm{e}^{\perp}\,.

Under these
(S,T)
–coordinates,
we can express
S_{25},\Gamma_{\rm shock},\Gamma_{\rm sym}
, and the domain near
P_{0}^{1}
as

(3.46)

\displaystyle S_{25}\cap B_{r_{*}}(P_{0}^{1})=\{\bm{\xi}(S,T)\,:\,S=a_{\rm ref}T,\,T>0,\,(S,T)\in B_{r_{*}}(\bm{0})\}\,,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap B_{r_{*}}(P_{0}^{1})=\{\bm{\xi}(S,T)\,:\,S=f_{\bm{e}}(T),\,T>0,\,(S,T)\in B_{r_{*}}(\bm{0})\}\,,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\rm sym}\cap B_{r_{*}}(P_{0}^{1})=\{\bm{\xi}(S,T)\,:\,S=a_{\rm sym}T,\,T>0,\,(S,T)\in B_{r_{*}}(\bm{0})\}\,,

\displaystyle\Omega\cap B_{r_{*}}(P_{0}^{1})=\{\bm{\xi}(S,T)\,:\,a_{\rm ref}T\leq f_{\bm{e}}(T)<S<a_{\rm sym}T,\,T>0,\,(S,T)\in B_{r_{*}}(\bm{0})\}\,,

where the positive constants
a_{\rm ref}=\tan({\theta_{25}-\hat{\theta}_{25}-\frac{\pi}{2}})

and
a_{\rm sym}=\cot\hat{\theta}_{25}
depend continuously
on
\theta_{1}\in(0,\theta^{\rm d})
,
and there exists a constant
C>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})

such that
C^{-1}\leq a_{\rm ref}<a_{\rm sym}\leq C
for all
\theta_{1}\in[\theta^{\rm s},\theta^{\rm d})
.
Note that
\hat{\theta}_{25}
is defined by (
2.28
),
and we have used Proposition
2.6
, including (
2.40
).

Definition 3.15

(Weighted Hölder norm)
.

For any
\sigma\in\mathbb{R},

\alpha\in(0,1),
and
m\in\mathbb{N},

the weighted Hölder norms are defined as follows
:

(i)

For any open bounded connected set
U\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{2},
let
\Gamma
be a closed portion of
\partial U
.
Write

\delta_{\bm{\xi}_{1}}\coloneqq{\rm dist}(\bm{\xi}_{1},\Gamma)\,,\quad\,\delta_{\bm{\xi}_{1},\bm{\xi}_{2}}\coloneqq\min\{\delta_{\bm{\xi}_{1}},\delta_{\bm{\xi}_{2}}\}\qquad\,\text{for any $\bm{\xi}_{1},\bm{\xi}_{2}\in U$}\,.

For any
u\in C^{m}(U),
define

\displaystyle\|u\|^{(\sigma),\Gamma}_{m,0,U}\coloneqq\sum_{0\leq|\bm{\beta}|\leq m}\sup_{\bm{\xi}_{1}\in U}\Big(\delta_{\bm{\xi}_{1}}^{\max\{|\bm{\beta}|+\sigma,0\}}\big|D^{\bm{\beta}}u(\bm{\xi}_{1})\big|\Big)\,,

\displaystyle[u]^{(\sigma),\Gamma}_{m,\alpha,U}\coloneqq\sum_{|\bm{\beta}|=m}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{\xi}_{1},\bm{\xi}_{2}\in U\\
\bm{\xi}_{1}\neq\bm{\xi}_{2}\end{subarray}}\Big(\delta_{\bm{\xi}_{1},\bm{\xi}_{2}}^{\max\{m+\alpha+\sigma,0\}}\frac{|D^{\bm{\beta}}u(\bm{\xi}_{1})-D^{\bm{\beta}}u(\bm{\xi}_{2})|}{|\bm{\xi}_{1}-\bm{\xi}_{2}|^{\alpha}}\Big)\,,

\displaystyle\|u\|^{(\sigma),\Gamma}_{m,\alpha,U}\coloneqq\|u\|^{(\sigma),\Gamma}_{m,0,U}+[u]^{(\sigma),\Gamma}_{m,\alpha,U}\,,

where
D^{\bm{\beta}}\coloneqq\partial_{\xi}^{\beta_{1}}\partial_{\eta}^{\beta_{2}}

for
\bm{\beta}\coloneqq(\beta_{1},\beta_{2})
with
\beta_{1},\beta_{2}\in\mathbb{N}

and
|\bm{\beta}|=\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}
.

(ii)

For any open bounded interval
I\subseteq\mathbb{R},
let
x_{0}\in\partial I
be an endpoint of
I
.
For any
f\in C^{m}(I),
define

\displaystyle\|f\|^{(\sigma),\{x_{0}\}}_{m,0,I}\coloneqq\sum_{k=0}^{m}\sup_{x\in I}\Big(|x-x_{0}|^{\max\{k+\sigma,0\}}\big|f^{(k)}(x)\big|\Big)\,,

\displaystyle[f]^{(\sigma),\{x_{0}\}}_{m,\alpha,I}\coloneqq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1},x_{2}\in I\\
x_{1}\neq x_{2}\end{subarray}}\Big(\min\big\{|x_{1}-x_{0}|,|x_{2}-x_{0}|\big\}^{\max\{m+\alpha+\sigma,0\}}\frac{|f^{(m)}(x_{1})-f^{(m)}(x_{2})|}{|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{\alpha}}\Big)\,,

\displaystyle\|f\|^{(\sigma),\{x_{0}\}}_{m,\alpha,I}\coloneqq\|f\|^{(\sigma),\{x_{0}\}}_{m,0,I}+[f]^{(\sigma),\{x_{0}\}}_{m,\alpha,I}\,.

Proposition 3.16
.

Fix
\gamma\geq 1
and
v_{2}\in(v_{2}^{\rm s},0)
.
Let
\sigma_{3}>0
be the constant from
Proposition
3.14
.
For small constants
\sigma_{\rm{s}}\in(0,\frac{\sigma_{3}}{2}]

and
\sigma_{\rm{d}}\in(0,\frac{\theta^{\rm d}}{10}),

there exist
r\in(0,r_{*}),

\alpha\in(0,1),
and
C>0
depending only
on
(\gamma,v_{2},\sigma_{\rm{s}},\sigma_{\rm{d}})
such that any admissible solution
\varphi

corresponding to

{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap\{{\theta}^{\rm{s}}+\sigma_{\rm{s}}\leq\theta_{1}\leq{\theta}^{\rm{d}}-\sigma_{\rm{d}}\}
satisfies the estimates
:

(3.47)

\displaystyle\big\|\varphi\big\|_{2,\alpha,\Omega\cap B_{r}(P_{0}^{1})}^{(-1-\alpha),\{P_{0}^{1}\}}+\big\|f_{\bm{e}}\big\|_{2,\alpha,(0,r)}^{(-1-\alpha),\{0\}}\leq C\,,\qquad|D^{m}_{\bm{\xi}}(\varphi-\varphi_{5})(P_{0}^{1})|=0\,\,\,\,\text{for $m=0,1$}\,.

The proof is similar to the proof of
[
2
, Proposition 3.42]
.
We omit the details of the proof and sketch only the main ideas here.

1
.
Hodograph transform.
Define
\bar{\phi}^{(h)}(S,T)\coloneqq\bar{\phi}(\bm{\xi}(S,T))

for function
\bar{\phi}=\varphi_{2}-\varphi
and the
(S,T)
–coordinates given by (
3.45
).
By property (
3.44
), we can apply the hodograph transform

\bm{y}=(y_{1},y_{2})=(\bar{\phi}^{(h)}(S,T),T)
to
\bar{\phi}
for the original system (
3.42
).
We write
S\eqqcolon v(y_{1},y_{2})
if it satisfies
\bar{\phi}^{(h)}(S,y_{2})=y_{1}
for any

\bm{y}\in\mathcal{D}^{(\theta_{1})}_{r_{*}}\coloneqq\big\{(\bar{\phi}^{(h)}(S,T),T)\,:\,\bm{\xi}(S,T)\in\Omega\cap B_{r_{*}}(P_{0}^{1})\big\}\,,

from which we obtain

\partial_{y_{1}}v=\frac{1}{\partial_{S}\bar{\phi}^{(h)}}\,,\qquad\quad\partial_{y_{2}}v=-\frac{\partial_{T}\bar{\phi}^{(h)}}{\partial_{S}\bar{\phi}^{(h)}}\,.

It follows from Lemma
3.3
and property (
3.44
) that
there exists a constant
K>1
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
such that

(3.48)

\frac{1}{K}\leq\partial_{y_{1}}v\leq\frac{2}{d_{0}^{\prime}}\,,\qquad|v|+|D_{\bm{y}}v|\leq 2K\,.

Under the hodograph transform, system (
3.42
) is equivalent to

(3.49)

\begin{cases}\,\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{2}a_{ij}(D_{\bm{y}}v,v,\bm{y})\partial_{y_{i}y_{j}}v=0&\quad\text{in}\;\mathcal{D}^{(\theta_{1})}_{r_{*}}\,,\\
\,g_{h}^{\rm sym}(D_{\bm{y}}v,v,\bm{y})=0&\quad\text{on}\;\Gamma^{(h)}_{\rm sym}\,,\\
\,g_{h}^{\rm sh}(D_{\bm{y}}v,v,\bm{y})=0&\quad\text{on}\;\Gamma^{(h)}_{\rm shock}\,,\end{cases}

for suitable functions
(a_{ij},g_{h}^{\rm sym},g_{h}^{\rm sh})
, with the boundary sets given by

\displaystyle\Gamma^{(h)}_{\rm sym}\coloneqq\big\{(\bar{\phi}^{(h)}(S,T),T)\,:\,\bm{\xi}(S,T)\in\Gamma_{\rm sym}\cap B_{r_{*}}(P_{0}^{1})\big\}\,,

\displaystyle\Gamma^{(h)}_{\rm shock}\coloneqq\big\{(0,T)\,:\,\bm{\xi}(S,T)\in\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap B_{r_{*}}(P_{0}^{1})\big\}\,.

Due to (
3.48
), we extend the domain of functions
(a_{ij},g_{h}^{\rm sym},g_{h}^{\rm sh})
to the set:

U\coloneqq\big\{(\bm{p},z,\bm{y})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{D}^{(\theta_{1})}_{r_{*}}\,:\,|\bm{p}|+|z|\leq 2K\big\}\,,

and continue to use the same notation as in (
3.49
) for this modified system.

2
.
Hölder continuity of
{g}_{h}^{\rm sym}
at the corner.

From Lemma
3.3
, there exists a constant
\tau_{0}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
such that

\Gamma^{(h)}_{\rm sym}\cup\Gamma^{(h)}_{\rm shock}\subseteq\{\bm{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\,:\,y_{2}>\tau_{0}|y_{1}|\}\,.

Moreover,
\Gamma^{(h)}_{\rm shock}
is in
C^{2}
up to endpoint
\{\bm{0}\}
.

From the definition of admissible solutions and the hodograph transform,
we obtain that

v\in C^{1}(\overline{\mathcal{D}^{(\theta_{1})}_{r_{*}}})\cap C^{2}(\mathcal{D}^{(\theta_{1})}_{r_{*}}\cup\Gamma^{(h)}_{\rm shock})\cap C^{3}(\mathcal{D}^{(\theta_{1})}_{r_{*}})

satisfies (
3.48
).
From (
3.43
) and the notation defined in Step
1
,
we can obtain the boundedness of coefficients
({a}_{ij},{g}_{h}^{\rm sym},{g}_{h}^{\rm sh})
in the modified system (
3.49
).
For any
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap\{\theta^{\rm s}+\sigma_{\rm s}\leq\theta_{1}<\theta^{\rm d}\}

with
\sigma_{\rm s}\in(0,\frac{\sigma_{3}}{2}]
, we may reduce
\lambda_{*}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\sigma_{\rm s})

in (
3.41
) to obtain the uniform ellipticity of the modified system (
3.49
) in
\mathcal{D}^{(\theta_{1})}_{r_{*}}

by using (
3.48
) and the hodograph transform.
It follows from Lemma
3.3
, (
3.41
), and (
3.48
)
that there exists a constant
\lambda_{1}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\sigma_{\rm s})
such that,
for any
y\in\Gamma^{(h)}_{\rm shock}
,

\displaystyle\bm{\nu}\cdot D_{\bm{p}}g_{h}^{\rm sh}(D_{\bm{y}}v,v,\bm{y})=\frac{1}{v_{y_{1}}}D\bar{\phi}\cdot D_{\bm{q}}g^{\rm sh}(D\varphi,\varphi,\bm{\xi})\geq\lambda_{1}\,,

where we have used the expression of
D_{\bm{q}}g^{\rm sh}(D\varphi,\varphi,\bm{\xi})

on
\Gamma^{(h)}_{\rm shock}
, and
\bm{\nu}=(1,0)
is the unit normal vector to
\Gamma^{(h)}_{\rm shock}
.
From (
3.48
),
|D_{\bm{p}}{g}_{h}^{\rm sym}(D_{\bm{y}}v,v,\bm{y})|\geq\frac{d_{0}^{\prime}}{2}>0
.

Similar to the proof of
[
2
, Lemma 3.40]
,
one can show that, for small
\sigma_{\rm{d}}\in(0,\frac{\theta^{\rm d}}{10})
,
there exist constants
M>0
and
\bar{r}\in(0,r_{*})
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\sigma_{\rm{d}})
such that
any admissible solution
\varphi
corresponding to

{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap\{\theta^{\rm s}\leq\theta_{1}<\theta^{\rm d}-\sigma_{\rm{d}}\}
satisfies

\partial_{q_{1}}g_{\rm mod}^{\rm sh}(D\varphi,\varphi,\bm{\xi})\leq-M^{-1}\qquad\text{for all $\bm{\xi}\in\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap B_{\bar{r}}(P_{0}^{1})$}\,.

Then, for any
\bm{y}\in\Gamma^{(h)}_{\rm shock}
,

|{\rm det}D_{\bm{p}}({g}_{h}^{\rm sym},{g}_{h}^{\rm sh})|>0
holds.

It follows from
[
10
, Proposition 4.3.7]
that
there exist constants
\alpha_{1}\in(0,1)
,
C>0
,
and
r_{0}\in(0,\bar{r}]

depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\sigma_{\rm s},\sigma_{\rm d})
such that,
for any
\bm{y}\in\overline{\mathcal{D}^{(\theta_{1})}_{\bar{r}}\cap B_{r_{0}}(\bm{0})}
,

(3.50)

\big|{g}^{\rm sym}_{h}(D_{\bm{y}}v(\bm{y}),v(\bm{y}),\bm{y})-{g}^{\rm sym}_{h}(D_{\bm{y}}v(\bm{0}),v(\bm{0}),\bm{0})\big|\leq C|\bm{y}|^{\alpha_{1}}\,.

3
.
Hölder continuity of
v(\bm{y})
at the corner.

Notice that both
{g}_{h}^{\rm sym}
and
{g}_{h}^{\rm sh}
are Lipschitz continuous on
(p,z,\bm{y})
.
Since
v\in C^{1}(\overline{\mathcal{D}^{(\theta_{1})}_{\bar{r}}})
,
inequality (
3.50
) also holds for any
\bm{y}\in\Gamma^{(h)}_{\rm shock}
after replacing
{g}_{h}^{\rm sym}

by
{g}_{h}^{\rm sh}
.
Moreover, functions
({g}_{h}^{\rm sym},{g}_{h}^{\rm sh})(\cdot,v(\bm{0}),\bm{0})

are bounded in
C^{1,\alpha_{1}}(\overline{B_{2K}(D_{\bm{y}}v(\bm{0}))})
,
and
|{\rm det}D_{\bm{p}}({g}_{h}^{\rm sym},{g}_{h}^{\rm sh})(D_{\bm{y}}v(\bm{0}),v(\bm{0}),\bm{0})|>0
follows from Step
2
.
Then, from
[
10
, Proposition 4.3.9]
, there exist
r_{1}\in(0,r_{0})
and
C_{1}>0

depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\sigma_{\rm s},\sigma_{\rm d})
such that

(3.51)

|D_{\bm{y}}v(\bm{y})-D_{\bm{y}}v(\bm{0})|\leq C_{1}|\bm{y}|^{\alpha_{1}}\qquad\text{for any $\bm{y}\in\Gamma^{(h)}_{\rm shock}\cap B_{r_{1}}(\bm{0})$}\,.

By the hodograph transform and the geometric properties of the domain near corner
P_{0}^{1}
,
it follows from (
3.50
)–(
3.51
) that

(3.52)

\displaystyle|\partial_{\eta}\varphi(\bm{\xi})-\partial_{\eta}\varphi(P_{0}^{1})|\leq C|\bm{\xi}-P_{0}^{1}|^{\alpha_{1}}\quad

\displaystyle\text{for any $\bm{\xi}\in\overline{\Omega\cap B_{r_{1}}(P_{0}^{1})}$}\,,

\displaystyle|D\varphi(\bm{\xi})-D\varphi(P_{0}^{1})|\leq C|\bm{\xi}-P_{0}^{1}|^{\alpha_{1}}\quad

\displaystyle\text{for any $\bm{\xi}\in\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap B_{r_{1}}(P_{0}^{1})$}\,.

The open domain
\Omega_{r_{1}}\coloneqq\Omega\cap B_{r_{1}}(P_{0}^{1})
contains two Lipschitz boundaries

\Gamma^{1}\coloneqq\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap B_{r_{1}}(P_{0}^{1})

and
\Gamma^{2}\coloneqq\Gamma_{\rm sym}\cap B_{r_{1}}(P_{0}^{1})
intersecting at corner
P_{0}^{1}
.
We also take

B^{(1)}(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})\coloneqq(1,0)\cdot(\bm{p}-D\bar{\phi}(P_{0}^{1}))\,,\qquad B^{(2)}(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})\coloneqq\bar{g}_{\rm sym}(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})\,.

Now we update system (
3.42
)
for
\bar{\phi}\in C^{1}(\overline{\Omega_{r_{1}}})\cap C^{2}(\Omega_{r_{1}}\cup\Gamma^{1})\cap C^{3}(\Omega_{r_{1}})

into domain
\Omega_{r_{1}}
, replacing the boundary conditions by

B^{(1)}(D\bar{\phi},\bar{\phi},\bm{\xi})=\bar{h}(\bm{\xi})\,\,\,\,\text{on}\;\Gamma^{1}\,,\qquad\,\,\,B^{(2)}(D\bar{\phi},\bar{\phi},\bm{\xi})=0\,\,\,\,\text{on}\;\Gamma^{2}\,,

where
\bar{h}(\bm{\xi})\coloneqq(1,0)\cdot(D\bar{\phi}(\bm{\xi})-D\bar{\phi}(P_{0}^{1}))
, which satisfies

|\bar{h}(\bm{\xi})-\bar{h}(P_{0}^{1})|\leq C|\bm{\xi}-P_{0}^{1}|^{\alpha_{1}}\qquad\text{for any $\bm{\xi}\in\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap B_{r_{1}}(P_{0}^{1})$}\,.

We can also extend the domain of functions
(\bar{A}_{ij},B^{(1)},B^{(2)})
for the updated system to the set:

V\coloneqq\{(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}\times\overline{\Omega_{r_{1}}}\,:\,|\bm{p}|+|z|\leq 2K_{1}\}\,.

Using
[
10
, Proposition 4.3.7]

again, we obtain that there exist
\alpha\in(0,\alpha_{1}]
,
r_{2}\in(0,r_{1}]
, and
C>0

depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\sigma_{\rm s},\sigma_{\rm d})
such that,
for any
\bm{\xi}\in\overline{\Omega\cap B_{r_{2}}(P_{0}^{1})}
,

\big|B^{(1)}(D\bar{\phi}(\bm{\xi}),\bar{\phi}(\bm{\xi}),\bm{\xi})-B^{(1)}(D\bar{\phi}(P_{0}^{1}),\bar{\phi}(P_{0}^{1}),P_{0}^{1})\big|\leq C\big|\bm{\xi}-P_{0}^{1}\big|^{\alpha}\,,

which, combined with (
3.52
),
implies that

(3.53)

\big|D\varphi(\bm{\xi})-D\varphi(P_{0}^{1})\big|\leq C\big|\bm{\xi}-P_{0}^{1}\big|^{\alpha}\qquad\text{for any $\bm{\xi}\in\overline{\Omega\cap B_{r_{2}}(P_{0}^{1})}$}\,.

4
.
Weighted Hölder estimate near corner
P_{0}^{1}
.

From Lemma
3.2
,
Remark
2.2
(
i
),
and (
3.53
),
there exists a constant
\varepsilon_{\rm bd}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
such that,
for any
\bm{\xi}_{0}\in\Gamma_{\rm bd}\cap B_{r_{3}}(P_{0}^{1})
for some
r_{3}\in(0,r_{2}]

with either
\Gamma_{\rm bd}\coloneqq\Gamma_{\rm shock}
or
\Gamma_{\rm bd}\coloneqq\Gamma_{\rm sym}
,

\big\{\bm{\xi}\in\partial\Omega\,:\,|\bm{\xi}-\bm{\xi}_{0}|<\varepsilon_{\rm bd}|\bm{\xi}_{0}-P_{0}^{1}|\big\}\subseteq\Gamma_{\rm bd}\,.

We now consider the following problem
for
\varphi\in C^{1}(\overline{\Omega\cap B_{r_{3}}(P_{0}^{1})})\cap C^{3}({\Omega\cap B_{r_{3}}(P_{0}^{1})})
:

\begin{cases}\,\mbox{div}\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(D\varphi,\varphi)+\tilde{\mathcal{B}}(D\varphi,\varphi)=0&\quad\text{in}\;{\Omega\cap B_{r_{3}}(P_{0}^{1})}\,,\\
\,g_{\rm mod}^{\rm sh}(D\varphi,\varphi,\bm{\xi})=0&\quad\text{on}\;\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap B_{r_{3}}(P_{0}^{1})\,,\\
\,g_{\rm sym}(D\varphi,\varphi,\bm{\xi})=0&\quad\text{on}\;\Gamma_{\rm sym}\cap B_{r_{3}}(P_{0}^{1})\,,\end{cases}

where
(\tilde{\mathcal{A}},\tilde{\mathcal{B}})(\bm{q},w)
are defined on

\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}

from Lemma
3.5
,
g_{\rm mod}^{\rm sh}(\bm{q},w,\bm{\xi})
is defined
on
\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}\times\overline{\Omega\cap B_{r_{3}}(P_{0}^{1})}
by (
3.28
),
and
g_{\rm sym}(\bm{q},w,\bm{\xi})=(0,1)\cdot\bm{q}
.
Combining the above with property (
3.53
) and
using
[
10
, Proposition 4.3.11(i)]
,
we obtain that there exist
\tilde{\alpha}\in(0,\alpha]

and
C>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\sigma_{\rm s},\sigma_{\rm d})
such that

\varphi\in C^{1,\tilde{\alpha}}(\Omega\cap B_{r}(P_{0}^{1}))
satisfies

\|\varphi\|_{1,\,\tilde{\alpha},\,\Omega\cap B_{r}(P_{0}^{1})}\leq C

and, for
\Gamma_{\rm shock}
given by (
3.46
),

\|f_{\bm{e}}\|_{1,\tilde{\alpha},(0,r)}\leq C
.
Finally, using
[
10
, Proposition 4.3.11(ii)]
, we obtain (
3.47
).

3.4.
Compactness of the set of admissible solutions

Fix
\gamma\geq 1
and
v_{2}\in(v_{\min},0)
.
For any
\theta_{\ast}\in(0,\theta^{\rm d})
,
the arguments in §
3.3
are also valid
for the weighted
C^{2,\alpha}
–estimates near
\Gamma^{6}_{\rm sonic}
with respect to
\theta_{2}\in[0,\theta^{\rm d})
.
According to all the
a priori
estimates obtained in Lemma
3.9
,
Corollary
3.10
, and Propositions
3.12
–
3.14

and
3.16
, there exists a constant
\bar{\alpha}\in(0,1)
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast})
such that the set:

\Big\{\,\|\varphi\|_{1,\bar{\alpha},\overline{\Omega}}+\|f_{\rm sh}\|_{1,\bar{\alpha},[\xi^{P_{3}},\xi^{P_{2}}]}\,:\,\;\parbox{140.00021pt}{$\varphi$ is an admissible solution \\
corresponding to ${\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap[0,\theta_{\ast}]^{2}$ }\;\;\Big\}

is bounded, where
\eta=f_{\rm{sh}}(\xi)
is the expression of
\Gamma_{\rm shock}
as a graph,
given by Lemma
3.2
.
For each admissible solution, the corresponding pseudo-subsonic region
\Omega
is a bounded domain
enclosed by
\Gamma^{5}_{\rm sonic}
,
\Gamma_{\rm shock}
,
\Gamma^{6}_{\rm sonic}
,
and
\Gamma_{\rm sym}
.
These four curves intersect only at
P_{i}
for
i=1,2,3,4
.
Note that
\Gamma^{5}_{\rm sonic},O_{5},P_{1}
, and
P_{2}
depend continuously
on
\theta_{1}\in[0,\theta^{\rm d}]
, while
\Gamma^{6}_{\rm sonic},O_{6},P_{3}
, and
P_{4}

depend continuously on
\theta_{2}\in[0,\theta^{\rm d}]
.
Combining the above results with (
3.13
) and Lemma
3.7
,
we have the following compactness result;
the details of the proof can be found in
[
10
, Proposition 11.6.1]
.

Lemma 3.17
.

Fix
\gamma\geq 1,

v_{2}\in(v_{\min},0),
and
\theta_{\ast}\in(0,\theta^{\rm d})
.
Let
\big\{{\bm{\theta}}^{(j)}\big\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\subseteq\Theta\cap[0,\theta_{\ast}]^{2}

be a sequence of parameters satisfying that

\lim_{j\to\infty}{\bm{\theta}}^{(j)}={\bm{\theta}}^{(\infty)}

for some
{\bm{\theta}}^{(\infty)}\in[0,\theta_{\ast}]^{2}
.
For each
j\in\mathbb{N},
let
\varphi^{(j)}
be an admissible solution corresponding to
parameters
{\bm{\theta}}^{(j)},

with pseudo-subsonic region
\Omega^{(j)}

and curved transonic shock
\Gamma^{(j)}_{\rm shock}
.
Then there exists a subsequence
\big\{\varphi^{(j_{k})}\big\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
the following properties hold
:

(i)

\big\{\varphi^{(j_{k})}\big\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
converges uniformly on any compact subset of
\overline{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}
to a function

\varphi^{(\infty)}\in C^{0,1}_{\rm loc}\big(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\big),

and the limit function
\varphi^{(\infty)}
is an admissible solution corresponding
to
{\bm{\theta}}^{(\infty)}
;

(ii)

\Omega^{(j_{k})}\to\Omega^{(\infty)}
in the Hausdorff metric
;

(iii)

If
\bm{\xi}^{(j_{k})}\in\overline{\Omega^{(j_{k})}}
for each
k\in\mathbb{N},
and
\big\{\bm{\xi}^{(j_{k})}\big\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}

converges to
\bm{\xi}^{(\infty)}\in\overline{\Omega^{(\infty)}},
then

\varphi^{(j_{k})}(\bm{\xi}^{(j_{k})})\to\varphi^{(\infty)}(\bm{\xi}^{(\infty)})\,,\quad D\varphi^{(j_{k})}(\bm{\xi}^{(j_{k})})\to D\varphi^{(\infty)}(\bm{\xi}^{(\infty)})\qquad\,\,\mbox{as $k\to\infty$}\,,

where, in the case that
\bm{\xi}^{(j_{k})}\in\Gamma^{(j_{k})}_{\rm shock},

the derivative of
\varphi^{(j_{k})}
at
\bm{\xi}^{(j_{k})}
is defined as the limit of
