the derivative from the interior of
\Omega^{(j_{k})}
:

\qquad D\varphi^{(j_{k})}(\bm{\xi}^{(j_{k})})\coloneqq\lim_{\bm{\xi}\to\bm{\xi}^{(j_{k})},\,\bm{\xi}\in\Omega^{(j_{k})}}D\varphi^{(j_{k})}(\bm{\xi})\,,

and, similarly, for
\bm{\xi}^{(\infty)}\in\Gamma^{(\infty)}_{\rm shock},

\qquad D\varphi^{(\infty)}(\bm{\xi}^{(\infty)})\coloneqq\lim_{\bm{\xi}\to\bm{\xi}^{(\infty)},\,\bm{\xi}\in\Omega^{(\infty)}}D\varphi^{(\infty)}(\bm{\xi})\,.

4.
Iteration Method and Existence of Admissible Solutions

In this section, we carefully construct the iteration set and the iteration map based
on the uniform estimates obtained in Section
3
.
Then we apply the Leray-Schauder degree theorem to show the existence of admissible solutions.
The construction of the iteration set follows closely the process
described in
[
2
, Chapter 4]
with the main difference
that both sonic arcs
\Gamma^{5}_{\rm sonic}
and
\Gamma^{6}_{\rm sonic}
may
degenerate into single points.
In this section, we always fix

\gamma\geq 1
and
v_{2}\in(v_{\min},0)
.

4.1.
Mapping between the elliptic and standard domains

4.1.1.
Mapping into the standard domain

Define the standard domain to be the rectangle
\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}\coloneqq(-1,1)\times(0,1)
.
Before constructing a map between
\Omega
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}
,
we introduce some useful notation and basic geometric properties.
Let
\varphi_{2}
be given by (
2.17
),
and let
(\varphi_{5},\varphi_{6})
be given by (
2.33
).
For any
\delta_{0}>0
, we define

S_{2j}^{\delta_{0}}\coloneqq\{\bm{\xi}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{j})(\bm{\xi})=-\delta_{0}\}
and

q_{j}^{\delta_{0}}\coloneqq{\rm dist}(O_{j},S_{2j}^{\delta_{0}})
for
j=5,6
, and also write

\displaystyle u_{5}^{\delta_{0}}\coloneqq u_{5}+q_{5}^{\delta_{0}}\sin{\theta_{25}}\geq 0\,,\qquad u_{6}^{\delta_{0}}\coloneqq u_{6}-q_{6}^{\delta_{0}}\sin{\theta_{26}}\leq 0\,.

For constant
\hat{\delta}>0
from (
3.15
), we set
\tilde{\delta}_{0}\coloneqq\frac{\hat{\delta}}{4}
,
which depends only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
.
Then, for
\hat{c}_{j}
given by (
3.14
), a direct calculation gives that,
for any
{\bm{\theta}}\in\overline{\Theta}
,

\hat{c}_{j}-q_{j}^{\delta_{0}}\geq\hat{\delta}-\frac{\delta_{0}}{\ell(\rho_{j})}\geq 2\tilde{\delta}_{0}\,,

after choosing
\delta_{0}>0
small enough, depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
, and
using Lemma
2.8
.
Therefore,
S_{2j}^{\delta_{0}}
and
\partial B_{\hat{c}_{j}}(O_{j})
intersect
at two distinct points for any
{\bm{\theta}}\in\overline{\Theta}
.

Definition 4.1

(Extended domain
Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}
)
.

Let
\delta_{0}>0
be chosen as above.
Define
P_{2}^{\prime}
and
P_{3}^{\prime}
by

\{P^{\prime}_{2}\}\coloneqq S_{25}^{\delta_{0}}\cap\partial B_{\hat{c}_{5}}(O_{5})\cap\{\xi\geq u_{5}^{\delta_{0}}\}\,,\qquad\{P^{\prime}_{3}\}\coloneqq S_{26}^{\delta_{0}}\cap\partial B_{\hat{c}_{6}}(O_{6})\cap\{\xi\leq u_{6}^{\delta_{0}}\}\,.

Denote by
\Gamma_{\rm sonic}^{5,\delta_{0}}
the arc of
\partial B_{\hat{c}_{5}}(O_{5})\cap\{\xi\geq u_{5}^{\delta_{0}}\}

with endpoints
\{P_{1},P^{\prime}_{2}\},

and denote by
\Gamma_{\rm sonic}^{6,\delta_{0}}
the arc of
\partial B_{\hat{c}_{6}}(O_{6})\cap\{\xi\leq u_{6}^{\delta_{0}}\}
with endpoints
\{P_{4},P^{\prime}_{3}\}
.
Define the extended domain
Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}
as the open bounded region enclosed by

\Gamma_{\rm sonic}^{5,\delta_{0}},\Gamma_{\rm sonic}^{6,\delta_{0}},S_{25}^{\delta_{0}},S_{26}^{\delta_{0}},

and
\Gamma_{\rm sym};
see
Fig.
4.1
.

Figure 4.1
.
The extended domain
Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}
in grey for the case:
{\bm{\theta}}\in[\theta^{\rm s},\theta^{\rm d})\times(0,\theta^{\rm s})
.

For any
\bm{\xi}=(\xi,\eta)\in(\mathbb{R}\setminus[u_{6}^{\delta_{0}},u_{5}^{\delta_{0}}])\times[0,\infty)
, define the
(x,y)
–coordinates by
(x,y)\coloneqq\mathcal{R}(\bm{\xi})
if and only if

(4.1)

\bm{\xi}=\begin{cases}O_{6}+({c}_{6}-x)\left(\cos(\pi-y),\,\sin(\pi-y)\right)&\quad\text{if}\;\xi<u_{6}^{\delta_{0}}\,,\\
O_{5}+({c}_{5}-x)\left(\cos y,\,\sin y\right)&\quad\text{if}\;\xi>u_{5}^{\delta_{0}}\,,\end{cases}

with
y\in(0,\pi)\,
. For any constant
\epsilon>0
, we define two sets
\mathcal{D}^{5}_{\epsilon},\mathcal{D}^{6}_{\epsilon}\subseteq Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}
by

(4.2)

\mathcal{D}^{5}_{\epsilon}\coloneqq\big\{\bm{\xi}\in Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}\,:\,\xi>u_{5}^{\delta_{0}}\big\}\setminus\overline{B_{\hat{c}_{5}-\epsilon}(O_{5})}\,,\qquad\mathcal{D}^{6}_{\epsilon}\coloneqq\big\{\bm{\xi}\in Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}\,:\,\xi<u_{6}^{\delta_{0}}\big\}\setminus\overline{B_{\hat{c}_{6}-\epsilon}(O_{6})}\,.

Let
\omega_{0}\in(0,\frac{\pi}{2})
be the solution of

(q^{\delta_{0}}+\tilde{\delta}_{0})\cos\omega_{0}=q^{\delta_{0}}
, where
q^{\delta_{0}}\coloneqq\max\{q_{5}^{\delta_{0}},q_{6}^{\delta_{0}}\}
.
Then

\tilde{\omega}_{0}\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}\inf_{{\bm{\theta}}\in\overline{\Theta}}\big\{\theta_{25}-\tfrac{\pi}{2},\,\tfrac{\pi}{2}-\theta_{26},\,\omega_{0}\big\}>0

by (
3.14
)–(
3.15
) and Lemma
2.8
.
A direct computation shows that, for any
\epsilon\in(0,\tilde{\delta}_{0})
,

(4.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{5}_{\epsilon}\subseteq\big\{\bm{\xi}\,:\,x_{P_{2}}<x<x_{P_{2}}+\epsilon,\,0<y<\theta_{25}-\tfrac{\pi}{2}-\tilde{\omega}_{0}\big\}\cap\big\{\xi>u_{5}^{\delta_{0}}+\tilde{\delta}_{0}\sin{\tilde{\omega}_{0}}\big\}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{6}_{\epsilon}\subseteq\big\{\bm{\xi}\,:\,x_{P_{3}}<x<x_{P_{3}}+\epsilon,\,0<y<\tfrac{\pi}{2}-\theta_{26}-\tilde{\omega}_{0}\big\}\cap\big\{\xi<u_{6}^{\delta_{0}}-\tilde{\delta}_{0}\sin{\tilde{\omega}_{0}}\big\}\,.

In particular, we fix a constant
k>4
sufficiently large, depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
,
such that
\max\{\hat{c}_{5},\hat{c}_{6}\}\leq\frac{k\tilde{\delta}_{0}}{4}\sin{\tilde{\omega}_{0}}

for any
{\bm{\theta}}\in\overline{\Theta}
and

(4.4)

\mathcal{D}^{5}_{\frac{3\hat{c}_{5}}{k}}\subseteq\big\{\xi>u_{5}^{\delta_{0}}+\frac{3\hat{c}_{5}}{k}\big\}\,,\qquad\mathcal{D}^{6}_{\frac{3\hat{c}_{6}}{k}}\subseteq\big\{\xi<u_{6}^{\delta_{0}}-\frac{3\hat{c}_{6}}{k}\big\}\,.

For each
{\bm{\theta}}\in\overline{\Theta}
, we define an invertible map

\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}:\overline{Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}}\to[-1,1]\times[0,\infty)
,
which flattens the extended sonic arcs
\Gamma^{5,\delta_{0}}_{\rm sonic}

and
\Gamma^{6,\delta_{0}}_{\rm sonic}
.
For each admissible solution
\varphi
corresponding to parameters
{\bm{\theta}}\in\overline{\Theta}
,
we further construct a map
G_{2,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}
from
\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}(\Omega)
to the standard domain
\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}
,
where
\Omega
is the pseudo-subsonic domain associated with
\varphi
.
Finally, we present the
a priori
estimates in the standard domain for admissible solutions
under
map
G_{2,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}\circ\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}
.

Construction of
\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}
.

For
r_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
and
r_{\rm d}>0
, we fix a basic cut-off function

\tilde{\zeta}(\mathrel{\,\cdot\,};r_{0},r_{\rm d})\in C^{4}(\mathbb{R})

such that

\tilde{\zeta}^{\prime}(\mathrel{\,\cdot\,};r_{0},r_{\rm d})\in[0,\frac{2}{r_{\rm d}}]
and

(4.5)

\tilde{\zeta}(r;\,r_{0},r_{\rm d})=\begin{cases}\,0&\quad\text{for}\;r\leq r_{0}\,,\\
\,1&\quad\text{for}\;r\geq r_{0}+r_{\rm d}\,.\end{cases}

For
j=5,6
, let the cut-off functions
(\zeta_{j},\chi_{j})
be defined by

\displaystyle\zeta_{j}(r)\coloneqq\tilde{\zeta}(r;\,(1-\frac{3}{k})\hat{c}_{j},\frac{\hat{c}_{j}}{k})\,,\qquad\chi_{j}(\xi)\coloneqq\tilde{\zeta}((-1)^{j-1}\xi;\,(-1)^{j-1}u_{j}^{\delta_{0}},\frac{2\hat{c}_{j}}{k})\,.

Then we define

F_{1}:\overline{Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}}\to\mathbb{R}^{2}

by
F_{1}(\xi,\eta)\coloneqq(h_{1}(\xi,\eta),\eta)
, where

h_{1}(\bm{\xi})\coloneqq\zeta_{\rm d}(\bm{\xi})u_{r}+\big(1-\zeta_{\rm d}(\bm{\xi})\big)\xi\,,

and
\zeta_{\rm d}
and
u_{r}
are given by

\displaystyle\zeta_{\rm d}(\bm{\xi})\coloneqq\chi_{5}(\xi)\zeta_{5}(|\bm{\xi}-O_{5}|)+\chi_{6}(\xi)\zeta_{6}(|\bm{\xi}-O_{6}|)\,,

\displaystyle u_{r}\coloneqq(u_{5}+|\bm{\xi}-O_{5}|)\chi_{5}(\xi)+(u_{6}-|\bm{\xi}-O_{6}|)\chi_{6}(\xi)\,.

For each
{\bm{\theta}}\in\overline{\Theta}
,
we write
s_{5}\coloneqq u_{5}+\hat{c}_{5}>0
and
s_{6}\coloneqq u_{6}-\hat{c}_{6}<0
,
which depend continuously on
{\bm{\theta}}\in\overline{\Theta}
.
Let
\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}(\Gamma)
be defined by (
3.21
).
By construction of
map
F_{1}
, we observe that

h_{1}(\bm{\xi})=u_{j}+(-1)^{j-1}{\rm dist}(\bm{\xi},O_{j})\qquad\,\text{for any
$\bm{\xi}\in Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}\cap\mathcal{N}_{\frac{2\hat{c}_{j}}{k}}(\Gamma^{j,\delta_{0}}_{\rm sonic})$}\,,

and
\overline{F_{1}(Q^{{\bm{\theta}}})}\subseteq[s_{6},s_{5}]\times[0,\infty)
.
Let the linear map
L_{{\bm{\theta}}}:[s_{6},s_{5}]\to[-1,1]
be given by

(4.6)

\displaystyle L_{{\bm{\theta}}}(s^{\prime})\coloneqq d_{L}(s^{\prime}-s_{6})-1\qquad\,\text{with}\;d_{L}\coloneqq\frac{2}{s_{5}-s_{6}}\,,

which maps
interval
[s_{6},s_{5}]
to the standard interval
[-1,1]
.
For

k>4
given by (
4.4
),
define the cut-off functions
\tilde{\chi}_{j}

by

\tilde{\chi}_{j}(s^{\prime})\coloneqq\tilde{\zeta}((-1)^{j-1}s^{\prime};\,(-1)^{j-1}u_{j}+(1-\frac{1}{k})\hat{c}_{j},\frac{\hat{c}_{j}}{2k})\qquad\,\mbox{for $j=5,6$}\,.

We define a function
h_{2}:\overline{F_{1}(Q^{{\bm{\theta}}})}\to[0,\infty)
by

\displaystyle h_{2}(s^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime})\coloneqq\tilde{\chi}_{5}\arcsin{\big(\frac{t^{\prime\prime}}{s^{\prime}-u_{5}}\big)}+\tilde{\chi}_{6}\arcsin{\big(\frac{t^{\prime\prime}}{u_{6}-s^{\prime}}\big)}+(1-\tilde{\chi}_{5}-\tilde{\chi}_{6})\,t^{\prime\prime}\,,

where
\tilde{\chi}_{5}
and
\tilde{\chi}_{6}
are evaluated at
s^{\prime}
.
For maps
F_{1}
and
h_{2}
constructed above, we see that

\displaystyle h_{2}\circ F_{1}(\bm{\xi})=y\qquad\,\text{for any $\bm{\xi}\in Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}\cap\big(\mathcal{N}_{\frac{\hat{c}_{5}}{2k}}(\Gamma^{5,\delta_{0}}_{\rm sonic})\cup\mathcal{N}_{\frac{\hat{c}_{6}}{2k}}(\Gamma^{6,\delta_{0}}_{\rm sonic})\big)$}\,,

for the
(x,y)
–coordinates given by (
4.1
).

Finally, we define
map
\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}:\overline{Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}}\to[-1,1]\times[0,\infty)
by

(4.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}(\bm{\xi})\coloneqq(L_{{\bm{\theta}}}\circ h_{1}(\bm{\xi}),\,h_{2}\circ F_{1}(\bm{\xi}))\qquad\,\mbox{for any $\bm{\xi}\in\overline{Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}}$}\,,

and write the coordinates
(s,t^{\prime})\coloneqq\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}(\bm{\xi})
.

It can be verified directly that
\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}(\Gamma_{\rm sym})=\{(s,0)\,:\,-1<s<1\}
.
Moreover,
map
\mathcal{G}^{{\bm{\theta}}}_{1}:\overline{Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}}\to[-1,1]\times[0,\infty)
constructed above satisfies the following properties by using (
3.3
), (
4.5
), and Lemma
3.17
;
the proof is similar to
[
2
, Lemmas 4.3 and 4.5]
.

Lemma 4.2
.

There exists a constant
\mu_{\mathcal{G}_{1}}\in(0,1)
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
such that,
for each
{\bm{\theta}}\in\overline{\Theta},

map
\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}
defined by (
4.7
) satisfies the following
:

(i)

\|\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}\|_{C^{4}(\overline{Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}})}+\|(\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}})^{-1}\|_{C^{4}(\overline{\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}(Q^{{\bm{\theta}}})})}\leq\mu_{\mathcal{G}_{1}}^{-1},

and
|{\rm det}(D\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}})|\geq\mu_{\mathcal{G}_{1}}
in
\overline{Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}}
;

(ii)

For
j=5,6,
and for any
(s,t^{\prime})\in\overline{\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}(Q^{{\bm{\theta}}})}
,

\partial_{t^{\prime}}\big((\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{j})\circ(\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}})^{-1}\big)(s,t^{\prime})\leq-\mu_{\mathcal{G}_{1}}
;

(iii)

For any
\theta_{\ast}\in(0,\theta^{\rm d}),
there exists a constant
\mu_{\ast}>0

depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast})
such that any admissible solution

\varphi
corresponding to

{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\leq\theta_{\ast}\}
satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{t^{\prime}}\big((\varphi_{2}-\varphi)\circ(\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}})^{-1}\big)(s,t^{\prime})\leq-\mu_{\ast}<0\qquad\text{for all $(s,t^{\prime})\in\overline{\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}(\Omega)}$}\,.

Using Lemma
4.2
(
ii
),
there exists a unique function
f_{{\bm{\theta}}}\in C^{0,1}([-1,1])
such that

\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}\big(\{\bm{\xi}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\,:\,\varphi_{2}=\max\{\varphi_{5},\varphi_{6}\}-\delta_{0}\}\big)=\{(s,f_{{\bm{\theta}}}(s))\,:\,-1<s<1\}\,,

which follows from the implicit function theorem, and

(4.8)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}(Q^{{\bm{\theta}}})=\big\{(s,t^{\prime})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\,:\,-1<s<1,\,0<t^{\prime}<f_{{\bm{\theta}}}(s)\big\}\,.

Using (
3.13
), Definition
2.11
(
ii
),
Lemma
3.9
(
ii
), Corollary
3.10
,
Propositions
3.12
–
3.14
and
3.16
,
and Lemma
4.2
,
we introduce a function
\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}:[-1,1]\to[0,\infty)
representing
\Gamma_{\rm shock}

in the
(s,t^{\prime})
–coordinates
and state its important properties; the proof is similar to
[
2
, Proposition 4.6]
.

Proposition 4.3
.

For each admissible solution
\varphi
corresponding to
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta,

there exists a unique function
\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}:[-1,1]\to[0,\infty)
satisfying the following properties
:

(i)

In the
(s,t^{\prime})
–coordinates
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}(\Omega)=\big\{(s,t^{\prime})\,:\,-1<s<1,\,0<t^{\prime}<\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}(s)\big\}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}(\Gamma_{\rm shock})=\big\{(s,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}(s))\,:\,-1<s<1,\,t^{\prime}=\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}(s)\big\}\,,

and, for any interval
I_{1}\Subset(-1,1),

\|\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}\|_{C^{3}(\overline{I_{1}})}\leq C_{{}_{I_{1}}}

for some constant
C_{I_{1}}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},I_{1})
.

(ii)

Let
Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}_{0}
be the bounded region enclosed by

\Gamma^{5}_{\rm sonic},\Gamma^{6}_{\rm sonic},S_{25},S_{26},

and
\Gamma_{\rm sym},
which satisfies
\Omega\subseteq Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}_{0}\subseteq Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}
.
For
\mathcal{D}^{5}_{\epsilon}
and
\mathcal{D}^{6}_{\epsilon}
given by (
4.2
),
there exist unique functions
\mathfrak{g}_{5}
and
\mathfrak{g}_{6}
with

\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}(Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}_{0}\cap\mathcal{D}^{j}_{\epsilon})=\big\{(s,t^{\prime})\in\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}\,:\,1+(-1)^{j}s<d_{L}\epsilon,\,0<t^{\prime}<\mathfrak{g}_{j}(s)\big\}\qquad\,\text{for $j=5,6$}\,.

Let
\epsilon^{\ast}_{0}>0
be the smallest
constant

\varepsilon_{0}
in
Step
1
of the proofs of
Propositions
3.12

and
3.14
.
For
d_{L}>0
given by (
4.6
)
,
set
\hat{\epsilon}^{*}_{0}\coloneqq d_{L}\epsilon^{*}_{0}
.
There exists a constant
C>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
such that

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{g}_{5}\|_{C^{3}([1-\hat{\epsilon}^{*}_{0},1])}+\|\mathfrak{g}_{6}\|_{C^{3}([-1,-1+\hat{\epsilon}^{*}_{0}])}\leq C\,.

(iii)

For each
\theta_{\ast}\in(0,\theta^{\rm d}),
there exist
\bar{\alpha}\in(0,1)

and
C_{\theta_{\ast}}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast})
such that,
for any admissible solution corresponding to

{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\leq\theta_{\ast}\},

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}\|^{(-1-\bar{\alpha}),\{-1\}}_{2,\bar{\alpha},(-1,-1+\hat{\epsilon}_{0}^{\ast})}+\|\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}\|^{(-1-\bar{\alpha}),\{1\}}_{2,\bar{\alpha},(1-\hat{\epsilon}_{0}^{\ast},1)}\leq C_{\theta_{\ast}}\,,

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}^{m}}{{\rm d}s^{m}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}-\mathfrak{g}_{j})((-1)^{j-1})=0\qquad\text{for $m=0,1,$ and $j=5,6$}\,.

where

\|\cdot\|^{(\sigma),\{x_{0}\}}_{2,\alpha,I}
is given by
Definition
3.15
(
ii
)
.
Note that the properties above are equivalent to

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}-\mathfrak{g}_{5}\|^{(1+\bar{\alpha}),({\rm par})}_{2,\bar{\alpha},(1-\hat{\epsilon}_{0}^{\ast},1)}+\|\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}-\mathfrak{g}_{6}\|^{(1+\bar{\alpha}),({\rm par})}_{2,\bar{\alpha},(-1,-1+\hat{\epsilon}_{0}^{\ast})}\leq C^{\prime}_{\theta_{\ast}}

for some constant
C^{\prime}_{\theta_{\ast}}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast}),

where

\|\cdot\|^{(1+\alpha),({\rm par})}_{2,\alpha,I}
is given
by
Definition
3.11
(
ii
)
with replacement of
x
by
1-|s|
.

(iv)

For each
\theta_{\ast}\in(0,\theta^{\rm d}),
there exists a constant
\hat{k}>1

depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast})
such that, for any admissible solution
\varphi

corresponding to
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\leq\theta_{\ast}\}

and any
s\in[-1,1],

\begin{split}&\min\{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}(-1)+\frac{s+1}{\hat{k}},\,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}(1)+\frac{1-s}{\hat{k}},\,\frac{1}{\hat{k}}\}\leq\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}(s)\\
&\hskip 40.00006pt\leq\min\{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}(-1)+\hat{k}(s+1),\,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}(1)+\hat{k}(1-s),\,f_{{\bm{\theta}}}(s)-\frac{1}{\hat{k}}\}\,.\end{split}

Remark 4.1
.

By
Propositions
3.12
–
3.13
,
for each
\alpha\in(0,1),

there exist
\hat{\epsilon}_{3}>0
and
C_{\alpha}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\alpha)
such that,
for any admissible solution corresponding to
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta,

\|\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}-\mathfrak{g}_{j}\|^{(\rm{par})}_{2,\alpha,I_{j}}\leq C_{\alpha}\qquad\text{whenever $\theta_{j-4}\leq\theta^{\rm s}$}\,,

for
j=5,6,

where
I_{j}\coloneqq\{s\in(-1,1)\,:\,1+(-1)^{j}s<\hat{\epsilon}_{3}\},

and

\|\cdot\|^{({\rm par})}_{2,{\alpha},I_{j}}
is given
by
Definition
3.11
(
ii
)
with replacement of
x
by
1-|s|
.

By
Proposition
3.14
,
for each
\alpha\in(0,1),
there exist constants
\hat{\epsilon}_{4}>0

and
C^{\prime}_{\alpha}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\alpha)
such that, for any admissible solution corresponding to
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta,

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}-\mathfrak{g}_{j}\|_{2,\alpha,\overline{I^{\prime}_{j}}}\leq C^{\prime}_{\alpha}\,,\qquad\;\frac{{\rm d}^{m}}{{\rm d}s^{m}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}-\mathfrak{g}_{j})((-1)^{j-1})=0\quad\,\text{for $m=0,1,2$}\,,

whenever
\theta_{j-4}\in[\theta^{\rm s},\theta^{\rm s}+\sigma_{3}]
for
j=5,6,

where
I^{\prime}_{j}\coloneqq\{s\in(-1,1)\,:\,1+(-1)^{j}s<\hat{\epsilon}_{4}\}
.

Construction of
u^{(\varphi,{\bm{\theta}})}
.

For each
{\bm{\theta}}\in\overline{\Theta}\setminus\{\bm{0}\}
,
it can be verified directly
that
S_{25}

and
S_{26}

given by Definition
2.7

intersect at the unique point
P_{I}\coloneqq(\xi^{P_{I}},\eta^{P_{I}})
with

(4.9)

\displaystyle\xi^{P_{I}}\coloneqq\frac{u_{5}\xi^{P_{0}^{1}}-u_{6}\xi^{P_{0}^{2}}}{u_{5}-u_{6}}\,,\qquad\eta^{P_{I}}\coloneqq\frac{u_{5}u_{6}}{u_{5}-u_{6}}\frac{\xi^{P_{0}^{1}}-\xi^{P_{0}^{2}}}{v_{2}}\,.

Similarly,
S_{25}^{\delta_{0}}
and
S_{26}^{\delta_{0}}
intersect at the unique point
P_{I}^{\delta_{0}}\coloneqq(\xi^{P_{I}},\eta^{P_{I}^{\delta_{0}}})
with

\eta^{P_{I}^{\delta_{0}}}\coloneqq\eta^{P_{I}}-\tfrac{\delta_{0}}{v_{2}}>\eta^{P_{I}}
.
In Lemma
A.3
, we show the limits:

\displaystyle\lim_{{\bm{\theta}}\to\bm{0},\,{\bm{\theta}}\in\overline{\Theta}\setminus\{\bm{0}\}}(\xi^{P_{I}},\eta^{P_{I}})=(0,\eta_{0})\,,

where
\eta_{0}
is given by (
2.23
).
Then we define
P_{I}|_{{\bm{\theta}}=\bm{0}}\coloneqq(0,\eta_{0})
such that
P_{I}
is
C(\overline{\Theta})
.
By (
4.9
) and Lemma
A.2
(
ii
),

\xi^{P_{I}}\geq 0
for any
{\bm{\theta}}\in\overline{\Theta}\cap\{\theta_{2}=0\}
.
Therefore, there exists a constant
\sigma_{\rm sep}>0
small enough, depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
, such that

\displaystyle\xi^{P_{3}}\leq-d_{\rm sep}<-\tfrac{d_{\rm sep}}{2}\leq\xi^{P_{I}}\qquad\text{for any ${\bm{\theta}}\in\overline{\Theta}\cap\{0\leq\theta_{2}\leq\sigma_{\rm sep}\}$}\,,

where
d_{\rm sep}>0
is given by (
3.10
).
Also, by Lemma
A.2
(
ii
),
there exists a small constant
\delta_{\rm sep}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\sigma_{\rm sep})
such that

\displaystyle\eta^{P_{3}}\leq\eta_{0}-\delta_{\rm sep}<\eta_{0}\leq\min\{a_{25},a_{26}\}\leq\eta^{P_{I}}\qquad\,\text{for any ${\bm{\theta}}\in\overline{\Theta}\cap\{\sigma_{\rm sep}\leq\theta_{2}\leq\theta^{\rm d}\}$}\,.

From the above two inequalities, using
P_{3},P_{I}\in S_{26}
and Lemma
2.8
, we obtain the uniform positive lower bound:

\displaystyle\inf_{{\bm{\theta}}\in\overline{\Theta}}(\xi^{P_{I}}-\xi^{P_{3}})>0\,.

Definition 4.4
.

Let
\tilde{\zeta}(r;r_{0},r_{\rm d})
be given by (
4.5
)
,

and let
\xi^{P_{I}}
be given by (
4.9
).
For
{\bm{\theta}}\in\overline{\Theta},
define

\chi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{*}(\xi)\coloneqq\tilde{\zeta}({-{}\xi};-\xi^{P_{I}},\,\frac{\xi^{P_{I}}-\xi^{P_{3}}}{5C_{\rm bg}})

for some constant
C_{\rm bg}>1
.
The smooth background function
\varphi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\ast}(\bm{\xi})
is defined by

\displaystyle\varphi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\ast}(\bm{\xi})\coloneqq\varphi_{5}(\bm{\xi})\,(1-\chi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\ast}(\xi))+\varphi_{6}(\bm{\xi})\,\chi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\ast}(\xi)\,.

For constant
\delta_{0}>0
from
Definition
4.1
,

constant
C_{\rm bg}
is fixed sufficiently large, depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2}),

such that
\max\{\varphi_{5},\varphi_{6}\}-\varphi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\ast}\leq\frac{\delta_{0}}{2}
.

From the construction above,
it can be checked that
\varphi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\ast}\leq\max\{\varphi_{5},\varphi_{6}\}
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}

and there exists a constant
\bar{k}>1
sufficiently large,
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
, such that, for
j=5,6
,

(4.10)

\displaystyle\varphi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\ast}=\varphi_{\bm{\theta}}=\varphi_{j}\,\qquad\text{in $\mathcal{D}^{j}_{\hat{c}_{j}/\bar{k}}$}\,,

where
\hat{c}_{j}
is given by (
3.14
).
Then

(4.11)

\big\{\bm{\xi}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\,:\,\xi^{P_{3}}<\xi<\xi^{P_{2}},\,(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\ast})(\bm{\xi})=0\big\}\subseteq Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}\,.

Using Lemma
4.2
(
ii
)
and Definition
4.4
,
we can directly obtain that, for any
{\bm{\theta}}\in\overline{\Theta}
,
there exists a constant
\bar{\mu}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
such that

(4.12)

\partial_{t^{\prime}}\big((\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\ast})\circ(\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}})^{-1}\big)(s,t^{\prime})\leq-\bar{\mu}\qquad\,\text{for any $(s,t^{\prime})\in\overline{\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}(Q^{{\bm{\theta}}})}$}\,.

For any admissible solution
\varphi
corresponding to parameters
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta
,
let
\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}:[-1,1]\to[0,\infty)
be the unique function given by Proposition
4.3
.
Then

G_{2,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}:\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}(Q^{{\bm{\theta}}})\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
is defined by

(4.13)

\displaystyle(s,t)=G_{2,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}(s,t^{\prime})\coloneqq(s,\frac{t^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}(s)})\qquad\,\text{for any $(s,t^{\prime})\in\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}(Q^{{\bm{\theta}}})$}\,.

By Proposition
4.3
(
iv
), map
G_{2,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}

is well defined and invertible with
G_{2,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}^{-1}(s,t)=(s,t\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}(s))
.
Then

G_{2,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}\circ\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}(\Omega)=\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}=(-1,1)\times(0,1)

by Proposition
4.3
(
i
).
Therefore,
u^{(\varphi,{\bm{\theta}})}
, defined as

(4.14)

\displaystyle u^{(\varphi,{\bm{\theta}})}(s,t)\coloneqq(\varphi-\varphi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\ast})\circ(G_{2,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}\circ\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}})^{-1}(s,t)\,,

is well defined for all
(s,t)\in\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}
.

A priori estimates for
u^{(\varphi,{\bm{\theta}})}
.

For
\epsilon^{\ast}_{0}>0
from Proposition
4.3
(
ii
),
define a constant
\epsilon^{\prime}_{0}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
as

(4.15)

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\prime}_{0}\coloneqq\min_{{\bm{\theta}}\in\overline{\Theta}}d_{L}\epsilon^{\ast}_{0}\,\,\leq\,\,\hat{\epsilon}^{*}_{0}\,,

where
d_{L}>0
is given by (
4.6
).
For
r\in(0,1)
and for
j=5,6
, we define

(4.16)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}^{\rm int}_{r}\coloneqq\big\{(s,t)\in\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}\,:\,|s|<1-r\big\}\,,\qquad\mathcal{Q}^{j}_{r}\coloneqq\big\{(s,t)\in\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}\,:\,1+(-1)^{j}\,s<r\big\}\,.

Definition 4.5

(Weighted Hölder norms in the standard domain)
.

For
\alpha\in(0,1)
and any
u\in C^{2}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}),
introduce the norm
:

\displaystyle\|u\|^{(\ast)}_{2,\alpha,\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}}\coloneqq\|u\|_{2,\alpha,\overline{\mathcal{Q}^{\rm int}_{\epsilon^{\prime}_{0}/4}}}+\sum_{j=5,6}\Big(\|u\|^{(1+\alpha),({\rm par})}_{2,\alpha,\mathcal{Q}^{j}_{\epsilon^{\prime}_{0}}}+\|u\|^{(1+\alpha),({\rm subs})}_{1,\alpha,\mathcal{Q}^{j}_{\epsilon^{\prime}_{0}}}\Big)\,,\vskip-8.0pt

where
\|\cdot\|_{2,\alpha,U}
is the standard Hölder norm, and the weighted Hölder
norms
\|\cdot\|^{(\sigma),({\rm subs})}_{m,\alpha,U}
and
\|\cdot\|^{(\sigma),({\rm par})}_{m,\alpha,U},

for an open set
U\subseteq\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter},

\sigma>0
,
and
m\in\mathbb{N},
are given as follows
:

(i)

For any
\bm{s}=(s,t),\,\tilde{\bm{s}}=(\tilde{s},\tilde{t})\in\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter},
define

\displaystyle\delta^{({\rm subs})}_{\alpha}(\bm{s},\tilde{\bm{s}})\coloneqq\big((s-\tilde{s})^{2}+(\max\{1-|s|,1-|\tilde{s}|\})^{2}(t-\tilde{t})^{2}\big)^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\,.

The Hölder norms with subsonic scaling are given by

\displaystyle\|u\|^{(\sigma),({\rm subs})}_{m,0,U}\coloneqq\sum_{0\leq k+l\leq m}\sup_{\bm{s}\in U}\big((1-|s|)^{k-\sigma}|\partial^{k}_{s}\partial^{l}_{t}u(\bm{s})|\big)\,,

\displaystyle[u]^{(\sigma),({\rm subs})}_{m,\alpha,U}\coloneqq\sum_{k+l=m}\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{s},\tilde{\bm{s}}\in U\\
\bm{s}\neq\tilde{\bm{s}}\end{subarray}}\Big(\min\big\{(1-|s|)^{\alpha+k-\sigma},(1-|\tilde{s}|)^{\alpha+k-\sigma}\big\}\frac{|\partial^{k}_{s}\partial^{l}_{t}u(\bm{s})-\partial^{k}_{s}\partial^{l}_{t}u(\tilde{\bm{s}})|}{\delta^{({\rm subs})}_{\alpha}(\bm{s},\tilde{\bm{s}})}\Big)\,,

\displaystyle\|u\|^{(\sigma),({\rm subs})}_{m,\alpha,U}=\|u\|^{(\sigma),({\rm subs})}_{m,0,U}+[u]^{(\sigma),({\rm subs})}_{m,\alpha,U}\,.

(ii)

For any
\bm{s}=(s,t),\tilde{\bm{s}}=(\tilde{s},\tilde{t})\in\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter},
define

\delta^{({\rm par})}_{\alpha}(\bm{s},\tilde{\bm{s}})\coloneqq\big((s-\tilde{s})^{2}+\max\{1-|s|,1-|\tilde{s}|\}(t-\tilde{t})^{2}\big)^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\,.

The Hölder norms with parabolic scaling are given by

\displaystyle\|u\|^{(\sigma),({\rm par})}_{m,0,U}\coloneqq\sum_{0\leq k+l\leq m}\sup_{\bm{s}\in U}\big((1-|s|)^{k+\frac{l}{2}-\sigma}|\partial^{k}_{s}\partial^{l}_{t}u(\bm{s})|\big)\,,

\displaystyle[u]^{(\sigma),({\rm par})}_{m,\alpha,U}\coloneqq\sum_{k+l=m}\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{s},\tilde{\bm{s}}\in U\\
\bm{s}\neq\tilde{\bm{s}}\end{subarray}}\Big(\min\big\{(1-|s|)^{\alpha+k+\frac{l}{2}-\sigma},(1-|\tilde{s}|)^{\alpha+k+\frac{l}{2}-\sigma}\big\}\frac{|\partial^{k}_{s}\partial^{l}_{t}u(\bm{s})-\partial^{k}_{s}\partial^{l}_{t}u(\tilde{\bm{s}})|}{\delta^{({\rm par})}_{\alpha}(\bm{s},\tilde{\bm{s}})}\Big)\,,

\displaystyle\|u\|^{(\sigma),({\rm par})}_{m,\alpha,U}=\|u\|^{(\sigma),({\rm par})}_{m,0,U}+[u]^{(\sigma),({\rm par})}_{m,\alpha,U}\,.

Denote by
C^{2,\alpha}_{(\ast)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})
the set of
all functions in
C^{2}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})
whose
\|\cdot\|^{(\ast)}_{2,\alpha,\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}}
–norms are finite.

Note that
C^{2,\alpha}_{(\ast)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})
is compactly embedded into
C^{2,\tilde{\alpha}}_{(\ast)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})

whenever
0\leq\tilde{\alpha}<\alpha<1
, which follows from the properties
given in
[
10
, Lemma 4.6.3 and Corollary 17.2.7]
.

Using Corollary
3.10
and Propositions
3.12
–
3.14
and
3.16
,
we have the following estimate for admissible solutions
\varphi
after mapping the pseudo-subsonic domain
\Omega

to the standard domain
\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}
; the proof is similar to
[
2
, Proposition 4.12]
.

Proposition 4.6
.

For each
\theta_{\ast}\in(0,\theta^{\rm d}),

there exist constants
M>0
and
\bar{\alpha}\in(0,\frac{1}{3}]
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast})

such that, for any admissible solution
\varphi
corresponding to
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\leq\theta_{\ast}\},

u=u^{(\varphi,{\bm{\theta}})}:\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}\to\mathbb{R}
,
defined by (
4.14
), satisfies
u\in C^{2,\bar{\alpha}}_{(\ast)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})
and

\displaystyle\|u\|^{(\ast)}_{2,\bar{\alpha},\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}}=\|u\|_{2,\bar{\alpha},\mathcal{Q}^{\rm int}_{\epsilon^{\prime}_{0}/4}}+\sum_{j=5,6}\Big(\|u\|^{(1+\bar{\alpha}),({\rm par})}_{2,\bar{\alpha},\mathcal{Q}^{j}_{\epsilon^{\prime}_{0}}}+\|u\|^{(1+\bar{\alpha}),({\rm subs})}_{1,\bar{\alpha},\mathcal{Q}^{j}_{\epsilon^{\prime}_{0}}}\Big)\leq M\,.

4.1.2.
Mapping to approximate admissible solutions

For each
{\bm{\theta}}\in\overline{\Theta}
, let the extended domain
Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}
be given by Definition
4.1
,
and let
\varphi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\ast}
be given by Definition
4.4
.
For each
s^{\ast}\in(-1,1)
, define

Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}(s^{\ast})\coloneqq Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}\cap(\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}})^{-1}(\{s=s^{\ast}\})
.
Then, for
i=1,2
,

\displaystyle\inf_{Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}((-1)^{i-1})}(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\ast})<0\leq\sup_{Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}((-1)^{i-1})}(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\ast})\,,

where the inequality on the right above becomes a strict inequality when
\theta_{i}\in[0,\theta^{\rm s})

and an equality when
\theta_{i}\in[\theta^{\rm s},\theta^{\rm d}]
.
Moreover, by the choice of
C_{\rm bg}>0
in Definition
4.4
,

\sup_{Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}(s)}(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\ast})-\inf_{Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}(s)}(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\ast})\geq\frac{\delta_{0}}{2}>0\qquad\,\text{for any $s\in(-1,1)$}\,.

Definition 4.7
.

Fix
\alpha\in(0,1),\,\theta_{\ast}\in(0,\theta^{\rm d}),

and
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\leq\theta_{\ast}\}
.
Let
u\in C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}})
be a function satisfying that

(4.17)

\displaystyle\inf_{Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}(s)}(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\ast})<u(s,1)<\sup_{Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}(s)}(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\ast})\qquad\,\text{for any $s\in(-1,1)$}\,.

(i)

For each
s\in(-1,1),
let

\bar{t}^{\prime}>0
be the unique solution
of
(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\ast})\circ(\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}})^{-1}(s,\bar{t}^{\prime})=u(s,1),

which exists by (
4.12
) and (
4.17
).
Define
\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}:(-1,1)\to(0,\infty)
by

\,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(s)\coloneqq\bar{t}^{\prime}
.

(ii)

For
\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}
and
G_{2,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}}
given by (
4.7
) and (
4.13
) respectively,
define

\mathfrak{F}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}:\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}\to Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}
by

\,\mathfrak{F}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}\coloneqq(\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}})^{-1}\circ G_{2,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}}^{-1}
.

(iii)

Define sets
\Omega(u,{\bm{\theta}})\coloneqq\mathfrak{F}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})
and

\Gamma_{\rm shock}(u,{\bm{\theta}})\coloneqq\mathfrak{F}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}((-1,1)\times\{1\})
.
Finally, define function
\varphi^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{\xi}):\Omega(u,{\bm{\theta}})\to\mathbb{R}
by

\,\varphi^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{\xi})\coloneqq(u\circ\mathfrak{F}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}^{-1})(\bm{\xi})+\varphi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\ast}(\bm{\xi})

for all
\bm{\xi}\in\Omega(u,{\bm{\theta}})
.

We call
\varphi^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}
above an approximate admissible solution
if it satisfies the same boundary conditions on
\Gamma_{\rm sonic}^{5}\cup\Gamma_{\rm sonic}^{6}

as the admissible solutions in Definition
2.11
.
For
\alpha\in(0,1)
and
\theta_{\ast}\in(0,\theta^{\rm d})
,
define the space of such approximate admissible solutions:

(4.18)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}^{\theta_{\ast}}_{\alpha}\coloneqq\bigg\{(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}})\times[0,\theta_{\ast}]^{2}\,:\,\,\parbox{110.00017pt}{$(u,{\bm{\theta}})$ satisfies~\eqref{eq:approx-admissible-condition-u} and\\
$(u,Du)(\pm 1,\mathrel{\,\cdot\,})=(0,\mathbf{0})$}\,\,\bigg\}.

By construction, for any
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\mathfrak{G}^{\theta_{\ast}}_{\alpha}
,
map
\mathfrak{F}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}
satisfies
that
P_{1}=\mathfrak{F}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(1,0)
,
P_{2}=\mathfrak{F}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(1,1)
,
P_{3}=\mathfrak{F}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(-1,1)
, and
P_{4}=\mathfrak{F}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(-1,0)

for points
P_{i}
,
i=1,2,3,4
, given by Definition
2.9
.
Furthermore,
\varphi^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}=\varphi_{2}
on
\Gamma_{\rm shock}(u,{\bm{\theta}})
.
Other properties of set
\mathfrak{G}^{\theta_{\ast}}_{\alpha}
are given below;
the proof is similar to
[
10
, Lemmas 12.2.7 and 17.2.13]
.

Lemma 4.8
.

Fix
\alpha\in(0,1)
and
\theta_{\ast}\in(0,\theta^{\rm d})
.
For each
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\mathfrak{G}^{\theta_{\ast}}_{\alpha},

\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}\in C^{1,\alpha}([-1,1])

and the following properties hold
:

(i)

\Omega(u,{\bm{\theta}})\cup\Gamma_{\rm shock}(u,{\bm{\theta}})\subseteq Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
.
Moreover,
\Gamma_{\rm shock}(u,{\bm{\theta}})
is a
C^{1,\alpha}
–curve up to
its endpoints
P_{2}
and
P_{3},

and is tangential to
S_{25}
and
S_{26}
at
P_{2}
and
P_{3}
respectively.
If
{f}_{j,0},j=5,6,
are given as in
Step
1
of the proofs
of
Propositions
3.12
and
3.14
,
then

\big(\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}\,,\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}\big)\big|_{s=(-1)^{j-1}}=\big({f}_{j,0}(0)\,,\frac{(-1)^{j}}{d_{L}}{f}^{\prime}_{j,0}(0)\big)\qquad\mbox{for $j=5,6$}\,,

where
d_{L}
is given by (
4.6
).

(ii)

Let
\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}(\Gamma)
denote the open
\varepsilon
-neighbourhood of a set
\Gamma,
as given by (
3.21
).
Let
\delta_{0}
be from
Definition
4.1
,
and
let the coordinates
(x,y)=\mathcal{R}(\bm{\xi})
be given by (
4.1
).
Then there exists a constant
\epsilon_{0}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
such that,
for
j=5,6,
and any
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0}),

\displaystyle\hat{\Omega}^{j}_{\epsilon}\coloneqq\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon_{0}}(\Gamma^{j,\delta_{0}}_{\rm sonic})\cap\big\{\bm{\xi}=\mathcal{R}^{-1}(x,y)\,:\,x_{P_{j-3}}<x<x_{P_{j-3}}+\epsilon\big\}\cap\Omega(u,{\bm{\theta}})

\displaystyle=\big\{\bm{\xi}=\mathcal{R}^{-1}(x,y)\,:\,x_{P_{j-3}}<x<x_{P_{j-3}}+\epsilon,\,0<y<\hat{f}_{j,{\rm sh}}(x)\big\}\,,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\rm shock}(u,{\bm{\theta}})\cap\partial\hat{\Omega}^{j}_{\epsilon}=\big\{\bm{\xi}=\mathcal{R}^{-1}(x,\hat{f}_{j,{\rm sh}}(x))\,:\,x_{P_{j-3}}<x<x_{P_{j-3}}+\epsilon\big\}\,,

\displaystyle\Gamma^{j}_{\rm sonic}\cap\partial\hat{\Omega}^{j}_{\epsilon}=\big\{\bm{\xi}=\mathcal{R}^{-1}(x_{P_{j-3}},y)\,:\,0<y<\hat{f}_{j,{\rm sh}}(x_{P_{j-3}})\big\}=\Gamma^{j}_{\rm sonic}\,,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\rm sym}\cap\partial\hat{\Omega}^{j}_{\epsilon}=\big\{\bm{\xi}=\mathcal{R}^{-1}(x,0)\,:\,x_{P_{j-3}}<x<x_{P_{j-3}}+\epsilon\big\}\,,

where
\hat{f}_{j,{\rm sh}}(x)\coloneqq\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}\circ L_{\bm{\theta}}(u_{j}+(-1)^{j-1}(c_{j}-x))

and
L_{\bm{\theta}}
is given by (
4.6
).

(iii)

Let
(u,{\bm{\theta}}),(\tilde{u},\tilde{{\bm{\theta}}})\in\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha}^{\theta_{\ast}}

satisfy
\|(u,\tilde{u})\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}})}<M
for some constant
M>0
.
Then there exists a constant
C>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast},M,\alpha)

such that

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}([-1,1])}+\|\mathfrak{F}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}})}\leq C\,,

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}-\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(\tilde{u},\tilde{{\bm{\theta}}})}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}([-1,1])}+\|\mathfrak{F}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}-\mathfrak{F}_{(\tilde{u},\tilde{{\bm{\theta}}})}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}})}

\displaystyle\,\,\,+\|\varphi^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}\circ\mathfrak{F}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}-\varphi^{(\tilde{u},\tilde{{\bm{\theta}}})}\circ\mathfrak{F}_{(\tilde{u},\tilde{{\bm{\theta}}})}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}})}+\|\varphi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\ast}\circ\mathfrak{F}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}-\varphi_{\tilde{{\bm{\theta}}}}^{\ast}\circ\mathfrak{F}_{(\tilde{u},\tilde{{\bm{\theta}}})}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}})}

\displaystyle\,\,\,\leq C\big(\|u-\tilde{u}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}})}+|{\bm{\theta}}-\tilde{{\bm{\theta}}}|\big)\,.

(iv)

Let
\epsilon_{0}>0
be the constant from (
ii
)
,

and let
\hat{\epsilon}_{0}\coloneqq d_{L}\epsilon_{0}
with
d_{L}>0
given by (
4.6
).
Assume that, for constants
\alpha\in(0,1),\,\sigma\in(1,2],
and
M>0,

(4.19)

\displaystyle\|u\|_{2,\alpha,\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}\cap\{|s|<1-\frac{\hat{\epsilon}_{0}}{10}\}}+\|u\|^{(\sigma),({\rm par})}_{2,\alpha,\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}\cap\{|s|>1-\hat{\epsilon}_{0}\}}\leq M\,.

Then there exists a constant
C>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast},\alpha,\sigma)
such that

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}\|_{2,\alpha,[-1+\frac{\hat{\epsilon}_{0}}{10},1-\frac{\hat{\epsilon}_{0}}{10}]}+\|\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}-\mathfrak{g}_{5}\|^{(\sigma),({\rm par})}_{2,\alpha,(1,1-\hat{\epsilon}_{0})}+\|\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}-\mathfrak{g}_{6}\|^{(\sigma),({\rm par})}_{2,\alpha,(-1,-1+\hat{\epsilon}_{0})}\leq CM\,,

where
\mathfrak{g}_{5}
and
\mathfrak{g}_{6}
are defined in

4.3
(
ii
)
.

Furthermore, for

I_{\rm so}\coloneqq(-1,-1+\hat{\epsilon}_{0})\cup(1-\hat{\epsilon}_{0},1),

define
\mathfrak{F}_{(0,{\bm{\theta}})}:I_{\rm so}\times(0,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}_{(0,{\bm{\theta}})}(s,t^{\prime})\coloneqq\begin{cases}\,(G_{2,\mathfrak{g}_{5}}\circ\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}})^{-1}(s,t^{\prime})&\quad\text{if}\;\;s\in I_{\rm so}\cap\{s>0\}\,,\\[2.84526pt]
\,(G_{2,\mathfrak{g}_{6}}\circ\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}})^{-1}(s,t^{\prime})&\quad\text{if}\;\;s\in I_{\rm so}\cap\{s<0\}\,.\end{cases}

Then there exists a constant
C_{0}>0

depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast})

such that

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{F}_{(0,{\bm{\theta}})}\|_{C^{3}(\overline{I_{\rm so}}\times[0,1])}+\|\mathfrak{F}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}\|_{2,\alpha,\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}\cap\{|s|<1-\frac{\hat{\epsilon}_{0}}{10}\}}+\|\mathfrak{F}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}-\mathfrak{F}_{(0,{\bm{\theta}})}\|^{(\sigma),({\rm par})}_{2,\alpha,I_{\rm so}\times(0,1)}\leq C_{0}\,.

(v)

Let
f_{\bm{\theta}}
be given by (
4.8
).
For constants
M>0
and
\delta_{\rm sh}>0,
assume that
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha}^{\theta_{\ast}}

satisfies (
4.19
) and, for any
s\in[-1,1],

\displaystyle\min\big\{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(-1)+\frac{s+1}{M},\,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(1)+\frac{1-s}{M},\,\delta_{\rm sh}\big\}\leq\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(s)

\displaystyle\hskip 40.00006pt\leq\min\big\{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(-1)+M(s+1),\,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(1)+M(1-s),f_{\bm{\theta}}(s)-\frac{1}{M}\big\}\,.

Then, for any
\epsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{4}\min{\{s_{5},-s_{6}\}}),
there exists a constant
C_{\epsilon}>0

depending only on

such that

\displaystyle\|(\mathfrak{F}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}^{-1},\varphi-\varphi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\ast})\|_{2,\alpha,\Omega(u,{\bm{\theta}})\setminus(\overline{\hat{\Omega}^{5}_{\epsilon}\cup\hat{\Omega}^{6}_{\epsilon}})}\leq C_{\epsilon}\,.

(vi)

Let

(u,{\bm{\theta}}),(\tilde{u},\tilde{{\bm{\theta}}})\in\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha}^{\theta_{\ast}}
satisfy

for some constant

.
For any open set

with

depending only

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{F}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}-\mathfrak{F}_{(\tilde{u},\tilde{{\bm{\theta}}})}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{K})}\leq C_{\delta}\big(\|(u-\tilde{u})(\cdot,1)\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([-1+\delta,1-\delta])}+|{\bm{\theta}}-\tilde{{\bm{\theta}}}|\big)\,,

\displaystyle\|(\varphi^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}\circ\mathfrak{F}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}-\varphi^{(\tilde{u},\tilde{{\bm{\theta}}})}\circ\mathfrak{F}_{(\tilde{u},\tilde{{\bm{\theta}}})},\,\psi^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}\circ\mathfrak{F}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}-\psi^{(\tilde{u},\tilde{{\bm{\theta}}})}\circ\mathfrak{F}_{(\tilde{u},\tilde{{\bm{\theta}}})}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{K})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\delta}\big(\|u-\tilde{u}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{K})}+|{\bm{\theta}}-\tilde{{\bm{\theta}}}|\big)\,,

where

is given by

for each

.

4.2.
Definition and properties of the iteration set

For any
\theta_{\ast}\in(0,\theta^{\rm d})
, we now define the iteration set

\mathcal{K}\subseteq C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}})\times[0,\theta_{\ast}]^{2}

for some
\alpha\in(0,1)
, as a set of functions satisfying the properties similar to the admissible solutions.
In the definition of
\mathcal{K}
, we construct a nonlinear boundary value problem in a fixed domain
and obtain the well-posedness and
a priori
estimates for the solutions.

4.2.1.
Definition of the iteration set

Before giving the definition of the iteration set,
we collect some useful estimates for admissible solutions.

Lemma 4.9
.

Fix
\theta_{\ast}\in(0,\theta^{{\rm d}})
.
For any admissible solution
\varphi
corresponding
to
{\bm{\theta}}\in\overline{\Theta}\cap\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\leq\theta_{\ast}\}
,

denote
\psi\coloneqq(\varphi-\max\{\varphi_{5},\varphi_{6}\})\circ\mathcal{R}^{-1}
.
Then there exist constants
C_{1},\,\bar{\epsilon}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
,
and
C^{\ast}_{2}>0
depending on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast})
such that,
for any
\epsilon\in(0,\bar{\epsilon}],

\displaystyle\|\varphi-\varphi_{5}\|_{C^{0,1}(\overline{\Omega})}+\|\varphi-\varphi_{6}\|_{C^{0,1}(\overline{\Omega})}\leq C_{1}\,,

\displaystyle|D_{(x,y)}\psi(x,y)|\leq C^{\ast}_{2}x\qquad\text{for}\;(x,y)\in\mathcal{R}\big(\overline{\Omega}\cap(\mathcal{D}^{5}_{\epsilon}\cup\mathcal{D}^{6}_{\epsilon})\big)\,.

Proof.

The first result follows directly from (
2.33
),
Proposition
2.6
, and Lemma
3.3
.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
\theta_{*}\in(\theta^{\rm s},\theta^{\rm d})
.
Then, for sufficiently small
\sigma>0
and any
{\bm{\theta}}\in[0,\theta^{\rm s}+\sigma]\times[0,\theta_{\ast}]
,

|D_{(x,y)}\psi(x,y)|\leq C_{2}x\qquad\,\text{for $(x,y)\in\mathcal{R}\big(\overline{\Omega}\cap\mathcal{D}^{5}_{\epsilon}\big)$}\,,

whenever
\epsilon\in(0,\bar{\epsilon}]
, where
\bar{\epsilon}>0

is the minimum of the constants,
\varepsilon>0
,
in Propositions
3.12
–
3.14
.

From the relations:
\ell(\rho_{5})\cos\theta_{25}=v_{2}
and
M_{2}^{(\theta_{1})}=|(-\xi^{P_{0}^{1}},v_{2})|
,
and Lemmas
A.2
(
i
)
and
2.3
,

(4.20)

\frac{{\rm d}x_{P_{0}^{1}}}{{\rm d}\theta_{1}}=\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}\theta_{1}}\big(c_{5}-(\xi^{P_{0}^{1}}-u_{5})\big)=\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}\theta_{1}}\big(c_{5}-\xi^{P_{0}^{1}}+v_{2}\tan\theta_{25}\big)>0\,.

Then there exists
C^{\prime}_{2}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast})
such that,
for any
{\bm{\theta}}\in[\theta^{\rm s}+\frac{\sigma}{2},\theta_{\ast}]\times[0,\theta_{\ast}]
,

|D_{(x,y)}\psi(x,y)|\leq C^{\prime}_{2}x^{\bar{\alpha}}\leq C^{\prime}_{2}\big(x_{P_{0}^{1}}|_{\theta_{1}=\theta^{\rm s}+\frac{\sigma}{2}}\big)^{\bar{\alpha}-1}x\qquad\,\text{for $(x,y)\in\mathcal{R}\big(\overline{\Omega}\cap\mathcal{D}^{5}_{\epsilon}\big)$}\,,

after possibly reducing
\bar{\epsilon}
further so that
\bar{\epsilon}<r
, for
r>0

from Proposition
3.16
and
\bar{\alpha}\in(0,\frac{1}{3}]

from Proposition
4.6
.
From the symmetry of the four-shock interaction problem, we can repeat the above discussion for
\psi(x,y)
in

\mathcal{R}(\overline{\Omega}\cap\mathcal{D}^{6}_{\epsilon})
.

\square

Define
u^{({\rm norm})}\in C^{3}(\overline{\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}})
by (
4.14
) with
{\bm{\theta}}=\bm{0}

and
\varphi=\varphi_{0}
from §
2.1.3
.
Note that
\varphi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\ast}\equiv\varphi_{0}
in
Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}
by Definition
4.4

because
\varphi_{5}=\varphi_{6}=\varphi_{0}
when
{\bm{\theta}}=\bm{0}
, which yields that

\displaystyle u^{({\rm norm})}\equiv 0\qquad\text{in $\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}$}\,.

Let
\bar{\alpha}\in(0,\frac{1}{3}]
be the constant from Proposition
4.6
,
\epsilon_{0}>0
from Lemma
4.8
(
ii
), and

\bar{\epsilon}>0
from Lemma

.
For constants
\alpha\in(0,\frac{\bar{\alpha}}{2}]
,
\delta_{1}
,
\delta_{2}
,
\delta_{3}
,

, and

to be specified later,
we now define the iteration set

\mathcal{K}\subseteq C^{2,\alpha}_{(\ast)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})\times[0,\theta_{\ast}]^{2}
,
where space

is given by Definition

.
For simplicity, in the following,
we use

that are given by Definition

.

Definition 4.10

(Iteration set)
.

Fix
\theta_{\ast}\in(0,\theta^{\rm d})
.
The iteration set
\mathcal{K}\subseteq C^{2,\alpha}_{(\ast)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})\times[0,\theta_{\ast}]^{2}

is the set of all
(u,{\bm{\theta}})
satisfying the following properties
:

(i)

\|u-u^{({\rm norm})}\|^{(\ast)}_{2,\alpha,\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}}<\mathscr{K}_{1}(\max\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\})

for
\mathscr{K}_{1}\in C^{0,1}(\mathbb{R})
given by

\displaystyle\mathscr{K}_{1}(\theta)=\begin{cases}\delta_{1}&\quad\text{if}\;\;\theta\leq\frac{\delta_{1}}{N_{1}}\,,\\
N_{0}&\quad\text{if}\;\;\theta\geq\frac{2\delta_{1}}{N_{1}}\,,\\
\text{linear}&\quad\text{otherwise}\,,\end{cases}

with
N_{0}\coloneqq\max\{10M,1\}
for

from
Proposition
4.6
.

(ii)

(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha}^{\theta_{\ast}}
,
where

is defined by (
4.18
).

(iii)

\Gamma_{\rm shock}
satisfies
{\rm dist}(\Gamma_{\rm shock},B_{1}(O_{2}))>N_{2}^{-1},
and

\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}
satisfies
\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}(\pm 1)\geq 0
and

\displaystyle\min_{i=1,2}\big\{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}((-1)^{i-1})+\frac{1+(-1)^{i}s}{N_{3}},\,\frac{1}{N_{3}}\big\}\leq\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}(s)

\displaystyle\leq\min_{i=1,2}\big\{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}((-1)^{i-1})+N_{3}(1+(-1)^{i}s),\,f_{\bm{\theta}}(s)-\frac{1}{N_{3}}\big\}\qquad\mbox{for all $s\in(-1,1)$},

with
N_{2}\coloneqq 2C_{\rm sh}
for

from

,

for

from

(

),

and
f_{\bm{\theta}}
given by (
4.8
).

(iv)

Let coordinates
(x,y)=\mathcal{R}(\bm{\xi})
be given by (

) and,

and

(4.21)

\displaystyle\varphi-\max{\{\varphi_{5},\varphi_{6}\}}>\mathscr{K}_{2}(\max\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\})\qquad

\displaystyle\text{in $\overline{\Omega}\setminus(\mathcal{D}^{5}_{\epsilon/10}\cup\mathcal{D}^{6}_{\epsilon/10})$}\,,

\displaystyle\partial_{\bm{e}_{S_{2j}}}(\varphi_{2}-\varphi)<-\mathscr{K}_{2}(\max\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\})\quad

\displaystyle\text{in $\overline{\Omega}\setminus\mathcal{D}^{j}_{\epsilon/10}$ for $j=5,6$}\,,

and

(4.22)

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}\psi(x,y)|<\mathscr{K}_{3}(\theta_{j-4})x\quad

\displaystyle\text{in $\mathcal{R}\big(\overline{\Omega}\cap(\mathcal{D}^{j}_{\epsilon_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{D}^{j}_{\epsilon/10})\big)$ for $j=5,6$}\,,

\displaystyle|\partial_{y}\psi(x,y)|<N_{4}x\quad

\displaystyle\text{in $\mathcal{R}\big(\overline{\Omega}\cap\big((\mathcal{D}^{5}_{\epsilon_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{D}^{5}_{\epsilon/10})\cup(\mathcal{D}^{6}_{\epsilon_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{D}^{6}_{\epsilon/10})\big)\big)$}\,,

\displaystyle|D_{(x,y)}\psi|<N_{4}\epsilon\quad

\displaystyle\text{in $\mathcal{R}\big(\overline{\Omega}\cap(\overline{\mathcal{D}^{5}_{\epsilon}}\cup\overline{\mathcal{D}^{6}_{\epsilon}})\big)$}\,,

\displaystyle\min\left\{\partial_{\bm{\nu}}(\varphi_{2}-\varphi),\,\partial_{\bm{\nu}}\varphi\right\}>\mu_{1}\quad

\displaystyle\text{on $\overline{\Gamma_{\rm shock}}$}\,,

\displaystyle\|\varphi-\varphi_{j}\|_{C^{0,1}(\overline{\Omega})}<N_{5}

\displaystyle\text{for $j=5,6$}\,,

for
\bm{e}_{S_{25}}
and
\bm{e}_{S_{26}}
given by (
2.35
)
,

and the unit normal vector

on

towards the interior of
\Omega
.
In the above conditions, functions
\mathscr{K}_{2},\mathscr{K}_{3}\in C^{0,1}(\mathbb{R})
are defined by

\displaystyle\mathscr{K}_{2}(\theta)\coloneqq\delta_{2}\min\big\{\theta-\frac{\delta_{1}}{N_{1}^{2}},\,\frac{\delta_{1}}{N_{1}^{2}}\big\}\,,\qquad\mathscr{K}_{3}(\theta)\coloneqq\begin{cases}\frac{2-\mu_{0}}{1+\gamma}\quad&\quad\text{if}\;\;0\leq\theta\leq\theta^{\rm s}+\frac{\sigma_{2}}{2}\,,\\
N_{4}\quad&\quad\text{if}\;\;\theta\geq\theta^{\rm s}+\sigma_{2}\,,\\
\text{linear }\quad&\quad\text{otherwise}\,,\end{cases}

for constants
\mu_{1},\,\sigma_{2},\,\mu_{0},\,\epsilon_{0},\,N_{4},
and
N_{5}
chosen as follows
:

•

Let
\delta^{\prime}=\delta^{\prime}(\gamma,v_{2})>0
be from

3.9
.
Choose

.

•

Let

and

of the proof
of

(

)
.

.
Choose

and

.

(v)

The density function
\rho(|D\varphi|,\varphi)
defined by (

)

\displaystyle\frac{\rho^{\ast}(\gamma)}{2}<\rho(|D\varphi|,\varphi)<2C_{\rm ub}\qquad\text{in $\overline{\Omega}\setminus(\mathcal{D}^{5}_{\epsilon/10}\cup\mathcal{D}^{6}_{\epsilon/10})$}

(vi)

Let the sound speed

as defined in (

Function
\varphi
satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{|D\varphi(\bm{\xi})|^{2}}{c^{2}(|D\varphi(\bm{\xi})|,\varphi(\bm{\xi}))}<1-\tilde{\mu}\,{\rm dist}^{\flat}(\bm{\xi},\Gamma^{5}_{\rm sonic}\cup\Gamma^{6}_{\rm sonic})\qquad\text{for $\bm{\xi}\in\overline{\Omega}\setminus\big(\mathcal{D}^{5}_{\epsilon/10}\cup\mathcal{D}^{6}_{\epsilon/10}\big)$}\,,

where

)

and

is defined in (

(vii)

The iteration boundary value problem

(4.23)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\,\mathcal{N}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\hat{\phi})=A_{11}\hat{\phi}_{\xi\xi}+2A_{12}\hat{\phi}_{\xi\eta}+A_{22}\hat{\phi}_{\eta\eta}=0\quad&\quad\text{in $\Omega$}\,,\\
\,\mathcal{M}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(D\hat{\phi},\hat{\phi},\bm{\xi})=0\quad&\quad\text{on $\Gamma_{\rm shock}$}\,,\\
\,\hat{\phi}=\max\{\varphi_{5},\varphi_{6}\}+\frac{1}{2}|\bm{\xi}|^{2}\quad&\quad\text{on $\Gamma^{5}_{\rm sonic}\cup\Gamma^{6}_{\rm sonic}$}\,,\\
\,\partial_{\eta}\hat{\phi}=0\quad&\quad\text{on $\Gamma_{\rm wedge}$}\,,\end{cases}

has a unique solution
\hat{\phi}\in C^{2}(\Omega)\cap C^{1}(\overline{\Omega}),

where the nonlinear operators
\mathcal{N}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}
and
\mathcal{M}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}
are determined later.
Moreover, solution
\hat{\phi}
,
under the transformation
:

(4.24)

\displaystyle\hat{u}\coloneqq(\hat{\phi}-\frac{1}{2}|\bm{\xi}|^{2}-\varphi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\ast})\circ\mathfrak{F}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}\qquad\text{in}\;\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}\,,

satisfies the estimate
:

(4.25)

\displaystyle\|\hat{u}-u\|^{(\ast)}_{2,\alpha,\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}}<\delta_{3}\,.

Definition 4.11

(Extended iteration set)
.

Define the extended iteration set
\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}\coloneqq\big\{(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in C^{2,\alpha}_{(\ast)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})\times[0,\theta_{\ast}]^{2}\,:\,(u,{\bm{\theta}})\text{ \rm satisfies {\rm Definition~\ref{def:iteration-set}\eqref{def:iteration-set-1}--\eqref{def:iteration-set-6}}}\big\}\,.

Write
\overline{\mathcal{K}}
and
\overline{\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}}
for the closures
of
\mathcal{K}
and
\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}

in
C^{2,\alpha}_{(\ast)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})\times[0,\theta_{\ast}]^{2}
respectively.

To complete Definition
4.10
, it remains to construct a suitable iteration boundary value
problem (
4.23
) through the definition of the nonlinear differential
operators
\mathcal{N}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}
and
\mathcal{M}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}
for each
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\overline{\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}}
.
The construction of these operators is similar to
[
2
, §4.4]
.

In the following, we write

\Gamma_{\rm sonic}\coloneqq\Gamma_{\rm sonic}^{5}\cup\Gamma_{\rm sonic}^{6}

and
\mathcal{D}_{r}\coloneqq\mathcal{D}_{r}^{5}\cup\mathcal{D}_{r}^{6}
for any
r>0

with
\mathcal{D}^{5}_{r}
and
\mathcal{D}^{6}_{r}
given by (
4.2
), respectively.
Moreover, for any
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\overline{\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}}
, we define

\displaystyle\phi\coloneqq\varphi+\frac{1}{2}|\bm{\xi}|^{2}\,,\qquad\psi\coloneqq(\varphi-\varphi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\ast})\circ\mathcal{R}^{-1}\equiv(\phi-\phi_{\bm{\theta}}^{\ast})\circ\mathcal{R}^{-1}\,,

where
\varphi=\varphi^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}
is given by Definition
4.7
,
\varphi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\ast}
is given by Definition
4.4
,
\phi_{\bm{\theta}}^{\ast}\coloneqq\varphi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\ast}+\frac{1}{2}|\bm{\xi}|^{2}
,
and coordinates
(x,y)=\mathcal{R}(\bm{\xi})
are given by (
4.1
).

Construction of
\mathcal{N}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}
in (
4.23
).

For
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\overline{\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}}
,
the nonlinear operator
\mathcal{N}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}
in (
4.23
) is defined by

(4.26)

\mathcal{N}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\hat{\phi})\coloneqq\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}A_{ij}(D\hat{\phi},\bm{\xi})\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\hat{\phi}\qquad\,\mbox{for any $\hat{\phi}\in C^{2}(\Omega)$}\,,

with
(\partial_{1},\partial_{2})\coloneqq(\partial_{\xi},\partial_{\eta})

and coefficients
A_{ij}(\bm{p},\bm{\xi})
,
i,j=1,2
, determined below such that
equation
\mathcal{N}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\hat{\phi})=0

is strictly elliptic in
\overline{\Omega}\setminus\Gamma_{\rm sonic}
and,
if
\phi
solves (
4.23
), then

\mathcal{N}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\phi)=0
coincides with (
3.1
).
Coefficients
A_{ij}(\bm{p},\bm{\xi})
are constructed
similarly to
[
2
, §4.4.1]
, the main difference being that both sonic boundaries
may now degenerate into single points.
We briefly describe the construction in the following four steps
and highlight where the differences arise.

1. Away from
\Gamma_{\rm sonic}
.

Let
\epsilon_{0}>0
be the constant from Lemma
4.8
(
ii
),
and let
\epsilon_{\rm eq}\in(0,\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2})
be chosen later.
Away from
\Gamma_{\rm sonic}
, coefficients
A_{ij}(\bm{p},\bm{\xi})
are independent of
\bm{p}
.
Indeed, for
i,j=1,2
, and
\bm{\xi}\in\Omega\setminus\mathcal{D}_{\epsilon_{\rm eq}/10}
, we define
A^{(1)}_{ij}(\bm{\xi})
by

(4.27)

\displaystyle A^{(1)}_{ij}(\bm{\xi})\coloneqq A^{\rm pot}_{ij}(D\phi(\bm{\xi}),\phi(\bm{\xi}),\bm{\xi})\,,

where

A^{\rm pot}_{ij}(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})\coloneqq\delta_{ij}c^{2}-(p_{i}-\bm{\xi}\cdot\bm{e}_{i})(p_{j}-\bm{\xi}\cdot\bm{e}_{j})

for
c=c(|\bm{p}-\bm{\xi}|,z-\frac{1}{2}|\bm{\xi}|^{2})

given in Definition
4.10
(
vi
), where
\bm{e}_{1}\coloneqq(1,0)
and
\bm{e}_{2}\coloneqq(0,1)
.

2. Supersonic near
\Gamma_{\rm sonic}
.

For constant
\mu_{0}>0
from Definition
4.10
(
iv
),
fix an odd function of a single variable
\zeta_{1}\in C^{3}(\mathbb{R})
such that,
for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\zeta_{1}(s)=\begin{cases}s&\quad\text{if}\;|s|\leq\frac{2-\frac{\mu_{0}}{5}}{1+\gamma}\,,\\
\frac{(2-\frac{\mu_{0}}{10})\sgn(s)}{1+\gamma}&\quad\text{if}\;|s|>\frac{2}{1+\gamma}\,,\end{cases}\quad 0\leq\zeta_{1}^{\prime}(s)\leq 10\,,\quad-\frac{20(1+\gamma)}{\mu_{0}}\leq\zeta_{1}^{\prime\prime}(|s|)\leq 0\,.

For any
\hat{\phi}\in C^{2}(\Omega)
, set

\displaystyle\hat{\psi}\coloneqq\big(\hat{\phi}-(\varphi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\ast}+\frac{1}{2}|\bm{\xi}|^{2})\big)\circ\mathcal{R}^{-1}\equiv\big(\hat{\phi}-\phi_{\bm{\theta}}^{\ast}\big)\circ\mathcal{R}^{-1}\qquad\text{in $\mathcal{R}(\Omega\cap\mathcal{D}_{2\epsilon_{\rm eq}})$}\,,

and define the nonlinear operator
\mathcal{N}^{\rm polar}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\hat{\phi})

in
\Omega\cap\mathcal{D}_{2\epsilon_{\rm eq}}
by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{N}^{\rm polar}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\hat{\phi})\coloneqq\Big(\big(2x-(\gamma+1)x\,\zeta_{1}(\frac{\hat{\psi}_{x}}{x})+\mathcal{O}^{\rm mod}_{1}\big)\hat{\psi}_{xx}&+\mathcal{O}^{\rm mod}_{2}\hat{\psi}_{xy}+\big(\frac{1}{c_{\bm{\theta}}}+\mathcal{O}^{\rm mod}_{3}\big)\hat{\psi}_{yy}\\
&\quad-{\,}\big(1+\mathcal{O}^{\rm mod}_{4}\big)\hat{\psi}_{x}+\mathcal{O}^{\rm mod}_{5}\hat{\psi}_{y}\Big)\circ\mathcal{R}\,,\end{split}

with the modified remainder terms
\mathcal{O}_{j}^{\rm mod}
,
j=1,\cdots,5
, given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{{\rm mod}}_{j}\coloneqq\mathcal{O}_{j}\big(x^{3/4}\zeta_{1}\big(\frac{\hat{\psi}_{x}}{x^{3/4}}\big),N_{4}(\gamma+1)x\,\zeta_{1}\big(\frac{\hat{\psi}_{y}}{N_{4}(\gamma+1)x}\big),\psi(x,y),x,c_{\bm{\theta}}\big)\,,

where

\mathcal{O}_{j}(p_{x},p_{y},z,x,c)
are given by (
3.26
),
constant
N_{4}>0
is from Definition
4.10
(
iv
),
and
c_{\bm{\theta}}\coloneqq c_{5}(1-\chi_{\bm{\theta}}^{\ast})+c_{6}\chi_{\bm{\theta}}^{\ast}
with
function
\chi_{\bm{\theta}}^{\ast}
from Definition
4.4
.

For

(\bm{p},\bm{\xi})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\Omega\cap\mathcal{D}_{2\epsilon_{\rm eq}})
,
coefficients
A^{(2)}_{ij}(\bm{p},\bm{\xi}),\,i,j=1,2,
are defined to be the second-order coefficients
of operator
c_{\bm{\theta}}\mathcal{N}^{\rm polar}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\hat{\phi})
in the
\bm{\xi}
–coordinates,
that is,

\displaystyle c_{\bm{\theta}}\mathcal{N}^{\rm polar}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\hat{\phi})\eqqcolon\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}A^{(2)}_{ij}(D_{\bm{\xi}}\hat{\phi},\bm{\xi})\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\hat{\phi}\qquad\,\,\text{in $\Omega\cap\mathcal{D}_{2\epsilon_{\rm eq}}$}\,.

In particular, there are no lower order terms in the above expression,
since they vanish identically in
\Omega\cap\mathcal{D}_{2\epsilon_{\rm eq}}
,
which can be verified by direct computation.

3. Subsonic cut-off near
\Gamma_{\rm sonic}
.

For any
\sigma\in(0,1)
, there exists a constant
C_{\sigma}>0

depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast},\sigma)
such that,
for any
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\overline{\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}}
, there is a function

v_{\sigma}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}\in C^{4}(\overline{\Omega})
with

(a)

\|v_{\sigma}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}-\phi\|_{C^{1}(\overline{\Omega})}\leq\sigma^{2}
and
\|v_{\sigma}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}\|_{C^{4}(\overline{\Omega})}\leq C_{\sigma}
;

(b)

For any sequence
\{(u_{k},{\bm{\theta}}_{k})\}\subset\overline{\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}}

converging to
(u,{\bm{\theta}})
in
C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}})\times[0,\theta_{\ast}]^{2}
,

\displaystyle v_{\sigma}^{(u_{k},{\bm{\theta}}_{k})}\circ\mathfrak{F}_{(u_{k},{\bm{\theta}}_{k})}\to v_{\sigma}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}\circ\mathfrak{F}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}\qquad\text{in $C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}})$}\,.

The proof of this statement can be found in
[
2
, Lemma 4.26]
.

For any
\sigma>0
, fix a family of cut-off functions
\varsigma_{\sigma}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
satisfying

(4.28)

\displaystyle\varsigma_{\sigma}(t)\coloneqq\varsigma_{1}\big(\frac{t}{\sigma}\big)\qquad\,\,\,\text{with}\,\,\,\varsigma_{1}(t)=\begin{cases}1&\,\,\text{for}\;t<1\,,\\
0&\,\,\text{for}\;t>2\,,\end{cases}\quad\mbox{and}\,\,\,0\leq\varsigma_{1}\leq 1\,\,\,\text{on $\mathbb{R}$}\,.

For a constant
\sigma_{\rm cf}\in(0,1)
to be specified later,
we define the subsonic coefficients:

\displaystyle A^{\rm subs}_{ij}(\bm{p},\bm{\xi})\coloneqq\varsigma_{\sigma_{\rm cf}}A^{\rm pot}_{ij}(\bm{p},\phi(\bm{\xi}),\bm{\xi})+(1-\varsigma_{\sigma_{\rm cf}})A^{\rm pot}_{ij}(Dv_{\sigma_{\rm cf}}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{\xi}),\phi(\bm{\xi}),\bm{\xi})\qquad\mbox{for $i,j=1,2$}\,,

with
\varsigma_{\sigma_{\rm cf}}=\varsigma_{\sigma_{\rm cf}}(|\bm{p}-Dv_{\sigma_{\rm cf}}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{\xi})|)
.
In particular, from now on, we choose
\sigma_{\rm cf}\coloneqq\sqrt{\delta_{1}}
.

Fix a cut-off function
\chi_{\rm eq}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
satisfying

\displaystyle\chi_{\rm eq}(\theta)=\begin{cases}1&\,\,\,\text{for}\;\theta\leq\theta^{\rm s}+\frac{\sigma_{3}}{4}\,,\\
0&\,\,\,\text{for}\;\theta\geq\theta^{\rm s}+\frac{\sigma_{3}}{2}\,,\end{cases}\qquad\chi^{\prime}_{\rm eq}(\theta)\leq 0\,\,\,\,\text{on $\mathbb{R}$}\,.

Then, for

(\bm{p},\bm{\xi})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\Omega\cap\mathcal{D}_{2\epsilon_{\rm eq}})
,
coefficients
A^{(3)}_{ij}(\bm{p},\bm{\xi}),\,i,j=1,2,
are defined by

\displaystyle A^{(3)}_{ij}(\bm{p},\bm{\xi})\coloneqq\chi_{\rm eq}(\theta_{l-4})A_{ij}^{(2)}(\bm{p},\bm{\xi})+(1-\chi_{\rm eq}(\theta_{l-4}))A_{ij}^{{\rm subs}}(\bm{p},\bm{\xi})\qquad\text{for $\bm{\xi}\in\Omega\cap\mathcal{D}^{l}_{2\epsilon_{\rm eq}},\,l=5,6$}\,.

4. Coefficients
A_{ij}(\bm{p},\bm{\xi})
.

We now complete the definition of coefficients
A_{ij}(\bm{p},\bm{\xi})

for the nonlinear differential operator
\mathcal{N}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}
given by (
4.26
).
Let
\epsilon_{0}
be from Lemma
4.8
(
ii
).
For each
\epsilon\in(0,\tfrac{\epsilon_{0}}{2})
, fix a family of cut-off functions

\zeta_{2}^{(\epsilon,{\bm{\theta}})}\in C^{4}(\overline{Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}})
with the following properties:

(a)

Function

\zeta_{2}^{(\epsilon,\,\mathclap{\cdot}\,)}(\cdot):(\bm{\xi},{\bm{\theta}})\mapsto\zeta_{2}^{(\epsilon,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{\xi})

is continuous on
\cup_{{\bm{\theta}}\in[0,\theta_{\ast}]^{2}}Q^{\bm{\theta}}\times\{{\bm{\theta}}\}
;

(b)

There exists a constant
C_{\epsilon}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\epsilon)

such that
\|\zeta_{2}^{(\epsilon,{\bm{\theta}})}\|_{C^{4}(\overline{Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}})}\leq C_{\epsilon}
;

(c)

\,\zeta_{2}^{(\epsilon,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{\xi})=\begin{cases}1&\quad\text{for}\;\bm{\xi}\in\Omega\setminus\mathcal{D}_{\epsilon}\,,\\
0&\quad\text{for}\;\bm{\xi}\in\Omega\cap\mathcal{D}_{\epsilon/2}\,.\end{cases}

Such a family of cut-off functions is constructed in
[
2
, Definition 4.28]
.

Coefficients
A_{ij}(\bm{p},\bm{\xi}),\,i,j=1,2,
are defined by

(4.29)

\displaystyle A_{ij}(\bm{p},\bm{\xi})\coloneqq\zeta_{2}^{(\epsilon_{\rm eq},{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{\xi})A_{ij}^{(1)}(\bm{\xi})+(1-\zeta_{2}^{(\epsilon_{\rm eq},{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{\xi}))A_{ij}^{(3)}(\bm{p},\bm{\xi})\qquad\mbox{for $(\bm{p},\bm{\xi})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\Omega$}\,.

We have the following lemma concerning the properties of coefficients
A_{ij}
; see
[
2
, Lemma 4.30]
.

Lemma 4.12
.

There exist positive constants

\epsilon^{(1)},\delta_{1}^{(1)},\epsilon_{\rm eq}\in(0,\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2}),\lambda_{0}\in(0,1),N_{\rm eq}\geq 1,

and
C>0

with
(\epsilon^{(1)},\delta_{1}^{(1)},\lambda_{0})
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2}),

(\epsilon_{\rm eq},N_{\rm eq})
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast}),

and
C>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast},\alpha)

such that,
whenever
(\epsilon,\delta_{1})
from
Definition
4.10

are chosen from
(0,\epsilon^{(1)}]\times(0,\delta_{1}^{(1)}],

for each
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\overline{\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}},
coefficients
A_{ij}(\bm{p},\bm{\xi})

of
\mathcal{N}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}
given by (
4.29
) satisfy the following properties
:

(i)

A_{12}(\bm{p},\bm{\xi})=A_{21}(\bm{p},\bm{\xi})

for all
(\bm{p},\bm{\xi})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\Omega
,

and
A_{ij}(\bm{p},\bm{\xi})=A_{ij}^{(1)}(\bm{\xi})

if
\bm{\xi}\in\Omega\setminus\mathcal{D}_{\epsilon_{\rm eq}}
.
Furthermore,
D_{\bm{p}}^{m}A_{ij}\in C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\overline{\Omega}\setminus\Gamma_{\rm sonic}))

for
m=0,1,2,

\lambda_{0}\,{\rm dist}(\bm{\xi},\,\Gamma_{\rm sonic})|\bm{\kappa}|^{2}\leq\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}A_{ij}(\bm{p},\bm{\xi})\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}\leq\lambda_{0}^{-1}|\bm{\kappa}|^{2}\qquad\,\mbox{for any $\bm{\kappa}=(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}$}\,,\\

for any
(\bm{p},\bm{\xi})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\Omega,
and

\displaystyle\|A_{ij}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\Omega)}+\|A_{ij}(\bm{p},\cdot)\|_{C^{3/4}(\overline{\Omega})}+\|D_{\bm{p}}A_{ij}(\bm{p},\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq N_{\rm eq}\qquad\mbox{for any $\bm{p}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}$}\,,

\displaystyle\|A_{ij}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\,\overline{\Omega\setminus\mathcal{D}_{\epsilon_{\rm eq}}}\,)}+s^{5}\|D_{\bm{p}}^{m}A_{ij}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\overline{\Omega}\setminus\mathcal{N}_{s}(\Gamma_{\rm sonic})))}\leq C\qquad\text{for each $s\in(0,\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2})$}\,.

(ii)

A_{ij}(\bm{p},\bm{\xi})=A_{ij}^{(3)}(\bm{p},\bm{\xi})

for any

(\bm{p},\bm{\xi})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\Omega\cap\mathcal{D}_{\epsilon_{\rm eq}/2}^{l}),\,l=5,6
.
Furthermore, if
\theta_{l-4}\leq\theta^{\rm s}+\frac{\sigma_{3}}{4},

then
A_{ij}(\bm{p},\bm{\xi})=A^{(2)}_{ij}(\bm{p},\bm{\xi})
;

whereas, if
\theta_{l-4}\in[\theta^{\rm s}+\delta,\theta_{\ast}]

for some
\delta\in(0,\frac{\sigma_{3}}{2}),

then

\displaystyle\lambda_{0}({\rm dist}(\bm{\xi},\,\Gamma_{\rm sonic})+\delta)|\bm{\kappa}|^{2}\leq\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}A_{ij}(\bm{p},\bm{\xi})\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}\leq\lambda_{0}^{-1}|\bm{\kappa}|^{2}\qquad\mbox{for any $\bm{\xi}\in\Omega\cap\mathcal{D}^{l}_{\epsilon_{\rm eq}/2}$}\,,

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{p}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}\|D_{\bm{p}}^{m}A_{ij}(\bm{p},\cdot)\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega\cap\mathcal{D}^{l}_{\epsilon_{\rm eq}/2}})}\leq C\qquad\text{for $m=0,1,2$}\,.

(iii)

Suppose that
\epsilon
from
Definition
4.10

satisfies
\epsilon\in(0,\frac{\epsilon_{\rm eq}}{2})
.
Then the equation
:

\mathcal{N}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\phi)=0

coincides with (
3.1
) in
\Omega\setminus\mathcal{D}_{\epsilon/10}
.
In addition, for
l=5,6,
if
x_{P_{0}^{l-4}}\geq\frac{\epsilon}{10}

or
\theta_{l-4}\geq\theta^{\rm s}+\frac{\sigma_{3}}{2},

then the equation
:

\mathcal{N}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\phi)=0
coincides with (
3.1
) in
\Omega\setminus\mathcal{D}^{11-l}_{\epsilon/10}
.

Construction of
\mathcal{M}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}
in (
4.23
).

We turn our attention to the definition of the nonlinear linear operator

\mathcal{M}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})
in (
4.23
).
The construction is presented in the following four steps that
follow closely
[
2
, §4.4.2]
.

1.

For
g^{\rm sh}
given by (
3.27
), set

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{0}(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})\coloneqq g^{\rm sh}(\bm{p}-\bm{\xi},z-\frac{1}{2}|\bm{\xi}|^{2},\bm{\xi})\,,\qquad\mathcal{M}_{1}(\bm{p},z,\xi)\coloneqq\mathcal{M}_{0}(\bm{p},z,(\xi,\frac{z}{v_{2}}))\,.

For
j=5,6
, write
\phi_{j}\coloneqq\varphi_{j}+\frac{1}{2}|\bm{\xi}|^{2}
,
which is independent of the
\eta
–coordinate since
O_{j}\in\{\eta=0\}
.
Then
(\xi,\frac{1}{v_{2}}\phi_{j}(\bm{\xi}))\in\{\varphi_{j}=\varphi_{2}\}

for any
\bm{\xi}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and
\mathcal{M}_{1}
is homogeneous in the sense that

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{1}(D\phi_{j}(\bm{\xi}),\phi_{j}(\bm{\xi}),\xi)=0\qquad\text{for all $\bm{\xi}=(\xi,\eta)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}$ and $j=5,6$}\,.

Furthermore, observe that
\bm{\xi}=(\xi,\eta)\in\Gamma_{\rm shock}

if and only if
\bm{\xi}\in\overline{\Omega}
with
\eta=\frac{1}{v_{2}}\phi(\bm{\xi})
, and hence

(4.30)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathcal{M}_{1}(D\phi(\bm{\xi}),\phi(\bm{\xi}),\xi)=\mathcal{M}_{0}(D\phi(\bm{\xi}),\phi(\bm{\xi}),\bm{\xi})\,,\\[2.84526pt]
D_{\bm{p}}\mathcal{M}_{1}(D\phi(\bm{\xi}),\phi(\bm{\xi}),\xi)=D_{\bm{p}}\mathcal{M}_{0}(D\phi(\bm{\xi}),\phi(\bm{\xi}),\bm{\xi})\,,\end{cases}\quad\text{for}\;\bm{\xi}\in\Gamma_{\rm shock}\,.

2.

For the cut-off function
\varsigma_{\sigma}
given by (
4.28
)
and a constant
\sigma_{\rm bc}>0
to be determined later, we set

(4.31)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{M}(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})\coloneqq&\,\big(\varsigma_{\sigma_{\rm bc}}^{(5)}\varsigma_{\sigma_{\rm bc}}^{(6)}+(1-\varsigma_{\sigma_{\rm bc}}^{(5)})\varsigma_{\sigma_{\rm bc}}^{(6)}+\varsigma_{\sigma_{\rm bc}}^{(5)}(1-\varsigma_{\sigma_{\rm bc}}^{(6)})\big)\mathcal{M}_{1}(\bm{p},z,\xi)\\
&\;+(1-\varsigma_{\sigma_{\rm bc}}^{(5)})(1-\varsigma_{\sigma_{\rm bc}}^{(6)})\mathcal{M}_{0}(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})\,,\end{split}

where
\varsigma_{\sigma_{\rm bc}}^{(j)}\coloneqq\varsigma_{\sigma_{\rm bc}}(|(\bm{p},z)-(D\phi_{j},\phi_{j}(\bm{\xi}))|)
for
j=5,6
.
There exist constants
\sigma_{\rm bc},\delta_{\rm bc},\bar{\epsilon}_{\rm bc}>0

depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
such that,
if
\epsilon
from Definition
4.10

satisfies
\epsilon\in(0,\bar{\epsilon}_{\rm bc})
, then

\mathcal{M}(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})
satisfies that, for all
\bm{\xi}\in\Gamma_{\rm shock}
,

\displaystyle\delta_{\rm bc}\leq D_{\bm{p}}\mathcal{M}(D\phi(\bm{\xi}),\phi(\bm{\xi}),\bm{\xi})\cdot\bm{\nu}_{\rm sh}(\bm{\xi})\leq\delta_{\rm bc}^{-1}\,,\qquad D_{z}\mathcal{M}(D\phi(\bm{\xi}),\phi(\bm{\xi}),\bm{\xi})\leq-\delta_{\rm bc}\,,

where
\bm{\nu}_{\rm sh}
is the unit normal vector to

\Gamma_{\rm shock}
towards the interior of
\Omega
.
These results follow from the calculations in
[
2
, Lemma 4.32]
.
Indeed, the first follows from a direct calculation
by using (
4.30
),
[
2
, Eq. A.18]
,
the fact that
\mathcal{M}_{0}
is
C^{1}
,
and the properties
in Definition
4.10
(
vi
)–(
v
).
For the second, direct computation gives

\displaystyle D_{z}\mathcal{M}_{0}(D\phi(\bm{\xi}),\phi(\bm{\xi}),\bm{\xi})

\displaystyle=-\rho^{2-\gamma}D\varphi\cdot\bm{\nu}_{\rm sh}(\bm{\xi})<0\qquad\text{for $\bm{\xi}\in\Gamma_{\rm shock}$}\,,

whilst, for
j=5,6
, and any
\bm{\xi}=(\xi,\eta)\in Q^{\bm{\theta}}
, we have

\displaystyle D_{z}\mathcal{M}_{1}(D\phi_{j}(\bm{\xi}),\phi_{j}(\bm{\xi}),\xi)

\displaystyle=-\rho_{j}^{2-\gamma}\,{\rm dist}(O_{j},S_{2j})+(-1)^{j}\frac{\rho_{j}-1}{v_{2}}\cos{\theta_{2j}}<0\,,

after which an argument similar to
[
2
, Lemma 4.32]
applies.
The constant,
\sigma_{\rm bc}>0
, is now fixed.

3.

For
j=5,6
, set

(4.32)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{(j)}(\bm{q},z,\bm{\xi})\coloneqq\mathcal{M}(\bm{q}+D\phi_{j},z+\phi_{j}(\bm{\xi}),\bm{\xi})\,.

Then, for the coordinates
(x,y)=\bm{x}=\mathcal{R}(\bm{\xi})
given by (
4.1
), define

(4.33)

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{M}}^{(j)}(q_{x},q_{y},z,\bm{x})\coloneqq\mathcal{M}^{(j)}\big((-1)^{j}(q_{x}\cos{y}+\frac{q_{y}\sin{y}}{c_{j}-x}),-q_{x}\sin{y}+\frac{q_{y}\cos{y}}{c_{j}-x},z,\mathcal{R}^{-1}(\bm{x})\big)\,.

4.

Finally, we extend the definition of
\mathcal{M}
in (
4.31
)
to all
(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}\times\overline{\Omega}
.
For each
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\overline{\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}}
and a constant
\sigma>0
, let
v^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}_{\sigma}\in C^{4}(\overline{\Omega})

be given as in Step
3
in the construction of
\mathcal{N}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}

above.
We define a linear operator
\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})
by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})\coloneqq&\,\mathcal{M}(Dv_{\sigma}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{\xi}),v_{\sigma}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{\xi}),\bm{\xi})+D_{\bm{p}}\mathcal{M}(Dv_{\sigma}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{\xi}),v_{\sigma}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{\xi}),\bm{\xi})\cdot\bm{p}\\
&\;\;+D_{z}\mathcal{M}(Dv_{\sigma}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{\xi}),v_{\sigma}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{\xi}),\bm{\xi})z\,,\end{split}

The operator,
\mathcal{M}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})
, in (
4.23
)
is then defined by

(4.34)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})\coloneqq\varsigma_{\sigma}\mathcal{M}(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})+(1-\varsigma_{\sigma})\mathcal{L}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}_{\sigma}(\bm{p}-Dv^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}_{\sigma}(\bm{\xi}),z-v^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}_{\sigma}(\bm{\xi}),\bm{\xi})

for
\varsigma_{\sigma}\coloneqq\varsigma_{\sigma}(|(\bm{p},z)-(Dv^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}_{\sigma}(\bm{\xi}),v^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}_{\sigma}(\bm{\xi}))|)

given by (
4.28
).
We have the following lemma concerning the properties of the nonlinear operator
\mathcal{M}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})
;
see
[
2
, Lemma 4.34]
.

Lemma 4.13
.

Let
\bar{\epsilon}_{\rm bc}
be the constant from
Step
2
above.
Then there exist positive constants
\epsilon_{\rm bc}\in(0,\bar{\epsilon}_{\rm bc}),\delta_{1}^{(2)},N^{(1)}_{1},\delta_{\rm bc},C,C_{\theta_{\ast}},

and
\epsilon_{\mathcal{M}}\in(0,\epsilon_{\rm bc}]

with
(\epsilon_{\rm bc},\delta^{(2)}_{1},N^{(1)}_{1},\delta_{\rm bc},C)

depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2}),

\epsilon_{\mathcal{M}}
depending on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast}),

and
C_{\theta_{\ast}}
depending on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast},\alpha)
such that,
whenever parameters
(\epsilon,\delta_{1},N_{1})

from
Definition
4.10
are chosen from

(0,\bar{\epsilon}_{\rm bc}]\times(0,\delta_{1}^{(2)}]\times[N_{1}^{(1)},\infty),

the following statements hold
:

For each
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\overline{\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}},

operator
\mathcal{M}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}:\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}\times\overline{\Omega}\to\mathbb{R}

given by (
4.34
) with
\sigma=\sigma_{\rm cf}\equiv\sqrt{\delta_{1}}
satisfies

(i)

\mathcal{M}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}\in C^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}\times\overline{\Omega})
and,
for all
(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}\times\overline{\Omega}
and
\bm{\xi}_{\rm sh}\in\overline{\Gamma_{\rm shock}},

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{M}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{0},0,\cdot),D^{m}_{(\bm{p},z)}\mathcal{M}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{p},z,\cdot))\|_{C^{3}(\overline{\Omega})}\leq C_{\theta_{\ast}}\qquad\text{for $m=1,2,3\,$},

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})=\mathcal{M}(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})\qquad\text{for $\left|(\bm{p},z)-(D\phi(\bm{\xi}),\phi(\bm{\xi}))\right|<\frac{1}{2}{\sqrt{\delta_{1}}}$}\,,

\displaystyle|D_{(\bm{p},z)}\mathcal{M}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})-D_{(\bm{p},z)}\mathcal{M}(D\phi(\bm{\xi}),\phi(\bm{\xi}),\bm{\xi})|\leq C\sqrt{\delta_{1}}\,,

\displaystyle\delta_{\rm bc}\leq D_{\bm{p}}\mathcal{M}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi}_{\rm sh})\cdot\bm{\nu}_{\rm sh}\leq\frac{1}{\delta_{\rm bc}},\qquad D_{z}\mathcal{M}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi}_{\rm sh})\leq-\delta_{\rm bc}\,,

where
\mathcal{M}(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})
is defined by (
4.31
)
,

and
\bm{\nu}_{\rm sh}
is the unit normal vector to
\Gamma_{\rm shock}
pointing into
\Omega
.

(ii)

Denote
\mathcal{B}_{\sigma,\Gamma_{\rm shock}}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})\coloneqq\mathcal{L}_{\sigma}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{p}-Dv_{\sigma}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{\xi}),z-v_{\sigma}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{\xi}),\bm{\xi})
and set

\mathcal{B}_{\sigma,\Gamma_{\rm shock}}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})\eqqcolon(b_{1}^{\rm sh}(\bm{\xi}),b_{2}^{\rm sh}(\bm{\xi}),b_{0}^{\rm sh}(\bm{\xi}),h^{\rm sh}(\bm{\xi}))\cdot(p_{1},p_{2},z,1)\,.

Then

\|(b_{i}^{\rm sh},h^{\rm sh})\|_{C^{3}(\overline{\Gamma_{\rm shock}})}\leq C_{\theta_{*}}

for
i=0,1,2
,
and, for all
(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}\times\overline{\Omega},

\displaystyle\|\big(\mathcal{M}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}-\mathcal{B}_{\sigma_{\rm cf},\Gamma_{\rm shock}}\big)(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})\|\leq C\sqrt{\delta_{1}}\,\char 12\relax{(\bm{p},z)-\big(Dv_{\sigma_{\rm cf}}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{\xi}),v_{\sigma_{\rm cf}}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{\xi})\big)}\,,

\displaystyle\left|D_{(\bm{p},z)}\big(\mathcal{M}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}-\mathcal{B}_{{\sigma_{\rm cf}},\Gamma_{\rm shock}}\big)(\bm{p},z,\bm{\xi})\right|\leq C\sqrt{\delta_{1}}\,.

(iii)

\mathcal{M}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}
is homogeneous in the sense that, for
j=5,6,

\displaystyle\qquad\mathcal{M}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(D\phi_{j}(\bm{\xi}),\phi_{j}(\bm{\xi}),\bm{\xi})=0\qquad\text{for all}\;\begin{cases}\,\bm{\xi}\in\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap\mathcal{D}_{\epsilon_{\mathcal{M}}}&\,\,\text{for any ${\bm{\theta}}\in[0,\theta_{\ast}]^{2}$}\,,\\
\,\bm{\xi}\in\Gamma_{\rm shock}&\,\,\text{when $\max\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\}\in[0,\frac{\delta_{1}}{N_{1}}]$}\,.\end{cases}

(iv)

For coordinates
(x,y)=\mathcal{R}(\bm{\xi})
given by (
4.1
)
,

j=5,6,
and
\bm{\xi}\in\overline{\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap\mathcal{D}^{j}_{\epsilon_{\rm bc}}},

define
\hat{\mathcal{M}}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}^{(j)}(q_{x},q_{y},z,x,y)
by (
4.33
) with
\mathcal{M}

replaced by
\mathcal{M}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}
in (
4.32
).
Then, for
j=5,6,

\hat{\mathcal{M}}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}^{(j)}
satisfies the following properties
when
\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap\mathcal{D}^{j}_{\epsilon_{\rm bc}}
is non-empty
:

\displaystyle\|\hat{\mathcal{M}}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}^{(j)}\|_{C^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{R}(\overline{\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap\mathcal{D}^{j}_{\epsilon_{\rm bc}}}))}\leq C_{\theta_{\ast}}\,,

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{M}}^{(j)}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{q},z,x,y)=\hat{\mathcal{M}}^{(j)}(\bm{q},z,x,y)\qquad\mbox{in
$\mathcal{R}(\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap\mathcal{D}^{j}_{\epsilon_{\rm bc}})\,$
for any $|(\bm{q},z)|\leq\frac{\delta_{\rm bc}}{C}\,$}{\rm,}

\displaystyle\partial_{a}\hat{\mathcal{M}}^{(j)}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{q},z,x,y)\leq-\delta_{\rm bc}\qquad\text{in $\mathcal{R}(\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap\mathcal{D}^{j}_{\epsilon_{\mathcal{M}}})\,$
for any $(\bm{q},z)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R},$}

with
\partial_{a}=\partial_{q_{x}},\,\partial_{q_{y}}
or
\partial_{z},

provided that
\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap\mathcal{D}^{j}_{\epsilon_{\mathcal{M}}}
is non-empty.

(v)

\mathcal{M}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(D\phi(\bm{\xi}),\phi(\bm{\xi}),\bm{\xi})=0
on
\Gamma_{\rm shock}
if and only if
\varphi=\phi-\frac{1}{2}|\bm{\xi}|^{2}
satisfies the Rankine–Hugoniot jump condition (
3.27
) on
\Gamma_{\rm shock}=\{\varphi=\varphi_{2}\}
.

4.2.2.
Well-posedness of the boundary value problem (
4.23
)

Since the complete definition of the iteration boundary value problem (
4.23
) has been given,
we now consider its well-posedness.

Lemma 4.14
.

Fix
\gamma\geq 1,\,v_{2}\in(v_{\min},0)
, and
\theta_{\ast}\in(0,\theta^{\rm d})
.
Let constant
\sigma_{2}
be from
Step
1
of the proof
of
Proposition
3.14
.
Let
\bar{\alpha}\in(0,\frac{1}{3}]
and
\epsilon_{0}>0
be from
Proposition
4.6

and
Lemma
4.8
(
ii
)
respectively,
and let
\alpha\in(0,\frac{\bar{\alpha}}{2}]
be from
Definition
4.10
.
Then there exist constants
\epsilon^{\rm(w)}\in(0,\epsilon_{0}],

\delta_{1}^{\rm(w)}\in(0,1),

N_{1}^{\rm(w)}\geq 1,

and
\alpha_{1}^{\ast}\in(0,\bar{\alpha}]
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast})
such that,
whenever parameters
(\epsilon,\delta_{1},N_{1})
in
Definition
4.10
are chosen
with
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon^{\rm(w)}],

\delta_{1}\in(0,\delta_{1}^{\rm(w)}],
and
N_{1}\geq N_{1}^{\rm(w)},

the following statements hold
:

Case (i).

If
\max\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\}\leq\theta^{\rm s}+\sigma_{2},

then the boundary value problem (
4.23
) associated
with
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\overline{\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}}\cap\{\max\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\}\leq\theta^{\rm s}+\sigma_{2}\}

has a unique solution

\hat{\phi}\in C^{2}(\Omega)\cap C^{1}(\overline{\Omega}\setminus({\Gamma^{5}_{\rm sonic}}\cup{\Gamma^{6}_{\rm sonic}}))\cap C^{0}(\overline{\Omega})
.
Moreover, there exists a constant
C>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast},\alpha)
such that solution
\hat{\phi}
satisfies

(4.35)

\|\hat{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq C\,,\qquad\,\,|\hat{\phi}(\bm{\xi})-\phi_{{\bm{\theta}}}(\bm{\xi})|\leq C\,{\rm dist}(\bm{\xi},\,\Gamma^{5}_{\rm sonic}\cup\Gamma^{6}_{\rm sonic})\,\,\,\,\,\text{in $\Omega$}\,,

for
\phi_{{\bm{\theta}}}\coloneqq\max\{\varphi_{5},\varphi_{6}\}+\frac{1}{2}|\bm{\xi}|^{2}
.
Furthermore, for each
d\in(0,\epsilon_{0}),
there exists a constant
C_{d}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast},\alpha,d)
such that

(4.36)

\|\hat{\phi}\|_{2,\alpha_{1}^{\ast},\Omega\setminus\mathcal{D}_{d}}\leq C_{d}\,.

Case (ii).

For each
\delta\in(0,\frac{\sigma_{2}}{2}),
whenever
\max\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\}\in[\theta^{\rm s}+\delta,\theta_{\ast}],

the boundary value problem (
4.23
) associated
with
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\overline{\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}}\cap\{\max{\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\}}\geq\theta^{\rm s}+\delta\}

has a unique solution
\hat{\phi}\in C^{2}(\Omega)\cap C^{1}(\overline{\Omega}\setminus({\Gamma^{5}_{\rm sonic}}\cup{\Gamma^{6}_{\rm sonic}}))\cap C^{0}(\overline{\Omega})
.
Moreover, there exists a constant
C^{\prime}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast},\alpha,\delta)
such that solution
\hat{\phi}

satisfies (
4.35
)
,
while there exists a constant
C^{\prime}_{d}>0
depending only
on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast},\alpha,\delta,d)
such that (
4.36
) holds.

Proof.

For Case (i), we fix

(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\overline{\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}}\cap\{\max\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\}\leq\theta^{\rm s}+\sigma_{2}\}

and fix a constant
h>0
.
Using
\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}
defined by (
4.7
), we rewrite the boundary value problem (
4.23
) associated with
(u,{\bm{\theta}})

as a new boundary value problem for the unknown function:

v(\bm{x})\coloneqq\hat{\phi}\circ(L_{h}\circ\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}})^{-1}(\bm{x})\qquad\,\text{for any $\bm{x}\in L_{h}\circ\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}(\Omega(u,{\bm{\theta}}))$}\,,

where
\bm{x}\coloneqq L_{h}(s,t^{\prime})=(\frac{s+1}{2}h,t^{\prime})
and
(s,t^{\prime})=\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}(\bm{\xi})
for any
\bm{\xi}\in\Omega(u,{\bm{\theta}})
.
We write

\Gamma_{0}\coloneqq L_{h}\circ\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}(\Gamma^{6}_{\rm sonic}),\,\,\,\Gamma_{1}\coloneqq L_{h}\circ\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}(\Gamma_{\rm shock}(u,{\bm{\theta}})),\,\,\,\Gamma_{2}\coloneqq L_{h}\circ\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}(\Gamma^{5}_{\rm sonic}),\,\,\,\Gamma_{3}\coloneqq L_{h}\circ\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}(\Gamma_{\rm sym})\,.

Moreover, we take

g_{\rm so}(\bm{x})\coloneqq(1-\varsigma_{h})\phi_{5}\circ(L_{h}\circ\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}})^{-1}(\bm{x})+\varsigma_{h}\phi_{6}\circ(L_{h}\circ\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}})^{-1}(\bm{x})\,,

where
\varsigma_{h}\coloneqq\varsigma_{1}(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{2}{h}x_{1})
, and the cut-off function

\varsigma_{1}(\cdot)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
is given by (
4.28
).
Using Lemmas
4.2
and
4.12
–
4.13
,
we can choose suitable constants
\epsilon^{(\rm w)}\in(0,\epsilon]
,
\delta_{1}^{(\rm w)}>0
,
and
N_{1}^{(\rm w)}\geq 1
such that the new boundary value problem for
v
satisfies the conditions in Proposition
B.1
when

\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon^{(\rm w)}]
,
\delta_{1}\in(0,\delta_{1}^{(\rm w)}]
,
and
N_{1}\geq N_{1}^{(\rm w)}
.
Therefore, Proposition
B.1
gives the existence and uniqueness
of a solution
