\hat{\phi}\in C(\overline{\Omega})\cap C^{2,\alpha_{1}}(\overline{\Omega}\setminus({\Gamma^{5}_{\rm sonic}}\cup{\Gamma^{6}_{\rm sonic}}))
of the boundary value problem (
4.23
)
satisfying (
4.35
)–(
4.36
).

For Case (ii), fix any
\delta\in(0,\frac{\sigma_{2}}{2})
.
In the subcase:
\min\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\}<\theta^{\rm s}+\delta\leq\max\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\}

(without loss of generality, we consider the case:
\theta_{1}<\theta^{\rm s}+\delta\leq\theta_{2}
),
one can follow the same analysis as above to check all the conditions for Proposition
B.2
, from which we obtain the existence of a unique
\hat{\phi}
satisfying
(
4.35
)–(
4.36
)
for any
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\overline{\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}}\cap\{\theta_{1}<\theta^{\rm s}+\delta\leq\theta_{2}\}
.
In the other subcase:
\min\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\}\in[\theta^{\rm s}+\delta,\theta_{*}]
,
we can apply Proposition
B.3
to prove the same results.

\square

For each
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\overline{\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}}
, the corresponding pseudo-subsonic
region
\Omega=\Omega(u,{\bm{\theta}})
depends continuously on
(u,{\bm{\theta}})
.
We rewrite (
4.23
) as a boundary value problem for

(4.37)

\hat{u}(s,t)\coloneqq(\hat{\phi}-\frac{1}{2}|\bm{\xi}|^{2}-\varphi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\ast})\circ\mathfrak{F}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(s,t)\qquad\,\text{in $\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}$}\,,

where
\varphi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\ast}
is given by Definition
4.4
,
and
\mathfrak{F}=\mathfrak{F}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}
is given by Definition
4.7
(
ii
).
We obtain

(4.38)

\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} \,&\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\mathcal{A}_{ij}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(D_{(s,t)}\hat{u},s,t)\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\hat{u}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\mathcal{A}_{i}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(D_{(s,t)}\hat{u},s,t)\partial_{i}\hat{u}=f^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}&&\quad\text{in $\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}$}\,,\\
&\mathscr{M}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(D_{(s,t)}\hat{u},\hat{u},s)=0&&\quad\text{on $\partial_{\rm sh}\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}$}\,,\\[2.84526pt]
&\hat{u}=0&&\quad\text{on $\partial_{\rm so}\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}$}\,,\\[-2.84526pt]
&\mathscr{B}^{\rm(w)}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(D_{(s,t)}\hat{u},s)\coloneqq\sum_{i=1}^{2}b^{\rm(w)}_{i}(s)\partial_{i}\hat{u}=0&&\quad\text{on $\partial_{\rm w}\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}$}\,,\end{aligned}\end{cases}

where
(\partial_{1},\partial_{2})\coloneqq(\partial_{s},\partial_{t})

and

\partial_{\rm sh}\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}\coloneqq(-1,1)\times\{1\}\,,\quad\partial_{\rm so}\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}\coloneqq\{-1,1\}\times(0,1)\,,\quad\partial_{\rm w}\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}\coloneqq(-1,1)\times\{0\}\,.

From Lemmas
4.8
and
4.12
–
4.14
,
we can obtain the following results; see
[
2
, Lemma 4.36]
.

Lemma 4.15
.

For every
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\overline{\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}},

let
\mathcal{A}_{ij}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})},\mathcal{A}_{i}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})},f^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})},\mathscr{M}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})},\mathscr{B}^{\rm(w)}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})},

and
b^{\rm(w)}_{i}
be given as in (
4.38
).
Then the following properties hold
:

(i)

\mathcal{A}_{ij}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})},\mathcal{A}_{i}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}\in C(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}),

f^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}\in C(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}),

\mathscr{M}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}\in C(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}\times\partial_{\rm sh}\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}),

and
\mathscr{B}^{\rm(w)}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}\in C(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}\times\partial_{\rm w}\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})
;

(ii)

If a sequence
\{(u_{k},{\bm{\theta}}_{k})\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}}

converges to
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\overline{\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}}

in

C^{2,\alpha}_{(*)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})\times[0,{\bm{\theta}}_{*}]^{2}
,
then the following sequences converge uniformly
:

•

(\mathcal{A}_{ij}^{(u_{k},{\bm{\theta}}_{k})},\mathcal{A}_{i}^{(u_{k},{\bm{\theta}}_{k})})\to(\mathcal{A}_{ij}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})},\mathcal{A}_{i}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})})

on compact subsets of
\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}
,

•

f^{(u_{k},{\bm{\theta}}_{k})}\to f^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}
on compact subsets of
\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}
,

•

\mathscr{M}_{(u_{k},{\bm{\theta}}_{k})}\to\mathscr{M}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}
on compact subsets
of
\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}\times\partial_{\rm sh}\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}
,

•

\mathscr{B}^{\rm(w)}_{(u_{k},{\bm{\theta}}_{k})}\to\mathscr{B}^{\rm(w)}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}
on compact subsets of
\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}\times\partial_{\rm w}\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}
.

Corollary 4.16
.

Let
\bar{\alpha}\in(0,1)
be from
Proposition
4.6
,

and let

\alpha_{1}^{*}\in(0,\bar{\alpha}],

\epsilon^{({\rm w})},\delta_{1}^{({\rm w})}
, and
N_{1}^{({\rm w})}

be from
Lemma
4.14
.
Let

\epsilon,\delta_{1},
and
N_{1}
from
Definition
4.10
satisfy

\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon^{({\rm w})}],

\delta_{1}\in(0,\delta_{1}^{({\rm w})}],
and
N_{1}\geq N_{1}^{({\rm w})}
.

(i)

For each
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\overline{\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}},

\hat{\phi}
solves
the boundary value problem (
4.23
) if and only if
\hat{u}
given
by (
4.37
) solves the boundary value problem (
4.38
).
Thus, (
4.38
) has a unique solution

\hat{u}\in C^{2}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})\cap C^{1}(\overline{\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}}\setminus\overline{\partial_{\rm so}\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}})\cap C(\overline{\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}})
.
Furthermore, there exists a constant
C\geq 1
depending on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{*},\alpha)
such that

|\hat{u}(s,t)|\leq C(1-|s|)\qquad\text{in $\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}$}\,.

For each
\hat{d}\in(0,\frac{1}{2}),
there exists
C_{\hat{d}}>0
depending
on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{*},\hat{d},\alpha)
such that

\|\hat{u}\|_{2,\alpha_{1}^{*},\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}\cap\{1-|s|>\hat{d}\}}\leq C_{\hat{d}}\,.

(ii)

Let
\{(u_{k},{\bm{\theta}}_{k})\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}}
converge
to
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\overline{\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}}

in
C^{1}(\overline{\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}})\times[0,\theta_{*}]^{2},
and let
\hat{u}_{k}
be the solution of
the boundary value problem (
4.38
) associated with
(u_{k},{\bm{\theta}}_{k})
.
Then there exists a unique solution

\hat{u}\in C^{2}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})\cap C^{1}(\overline{\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}}\setminus\overline{\partial_{\rm so}\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}})\cap C(\overline{\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}})

of the boundary value problem (
4.38
)
associated with
(u,{\bm{\theta}})
.
Moreover,
\hat{u}_{k}
converges to
\hat{u}
uniformly in
\overline{\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}}
and,
for any
\alpha^{\prime}\in[0,\alpha_{1}^{*})
,

•

\hat{u}_{k}\to\hat{u}
in
C^{1,\alpha^{\prime}}(K)
for any compact subset

K\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}}\setminus\overline{\partial_{\rm so}\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}}
,

•

\hat{u}_{k}\to\hat{u}
in
C^{2,\alpha^{\prime}}(K)
for any compact subset
K\subseteq\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}
.

(iii)

If
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\overline{\mathcal{K}},
then
(u,{\bm{\theta}})
satisfies

Definition
4.10
(
vii
)
with nonstrict inequality in (
4.25
).

Remark 4.2
.

For a constant
M>0,
define a set
\mathcal{K}^{E}_{M}
by

\mathcal{K}^{E}_{M}\coloneqq\left\{(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in C^{2,\alpha}_{(*)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})\times[0,{\bm{\theta}}_{*}]^{2}\,:\,\,\parbox{163.00027pt}{\rm$(u,{\bm{\theta}})$ satisfies
Definition~\ref{def:iteration-set}\eqref{def:iteration-set-2}--\eqref{def:iteration-set-6}\, \\
\hskip 5.69054pt and
$\|u\|^{(*)}_{2,\alpha,\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}}\leq M$ $\quad$}\right\}.

Let
\overline{\mathcal{K}^{E}_{M}}
be the closure of
\mathcal{K}^{E}_{M}
in
C^{2,\alpha}_{(*)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})\times[0,{\bm{\theta}}_{*}]^{2}
.
Then
Lemma
4.15
and
Corollary
4.16

still hold when
\overline{\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}}
is replaced by
\overline{\mathcal{K}^{E}_{M}}
for some
M>0
.

4.2.3.
Properties of the iteration set

From the well-posedness of the iteration boundary value problem (
4.23
),
we can obtain the following
a priori
estimates on the iteration set.
Let
\bar{\alpha}\in(0,\frac{1}{3}]
be the constant from Proposition
4.6
,
and let
(\epsilon^{\rm(w)},\delta_{1}^{\rm(w)},N_{1}^{\rm(w)})
be the constants from Lemma
4.14
.

Lemma 4.17

(
A priori
estimates)
.

There exist positive constants
\alpha^{\rm(ap)}\in(0,\frac{\bar{\alpha}}{2}),

\epsilon^{\rm(ap)}\in(0,\epsilon^{\rm(w)}],

\delta_{1}^{\rm(ap)}\in(0,\delta_{1}^{\rm(w)}],

N_{1}^{(\rm adm)}\geq N_{1}^{\rm(w)},

and
\delta_{3}^{(\rm ap)}
with
(\alpha^{\rm(ap)},\epsilon^{\rm(ap)},\delta_{1}^{\rm(ap)})

depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast}),

N_{1}^{\rm(adm)}
depending only
on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast},\delta_{1}),

and
\delta_{3}^{\rm(ap)}
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast},\delta_{1},\delta_{2},N_{1})

such that, whenever parameters
(\alpha,\epsilon,\delta_{1},\delta_{3},N_{1})

in
Definition
4.10
are chosen according to

•

(\alpha,\epsilon,\delta_{1})\in(0,\alpha^{\rm(ap)}]\times(0,\epsilon^{\rm(ap)}]\times(0,\delta_{1}^{\rm(ap)}]\,,

•

N_{1}\in[N_{1}^{\rm(adm)}(\delta_{1}),\infty)\,,

•

\delta_{3}\in(0,\delta_{3}^{\rm(ap)}(\delta_{1},\delta_{2},N_{1})]\,,

with parameter
\delta_{2}>0
to be determined later in
§
4.3
,
the following statements hold
:

(i)

For any admissible solution
\varphi
corresponding to
parameter
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\leq\theta_{\ast}\},

function
u=u^{(\varphi,{\bm{\theta}})}

given by (
4.14
) satisfies that
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\mathcal{K}
.

(ii)

There exists a constant
C>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast})
such that,
for each
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\overline{\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}},

the unique solution

\hat{\phi}\in C^{2}(\Omega)\cap C^{1}(\overline{\Omega}\setminus\Gamma_{\rm sonic})\cap C^{0}(\overline{\Omega})

of the boundary value problem (
4.23
) associated with
(u,{\bm{\theta}})
satisfies

(4.39)

\displaystyle\|\hat{u}\|^{(\ast)}_{2,2\alpha,\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}}\leq C\,,

for
\hat{u}:\overline{\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}}\to\mathbb{R}
given by (
4.24
)
,

whenever

(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\overline{\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}}
satisfies

(4.40)

\displaystyle\|u^{\sharp}-u\|_{C^{1}(\overline{\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}})}+|{\bm{\theta}}^{\sharp}-{\bm{\theta}}|\leq\delta^{\sharp}

for some
(u^{\sharp},{\bm{\theta}}^{\sharp})\in\overline{\mathcal{K}}

and some sufficiently small positive constant
\delta^{\sharp}

depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast},\delta_{2},\delta_{3},u^{\sharp},{\bm{\theta}}^{\sharp})
.

Proof.

We describe the proof in four steps,
which follow
[
2
, Corollary 4.40, Lemma 4.42, Lemma 4.44, Corollary 4.45]
closely.

1.

The proof of statement (
i
) above follows similarly to
[
2
, Corollary 4.40]
.
Indeed, let
u=u^{(\varphi,{\bm{\theta}})}
be given by (
4.14
) for any admissible
solution
\varphi
corresponding to
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap[0,\theta_{*}]^{2}
.
Then
(u,{\bm{\theta}})
automatically satisfies properties (
ii
)–(
v
)
of Definition
4.10
through the choices of constants

\{N_{i}\}_{i=2}^{5},\sigma_{2},\mu_{0},\mu_{1},\tilde{\mu},\rho^{\ast}(\gamma)
, and
C_{\rm ub}

in Definition
4.10
(
iii
)–(
v
).

Property (
i
) of Definition
4.10
follows from the choice of
N_{0}

in Definition
4.10
(
i
) and the choice of
N_{1}
below, in
which we use an argument
similar to
[
2
, Lemma 4.39]
.
Indeed, by Lemma
4.15
, Corollary
4.16
,
and Remark
4.2
, for any
\alpha\in(0,\frac{\bar{\alpha}}{2}]
, we have the following continuity property of admissible solutions:
For any sequence
\{{\bm{\theta}}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subseteq\Theta

with
{\bm{\theta}}_{k}\to\mathbf{0}
as
k\to\infty,

let
\varphi^{({\bm{\theta}}_{k})}
be any admissible solution corresponding to
{\bm{\theta}}_{k},
and let
u^{({\bm{\theta}}_{k})}

be given by (
4.14
) with
\varphi=\varphi^{({\bm{\theta}}_{k})}
and
{\bm{\theta}}={\bm{\theta}}_{k}
.
Then there exists a subsequence of
\{u^{({\bm{\theta}}_{k})}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}

converging in
C^{2,\alpha}_{(\ast)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})
to
u^{({\rm norm})}=0
.
Using this continuity property, for any
\delta_{1}\in(0,\delta_{1}^{\rm(w)}]
,
there exists
N_{1}^{\rm(adm)}\geq N_{1}^{\rm(w)}
depending on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast},\delta_{1})
such that

\|u-u^{\rm(norm)}\|^{(\ast)}_{2,\alpha,\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}}<\tfrac{\delta_{1}}{2}

whenever
\max\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\}\in(0,\tfrac{2\delta_{1}}{N_{1}^{\rm(adm)}}]
.

Therefore, we have shown that
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}
.
Finally, property (
vii
) of Definition
4.10

now follows from Corollary
4.16
because
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}
.
We conclude that
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\mathcal{K}
.

2.
It remains to prove statement (
ii
).
Fix any
(u^{\sharp},{\bm{\theta}}^{\sharp})\in\overline{\mathcal{K}}
.
We claim that there exist constants
\epsilon^{\rm(lb)}\in(0,\epsilon^{\rm(w)})

depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast})
,

\delta_{3}^{\rm(ap)}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast},\delta_{1},\delta_{2},N_{1})
,
and
\delta^{\sharp}
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast},\delta_{2},\delta_{3},u^{\sharp},{\bm{\theta}}^{\sharp})

such that

\hat{\phi}-(\varphi_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\ast}+\frac{1}{2}|\bm{\xi}|^{2})>0

in
\Omega
,
whenever
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\overline{\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}}
satisfies (
4.40
).
The proof of this claim follows from that for
[
2
, Lemma 4.42]
, which is split into two cases:
(i)
\max\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\}\in[\frac{2\delta_{1}}{N_{1}^{2}},\theta_{\ast}]
,
and (ii)
\max\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\}\in[0,\frac{2\delta_{1}}{N_{1}^{2}}]
.
In particular, we can choose
\delta_{3}^{\rm(ap)}=\frac{\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}{2N_{1}^{2}}

and
\epsilon^{\rm(lb)}=\bar{k}^{-1}\inf_{{\bm{\theta}}\in\overline{\Theta}}\{\hat{c}_{5},\hat{c}_{6}\}

for constant
\bar{k}>1
that has been fixed after
Definition
4.4
such that (
4.10
) holds.

3.

We obtain the
a priori
estimates for
\hat{\phi}
near
\Gamma_{\rm sonic}
.
Let coordinates
(x,y)=\mathcal{R}(\bm{\xi})
be given by (
4.1
).
For
j=5,6
, we have the following estimates (with estimates (b)–(c) below only
valid when
\theta_{\ast}\in(\theta^{\rm s},\theta^{\rm d})
):

(a)

Whenever
\theta_{j-4}\in[0,\theta^{\rm s})
, for each
\alpha^{\prime}\in(0,1)
,
there exist positive constants
\epsilon_{\rm p}\in(0,\epsilon_{0}]
and
C_{\alpha^{\prime}}

depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast},\alpha^{\prime})
with

\displaystyle\|(\hat{\phi}-\phi_{j})\circ\mathcal{R}^{-1}\|_{2,\alpha^{\prime},\mathcal{R}(\Omega\cap\mathcal{D}^{j}_{\epsilon_{\rm p}})}^{(2),(\rm par)}\leq C_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,;

(b)

There exists a constant
\delta_{\rm p}\in(0,\theta_{\ast}-\theta^{\rm s})

depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast})
such that,
whenever
\theta_{j-4}\in[\theta^{\rm s},\theta^{\rm s}+\delta_{\rm p}]
,
for each
\alpha^{\prime}\in(0,1)
, there exist positive constants
\epsilon_{\rm p}\in(0,\epsilon_{0}]

depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast})

and
C_{\alpha^{\prime}}
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast},\alpha^{\prime})
so that

\displaystyle\|\hat{\phi}-\phi_{j}\|_{C^{2,\alpha^{\prime}}(\Omega\cap\mathcal{D}^{j}_{\epsilon_{\rm p}})}\leq C_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,,\qquad\,\,D^{m}(\hat{\phi}-\phi_{j})(P_{0}^{j-4})=0\quad\text{for $m=0,1,2$}\,;

(c)

For
\delta_{\rm p}>0
as above, there exist constants
\hat{\alpha}\in(0,\frac{1}{3}]
and
C>0

depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast})
such that,
whenever
\theta_{j-4}\in[\theta^{\rm s}+\frac{1}{2}\delta_{\rm p},\theta_{\ast}]
,

\displaystyle\|\hat{\phi}-\phi_{j}\|_{2,\hat{\alpha},\Omega\cap\mathcal{D}^{j}_{\epsilon_{0}}}^{(-1-\hat{\alpha}),\{P_{0}^{j-4}\}}\leq C\,,\qquad\,\,D^{m}(\hat{\phi}-\phi_{j})(P_{0}^{j-4})=0\quad\text{for $m=0,1$}\,.

Using Step
2
, the proof of these
a priori
estimates follows from that for
[
2
, Lemma 4.44]
,
whereby it is necessary to further reduce constants
\epsilon
and
\delta_{1}

depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{\ast})
,
and we define
\alpha^{\rm(ap)}\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}\min\{\alpha_{1}^{*},\hat{\alpha}\}
with
\alpha_{1}^{*}
given in Lemma
4.14
.
In particular, the estimate in (i) is similar to that
for Propositions
3.12
–
3.13
,
with the free boundary replaced by a fixed boundary.
The estimate in (ii) is similar to that for Proposition
3.14
,
with the free boundary replaced again by a fixed boundary.
Finally, the estimate in (iii) is similar to that for Proposition
3.16
.

4.

Following the method from
[
2
, Proposition 4.12]
, we are able to combine the estimates
from Step
3
with the interior estimates from Lemma
4.14

to obtain (
4.39
).

\square

Let parameters
(\alpha,\epsilon,\delta_{1},\delta_{3},N_{1})
in Definition
4.10
be chosen as in Lemma
4.17
.
It is simple to verify that
\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}\subseteq C^{2,\alpha}_{(\ast)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})\times[0,\theta_{\ast}]^{2}

is relatively open.
Indeed, the argument is similar to that for
[
10
, Lemmas 12.8.1 and 17.5.1]

and
[
2
, Lemma 4.41]
.
Furthermore, following the proofs of
[
10
, Propositions 12.8.2 and 17.5.3]

and
[
2
, Lemma 4.46]
,
and applying the
a priori
estimates from Lemma
4.17
,
we conclude that the iteration set

\mathcal{K}\subseteq C^{2,\alpha}_{(\ast)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})\times[0,\theta_{\ast}]^{2}
is relatively open.

Proposition 4.18

(Openness of the iteration set)
.

For the choice of parameters
(\alpha,\epsilon,\delta_{1},\delta_{3},N_{1})
given in
Lemma
4.17
,

the iteration set
\mathcal{K}
defined by
Definition
4.10
is a relatively open subset
of
C^{2,\alpha}_{(\ast)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})\times[0,\theta_{\ast}]^{2}
.

4.3.
Definition of the iteration map

Fix
\theta_{*}\in(0,\theta^{\rm d})
.
For the iteration set
\mathcal{K}
given by Definition
4.10
,
let parameters
(\alpha,\epsilon,\delta_{1},\delta_{3},N_{1})
be chosen as in Lemma
4.17
.
Define

\mathcal{K}({\bm{\theta}})\coloneqq\big\{u\in C^{2,\alpha}_{(*)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})\,:\,(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\mathcal{K}\big\}\qquad\text{for each}\;{\bm{\theta}}\in[0,\theta_{*}]^{2}\,,

and similarly for
\overline{\mathcal{K}}({\bm{\theta}})
.
We now define an iteration map
\mathcal{I}:\overline{\mathcal{K}}\rightarrow C^{2,\alpha}_{(*)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})

satisfying the following properties:

(a)

For each
{\bm{\theta}}\in[0,\theta_{*}]^{2}
, there exists
u\in\mathcal{K}({\bm{\theta}})

satisfying
\mathcal{I}(u,{\bm{\theta}})=u
;

(b)

If
u
satisfies
\mathcal{I}(u,{\bm{\theta}})=u
, then
\varphi^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}
defined
in Definition
4.7
(
iii
) is an admissible solution
corresponding to
{\bm{\theta}}
.

Let
\hat{\phi}=\hat{\varphi}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}+\frac{1}{2}|\bm{\xi}|^{2}\in C^{2}(\Omega)\cap C^{1}(\overline{\Omega})

solve the iteration boundary value problem (
4.23
) associated with
(u,{\bm{\theta}})
in Definition
4.10
.
Accordingly, from the definition of
\hat{u}:\overline{\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}}\to\mathbb{R}
given by (
4.24
),
we have

\hat{\varphi}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\bm{\xi})=\hat{u}\circ(\mathfrak{F}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})})^{-1}(\bm{\xi})+\varphi^{*}_{{\bm{\theta}}}(\bm{\xi})\,,

where
\varphi^{*}_{{\bm{\theta}}}(\bm{\xi})
is given by Definition
4.4
.
We also define functions
(w,w_{2},\hat{w})
by

(4.41)

(w,w_{2},\hat{w})(s,t^{\prime})\coloneqq(\varphi-\varphi^{*}_{\bm{\theta}},\varphi_{2}-\varphi^{*}_{\bm{\theta}},\hat{\varphi}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}-\varphi^{*}_{\bm{\theta}})\circ(\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}})^{-1}(s,t^{\prime})\,.

From (
4.12
) and the implicit function theorem,
for any
{\bm{\theta}}\in\overline{\Theta}
, there exists a unique function

\mathfrak{g}_{2}:[-1,1]\to\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}}
satisfying
w_{2}(s,\mathfrak{g}_{2}(s))=0
on
[-1,1]
.
Then, for
Q^{{\bm{\theta}}}
defined by Definition
4.1
,

\left\{(s,t^{\prime})\,:\,-1<s<1,\,t^{\prime}=\mathfrak{g}_{2}(s)\right\}\subseteq\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}}(Q^{{\bm{\theta}}})\,.

It follows from (
4.11
), Lemma
4.2
,
and Definition
4.4
that
\|\mathfrak{g}_{2}\|_{C^{3}([-1,1])}\leq C

for some constant
C>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
.

For any
g\in C^{0,1}([-1,1])
satisfying
g(s)>0
for all
s\in(-1,1)
, introduce the following sets:

R_{\infty}\coloneqq(-1,1)\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
and

(4.42)

\displaystyle R_{g}\coloneqq\big\{(s,t^{\prime})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\,:\,-1<s<1,\,0<t^{\prime}<g(s)\big\}\,,

\displaystyle\Sigma_{g}\coloneqq\big\{(s,t^{\prime})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\,:\,-1<s<1,\,t^{\prime}=g(s)\big\}\,.

For each
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\overline{\mathcal{K}}
,

\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}=\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}:[-1,1]\to\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}}
given by Definition
4.7

is a Lipschitz function on
[-1,1]

and is positive on
(-1,1)
.
Then we can define sets
R_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}
and
\Sigma_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}
with
g=\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}
in (
4.42
).
Note that
w
and
\hat{w}
are defined on
R_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}
, while
w_{2}
is defined on
R_{\infty}
.

The
regularized distance
function

\delta_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}\in C^{\infty}(\overline{R_{\infty}}\setminus\overline{R_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}})
is given by Lemma
B.4
.
Let
C_{\rm rd}>0
be the constant from Lemma
B.4
,
which depends only on
{\rm Lip}[\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}]
.
We define

(4.43)

\delta^{*}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}(s,t^{\prime})\coloneqq C_{\rm rd}^{-1}\delta_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}(s,t^{\prime})\qquad\text{for any $(s,t^{\prime})\in\overline{R_{\infty}}\setminus\overline{R_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}}$}\,.

Then, for all
(s,t^{\prime})\in R_{(1+\kappa)\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}\setminus\overline{R_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}}
and all
\lambda\in[1,2]
,

(4.44)

(s,t^{\prime}-\lambda\delta^{*}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}(s,t^{\prime}))\in\{s\}\times\big[\,\frac{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}(s)}{3},\,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}(s)-(t^{\prime}-\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}(s))\big]\Subset R_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}\,,

where constant
\kappa\in(0,\frac{1}{3}]
depends only on
{\rm Lip}[\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}]
.

Let
\left\{(u_{k},{\bm{\theta}}_{k})\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}}

converge to
(u,{\bm{\theta}})
in
C^{2,\alpha}_{(*)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})\times[0,\theta_{*}]^{2}
.
Note that

\|\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}\|^{(-1-\alpha),\{-1,1\}}_{2,\alpha,(-1,1)}\leq CN_{0}\,,

where
N_{0}>0
is from Definition
4.10
(
i
),
and
C>0
depends only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\alpha)
. Moreover, for
\mathfrak{g}_{5}
and
\mathfrak{g}_{6}
defined in
Proposition
4.3
(
ii
),

\frac{{\rm d}^{m}}{{\rm d}s^{m}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}-\mathfrak{g}_{5})(1)=\frac{{\rm d}^{m}}{{\rm d}s^{m}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}-\mathfrak{g}_{6})(-1)=0\qquad\text{for $m=0,1$}\,.

Using Lemma
4.8
(
iii
)
and (
v
)–(
vi
),
we see that
\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u_{k},{\bm{\theta}}_{k})}
converges to
\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}
in
C^{0,1}([-1,1])
and

\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}\|\delta_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u_{k},{\bm{\theta}}_{k})}}-\delta_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}}\|_{C^{m}(K)}=0

for any compact set
K\subseteq\overline{R_{\infty}}\setminus\overline{R_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
,
which follows by Lemma
B.4
.

From
[
10
, Lemma 13.9.2]
, there exists a function
\Psi\in C^{\infty}_{\rm c}(\mathbb{R})

satisfying

\supp\Psi\subseteq[1,2]\,,\qquad\,\int^{\infty}_{-\infty}\lambda^{m}\Psi(\lambda)\,{\rm d}\lambda=1-\sgn(m)\quad\text{for $m=0,1,2$}\,.

Definition 4.19

(Extension operator)
.

For each
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\overline{\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}},

write
\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}=\mathfrak{g}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}_{\rm sh},
and let
\delta^{*}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}
be defined by (
4.43
) satisfying (
4.44
)
with constants
C_{\rm rd}>0
and
\kappa\in(0,\frac{1}{3}]
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{*})
.
For any
v\in C^{0}(\overline{R_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}})\cap C^{2}(R_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}\cup\Sigma_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}),

define the extension of
v(s,t^{\prime})
by

\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}(v)(s,t^{\prime})\coloneqq\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} \,&v(s,t^{\prime})\quad&&\text{for $(s,t^{\prime})\in\overline{R_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}}$}\,,\\
&\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}v(s,t^{\prime}-\lambda\delta^{*}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}(s,t^{\prime}))\Psi(\lambda)\,{\rm d}\lambda\quad&&\text{for $(s,t^{\prime})\in R_{(1+\kappa)\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}\setminus\overline{R_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}}$}\,.\end{aligned}\end{cases}

For simplicity, we use the following notation:
For any
a,b\in[-1,1]
, denote

R^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}[a,b]\coloneqq\big\{(s,t^{\prime})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\,:\,a<s<b\,,0<t^{\prime}<\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(s)\big\}\,.

We often use the notation
R_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}
to represent
R^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}
.

Combining the above with Lemma
4.8
(
iii
)
and the property that the uniform bound of
{\rm Lip}[\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}]
depends only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{*})
,
we obtain the following proposition, for which the details of the proof can be found
in
[
10
, Lemma 13.9.6, and Theorems 13.9.5 and 13.9.8]
for each case, respectively.

Proposition 4.20

(Properties of the extension operator
\mathcal{E}
)
.

Fix
\alpha\in(0,1)
.
For each
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\overline{\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}},

let the extension operator

\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}:C^{2}(R_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}\cup\Sigma_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}})\to C^{2}(R_{(1+\kappa)\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}})

be given by
Definition
4.19
.
We introduce the notation for the following three different cases
:

(a)

Fix any
(b_{1},b_{2})
with
-1<b_{1}<b_{2}<1,

and
C=C_{\rm int}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{*},\alpha)
.
For any
\alpha^{\prime}\in(0,\alpha),
denote the function spaces
:

X\coloneqq C^{2,\alpha}({R^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}[b_{1},b_{2}]}),\quad Y\coloneqq C^{2,\alpha}(R^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}_{(1+\kappa)\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}[b_{1},b_{2}]),\quad Y^{-}\coloneqq C^{2,\alpha^{\prime}}(R^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}_{(1+\frac{\kappa}{2})\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}[b_{1},b_{2}])\,;

(b)

Fix
\sigma>0
and
\epsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{4}]
.
Fix
(b_{1},b_{2})=(-1,-1+\epsilon)
or

(1-\epsilon,1),

and
C=C_{\rm par}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{*},\alpha,\sigma)
.
For any
\alpha^{\prime}\in(0,\alpha)
and
\sigma^{\prime}\in(0,\sigma),
denote the function spaces
:

\displaystyle X\coloneqq C_{(\sigma),({\rm par})}^{2,\alpha}({R^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}[b_{1},b_{2}]})\,,\qquad Y\coloneqq C_{(\sigma),({\rm par})}^{2,\alpha}({R^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}_{(1+\kappa)\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}[b_{1},b_{2}]})\,,

\displaystyle Y^{-}\coloneqq C_{(\sigma^{\prime}),({\rm par})}^{2,\alpha^{\prime}}({R^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}_{(1+\frac{\kappa}{2})\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}[b_{1},b_{2}]})\,;

(c)

Fix
(b_{1},b_{2})
with either
(b_{1},b_{2})=(b^{(1)}_{1},b^{(1)}_{2})=(\frac{1}{2},1)

or
(b_{1},b_{2})=(b^{(2)}_{1},b^{(2)}_{2})=(-1,-\frac{1}{2}),
and
C=C_{\rm sub}>0

depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{*},\alpha)
.
For any
\alpha^{\prime}\in(0,\alpha),
denote the function spaces
:

\displaystyle X\coloneqq C^{2,\alpha}_{(-1-\alpha),\{s=(-1)^{i-1}\}}({R^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}[b^{(i)}_{1},b^{(i)}_{2}]})\,,\quad Y\coloneqq C^{2,\alpha}_{(-1-\alpha),\{s=(-1)^{i-1}\}}({R^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}_{(1+\kappa)\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}[b^{(i)}_{1},b^{(i)}_{2}]})\,,\hskip-16.00008pt

\displaystyle Y^{-}\coloneqq C^{2,\alpha^{\prime}}_{(-1-\alpha^{\prime}),\{s=(-1)^{i-1}\}}({R^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}_{(1+\frac{\kappa}{2})\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}[b^{(i)}_{1},b^{(i)}_{2}]})\,.

Then the extension operator
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}
satisfies the following
:

(i)

There exists
C>0
for each case above such that

\|\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}(v)\|_{Y}\leq C\|v\|_{X}
.
Furthermore, for
Case

{\rm(c)},
if
(v,Dv)=(0,\mathbf{0})

on
\overline{R^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}}\cap\{x_{1}=(-1)^{i-1}\}
for
i=1,2,
then

(\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}(v),D\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}(v))=(0,\mathbf{0})\qquad\text{on $\overline{R^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}_{(1+\kappa)\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}}\cap\{x_{1}=(-1)^{i-1}\}$}\,.

(ii)

For each case,
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}:X\rightarrow Y
is linear and continuous.

(iii)

Suppose that
\{(u_{k},{\bm{\theta}}_{k})\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{K}^{\rm ext}}

converges to
(u,{\bm{\theta}})
in
C^{2,\tilde{\alpha}}_{(*)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})\times[0,\theta_{*}]^{2}

for some
\tilde{\alpha}\in(0,1)
.
Write
X_{k}
for the function space
X
with
(u,{\bm{\theta}})
replaced by
(u_{k},{\bm{\theta}}_{k})
.
If sequence
\{v_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subseteq X_{k}
satisfies

\,\,\|v_{k}\|_{X_{k}}\leq M
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}

for some constant
M>0
and
\{v_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
converges uniformly
to
v
on any compact set
K\Subset R^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}
for some
v\in X,
then

\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u_{k},{\bm{\theta}}_{k})}}(v_{k})\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}}(v)\qquad\text{in $Y^{-}$}\,,

where
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u_{k},{\bm{\theta}}_{k})}}(v_{k})
is well defined on
\overline{R^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}_{(1+\frac{\kappa}{2})\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}[b_{1},b_{2}]}
for large enough
k
.

The proof of the following result is similar to
[
2
, Lemma 5.5]
.

Lemma 4.21
.

Let parameters
(\alpha,\epsilon,\delta_{1},\delta_{3},N_{1})
in
Definition
4.10

be chosen as in
Lemma
4.17
,

with
\delta_{2}>0
to be specified later.
Then there exists a constant
\delta_{3}^{({\rm imp})}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{*},\delta_{2})

such that, if
\delta_{3}\in(0,\delta^{({\rm imp})}_{3}),

then, for each
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\overline{\mathcal{K}},

there is a unique function
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\rm sh}:[-1,1]\to\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}}
such that

(4.45)

\big(w_{2}-\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}}(\hat{w})\big)(s,\hat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\rm sh}(s))=0\qquad\,\text{for all $s\in[-1,1]$}\,.

Furthermore, there exists a constant
C>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{*})
such that

\hat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\rm sh}
satisfies

(4.46)

\displaystyle\left\{\,\begin{aligned} &\|\hat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\rm sh}-\mathfrak{g}_{2}\|_{2,2\alpha,(-1,1)}^{(-1-2\alpha),\{-1,1\}}\leq C\,,\qquad\|\hat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\rm sh}-\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}\|_{2,\frac{\alpha}{2},(-1,1)}\leq C\delta_{3}\,,\\
&\frac{{\rm d}^{m}}{{\rm d}s^{m}}(\hat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\rm sh}-\mathfrak{g}_{2})(\pm 1)=\frac{{\rm d}^{m}}{{\rm d}s^{m}}(\hat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\rm sh}-\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})})(\pm 1)=0\qquad\,\text{for $m=0,1$}\,.\end{aligned}\right.

Definition 4.22

(Iteration map)
.

Let parameters
(\alpha,\epsilon,\delta_{1},\delta_{3},N_{1})
in
Definition
4.10

be chosen as in
Lemma
4.17
,

with
\delta_{2}>0
to be specified later.
Furthermore, let
\delta_{3}\in(0,\delta_{3}^{({\rm imp})})
for constant
\delta_{3}^{({\rm imp})}>0
from
Lemma
4.21
.
For each
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\overline{\mathcal{K}},
let
\tilde{u}:\overline{\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}}\to\mathbb{R}
be given by

\tilde{u}\coloneqq\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}}(\hat{w})\circ(G_{2,\hat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\rm sh}})^{-1}\,,

where
G_{2,\hat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\rm sh}}
is given by (
4.13
) with
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\rm sh}:[-1,1]\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
from (
4.45
).
Then define the iteration map
\mathcal{I}:\overline{\mathcal{K}}\to C^{2,\alpha}_{(*)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})
by

\mathcal{I}(u,{\bm{\theta}})=\tilde{u}\,.

Note that map
\mathcal{I}:\overline{\mathcal{K}}\to C^{2,\alpha}_{(*)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})
defined as above
is reasonable, since

(4.47)

\|\mathcal{I}(u,{\bm{\theta}})\|^{(*)}_{2,2\alpha,\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}}\leq C\qquad\text{for all $(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\mathcal{\mathcal{K}}$}

for
C>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{*})
,
which follows from Propositions
4.18
and
4.20
,
and Lemma
4.21
.

4.4.
Proof of Theorem 2.1: Existence of admissible solutions

Fix
\theta_{*}\in(0,\theta^{\rm d})
. For the iteration map
\mathcal{I}
defined in
Definition
4.22
, we now prove that,
for any fixed point
u\in\overline{\mathcal{K}}({\bm{\theta}})
of
\mathcal{I}(\cdot,{\bm{\theta}})
for
some
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap[0,\theta_{*}]^{2}
,
then
\varphi=\varphi^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}
defined in Definition
4.7
(
iii
)
is an admissible solution corresponding to
{\bm{\theta}}
in the sense of Definition
2.11
.

Proposition 4.23

(Fixed points of map
\mathcal{I}(\cdot,{\bm{\theta}})
)
.

Let parameters
(\alpha,\epsilon,\delta_{1},\delta_{3},N_{1})
in
Definition
4.10

be chosen as in
Definition
4.22
.
Then parameters
(\epsilon,\delta_{1})
can be chosen even smaller, depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{*}),

such that, for each
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap[0,\theta_{*}]^{2},

u\in\mathcal{K}({\bm{\theta}})
is a fixed point of

\mathcal{I}(\cdot,{\bm{\theta}}):\overline{\mathcal{K}}({\bm{\theta}})\to C^{2,\alpha}_{(*)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})

if and only if
\varphi=\varphi^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}
defined in

Definition
4.7
(
iii
)
is an admissible solution
corresponding to
{\bm{\theta}}
in the sense of
Definition
2.11

after extending
\varphi
to
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
via (
2.41
).

Proof.

Since the proof is similar to that for
[
2
, Proposition 5.8]
,
we omit the details of the proof here and sketch only the main ideas in the following four steps.

1.

For
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap[0,\theta_{*}]^{2}
, let
\varphi
be an admissible solution corresponding to
{\bm{\theta}}
,
and let
u\coloneqq u^{(\varphi,{\bm{\theta}})}
be given by (
4.14
),
which satisfies
u\in\mathcal{K}({\bm{\theta}})
by Lemma
4.17
(
i
).
It follows directly from Definition
4.7
(
i
)
and the boundary condition on
\Gamma_{\rm shock}
that
\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}=\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}
.
It is clear that
\hat{\phi}\coloneqq\phi=\varphi+\frac{1}{2}|\bm{\xi}|^{2}
solves the iteration boundary value problem (
4.23
),
which indicates that
\hat{w}=w
in (
4.41
).
Then
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\rm sh}=\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}
by solving (
4.45
). We conclude that

\mathcal{I}(u,{\bm{\theta}})=\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}}(\hat{w})\circ(G_{2,\hat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\rm sh}})^{-1}=\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}({w})\circ(G_{2,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}})^{-1}=u\,,

so
u\in\mathcal{K}({\bm{\theta}})
is a fixed point of
\mathcal{I}(\cdot,{\bm{\theta}})
.

2.

On the other hand, for any fixed point
u
of
map
\mathcal{I}(\cdot,{\bm{\theta}}):\overline{\mathcal{K}}({\bm{\theta}})\to C^{2,\alpha}_{(*)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})
, let
\varphi^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}

be given by Definition
4.7
(
iii
).
We now show that
\varphi^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}

is an admissible solution in the sense of Definition
2.11
corresponding to
{\bm{\theta}}
.
Using Definition
4.7
(
i
), (
4.45
),
and Definition
4.22
, we have

w_{2}(s,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})})=u(s,1)=\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}}(\hat{w})(s,\hat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\rm sh})=w_{2}(s,\hat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\rm sh})\,,

from which we obtain that
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\rm sh}=\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}
,
\hat{u}=u
,
and
\hat{\varphi}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}=\varphi^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}
.

Define
v\coloneqq\hat{\varphi}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}-\varphi_{2}
.
Then
v
solves the following strictly elliptic equation:

\mathcal{L}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(v)=\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}A_{ij}(D\hat{\phi}(\bm{\xi}),\bm{\xi})\partial_{i}\partial_{j}v=0\,.

From the boundary conditions of the iteration boundary value problem (
4.23
) and
\hat{u}=u
above,
we see that
v\leq 0
on
\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cup\Gamma_{\rm sonic}

and
\partial_{\eta}v=-v_{2}>0
on
\Gamma_{\rm sym}
.
From the maximum principle and
Hopf’s lemma, we obtain that
\hat{\varphi}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}\leq\varphi_{2}
on
\overline{\Omega}
.

When

\max\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\}\in[\frac{2\delta_{1}}{N_{1}^{2}},\theta_{*}]
, we see that

\hat{\varphi}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}\geq\max\{\varphi_{5},\varphi_{6}\}
in
\Omega
,
which follows directly from (
4.21
) in Definition
4.10

and Step
1
of Lemma
4.17
.

When

\max\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\}\in(0,\frac{2\delta_{1}}{N_{1}^{2}}]
, we can show the same result
by applying the maximum principle as in the proof of
[
2
, Lemma 4.42]
.
Then inequality (
2.42
) in Definition
2.11
(
iv
) is proved.

Similarly, for inequality (
2.43
) in
Definition
2.11
(
iv
), we also split into two cases.
Denote
w\coloneqq\partial_{\bm{e}_{S_{25}}}(\varphi_{2}-{\varphi}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})})
.
We introduce
(X_{1},X_{2})
–coordinates by
\bm{\xi}\eqqcolon X_{1}\bm{e}_{S_{25}}+X_{2}\bm{e}^{\perp}_{S_{25}}
.
From the equation for
v
, one can derive the following strictly elliptic equation for
w=-\partial_{X_{1}}v
:

\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(w)\coloneqq\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\hat{A}_{ij}\partial_{X_{i}X_{j}}w+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\hat{A}_{i}\partial_{X_{i}}w=0\qquad\,\,\text{in $\Omega$}\,.

When
\max\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\}\in[\frac{2\delta_{1}}{N_{1}^{2}},\theta_{*}]
,
it follows from (
4.21
) in Definition
4.10
that

w<0
in
\overline{\Omega}\setminus\mathcal{D}^{5}_{\epsilon/10}
.
In domain
\Omega\cap\mathcal{D}^{5}_{\epsilon}
, one can check

(4.48)

\begin{cases}\,w=0\qquad&\quad\text{on $\Gamma^{5}_{\rm sonic}$}\,,\\
\,\bm{b}_{\rm w}\cdot Dw=0\qquad&\quad\text{with $\bm{b}_{\rm w}\cdot\bm{\nu}>0$ on $\Gamma_{\rm sym}$}\,,\\
\,\bm{b}_{\rm sh}\cdot Dw=0\qquad&\quad\text{with $\bm{b}_{\rm sh}\cdot\bm{\nu}>0$ on $\Gamma_{\rm shock}$}\,,\end{cases}

where
\bm{\nu}
is the unit normal vector on
\Gamma_{\rm sym}\cup\Gamma_{\rm shock}
pointing to
\Omega
.
Note that we have used
[
10
, Lemma 13.4.5]
to derive the boundary condition
on
\Gamma_{\rm shock}
.
By the maximum principle and Hopf’s lemma, we obtain that
w\leq 0
in
{\Omega}\cap\mathcal{D}^{5}_{\epsilon}

when
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{\rm sh}]
, for some sufficiently small
\epsilon_{\rm sh}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{*})
.

When
\max\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\}\in(0,\frac{2\delta_{1}}{N_{1}^{2}}]
,
using property (
i
) of Definition
4.10
,
one can follow the same procedure as the first case to derive that

w\leq 0
in
\Omega
when
\delta_{1}\in(0,\delta_{\rm sh}]
, for some
\delta_{\rm sh}>0

depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{*})
.
Therefore,
\hat{\varphi}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}
satisfies (
2.43
)
when
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{\rm sh}]
and
\delta_{1}\in(0,\delta_{\rm sh}]
,
since the argument above also works for
\partial_{\bm{e}_{S_{26}}}(\varphi_{2}-{\varphi}^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})})
.

3.
Now we check that
\mathcal{N}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\phi)=0

in Definition
4.10
(
vii
)
coincides with (
3.1
) in
\Omega
.
From Lemma
4.12
(
iii
),
this property holds in
\Omega\setminus\mathcal{D}_{\epsilon/10}
,
so it remains to show that this property holds in
\Omega\cap\mathcal{D}_{\epsilon/2}
.
We only consider domain
\Omega\cap\mathcal{D}^{5}_{\epsilon/2}
,
since the proof is the same for domain
\Omega\cap\mathcal{D}^{6}_{\epsilon/2}

due to the symmetry between
\theta_{1}
and
\theta_{2}
in the problem.

For any
\bm{\xi}\in\Omega\cap\mathcal{D}_{\epsilon}
, let coordinates
(x,y)=\mathcal{R}(\bm{\xi})
be given by (
4.1
).
Using the monotone property (
4.20
), we choose a constant

\epsilon^{\prime}_{\rm sh}\in(0,\epsilon_{\rm sh}]
sufficiently small, depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{*})
,
such that

\theta_{1}\leq\theta^{\rm s}+\min\{\frac{\sigma_{3}}{2},\delta_{\rm p}\}
whenever
x_{P_{0}^{1}}<\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}_{\rm sh}}{10}
,
for
\delta_{\rm p}>0
the constant from Step
3
(
b
)
in the proof of Lemma
4.17
.

Choose
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon^{\prime}_{\rm sh}]
, and suppose
x_{P_{0}^{1}}<\frac{\epsilon}{10}
.
Denote
\tilde{w}\coloneqq Ax-\partial_{x}\psi(x,y)
for
A\coloneqq\frac{1}{1+\gamma}(2-\frac{\mu_{0}}{5})

and
\psi=(\varphi^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}-\varphi_{5})\circ\mathcal{R}^{-1}
.
It is clear that
\tilde{w}=0
on
\mathcal{R}(\Gamma_{\rm sonic}^{5})
,
and
\tilde{w}_{y}=0
on
\mathcal{R}(\Gamma_{\rm sym}\cap\partial\mathcal{D}_{\epsilon/2}^{5})
.
From Lemma
4.13
(
i
)
and (
iii
)–(
iv
),
the boundary condition:
\mathcal{M}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(D\hat{\phi},\hat{\phi},\bm{\xi})=0

in (
4.23
) can be written as

(b_{1},b_{2},b_{0})\cdot(D\hat{\phi},\hat{\phi})=0\qquad\text{on $\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap\mathcal{D}_{\epsilon}^{5}$}\,,

for some
b_{0},b_{1},b_{2}\in[-\frac{1}{\delta_{\rm bc}},-\delta_{\rm bc}]
.
Using the
a priori
estimates (
4.39
) in the case:

\theta_{1}\leq\theta^{\rm s}+\delta_{\rm p}
,
we see that
|\psi_{x}|\leq\frac{1}{\delta^{2}_{\rm bc}}(|\psi_{y}|+|\psi|)\leq Cx^{\frac{3}{2}}

for any
(x,y)\in\mathcal{R}(\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap\mathcal{D}_{\epsilon}^{5})
.
Reducing
\epsilon^{\prime}_{\rm sh}>0
further, depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2})
, we obtain that
\tilde{w}>0
on

\mathcal{R}(\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap\mathcal{D}_{\epsilon}^{5})
for any
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon^{\prime}_{\rm sh}]
.

By Lemma
4.12
(
ii
) and the fact that
\hat{\phi}=\phi
,
we have

\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}A_{ij}(D\hat{\phi},\bm{\xi})\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\hat{\phi}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}A^{(3)}_{ij}(D\hat{\phi},\bm{\xi})\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\hat{\phi}=c_{\bm{\theta}}\mathcal{N}^{\rm polar}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\hat{\phi})\,,

from where we can derive the following equation for
\tilde{w}
:

a_{11}\tilde{w}_{xx}+2a_{12}\tilde{w}_{xy}+a_{22}\tilde{w}_{yy}+a_{1}\tilde{w}_{x}+a_{2}\tilde{w}_{y}=-A((\gamma+1)A-1)+E(x,y)\qquad\text{in $\mathcal{R}(\Omega\cap\mathcal{D}_{\epsilon/2}^{5})$}\,.

From expression (
3.26
) and the
a priori
estimates (
4.39
)
in Lemma
4.17
, reducing
\epsilon^{\prime}_{\rm sh}>0
further if necessary,
we obtain directly that
E(x,y)<A((\gamma+1)A-1)

in
\mathcal{R}(\Omega\cap\mathcal{D}_{\epsilon/2}^{5})
for any
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon^{\prime}_{\rm sh}]
.
Therefore, the property:
\tilde{w}=Ax-\partial_{x}\psi(x,y)\geq 0
holds
in
\mathcal{R}(\Omega\cap\mathcal{D}^{5}_{\epsilon/2})
, which follows from
Lemma
4.12
(
i
), the maximum principle, and Hopf’s lemma.

Now we show the inequality:
\partial_{x}\psi(x,y)\geq-Ax
.
From Step
2
, we have

\big(\partial_{e_{S_{25}}}(\varphi_{2}-\varphi)\big)\circ\mathcal{R}^{-1}=\psi_{x}\cos(\theta_{25}-y)-\frac{\sin(\theta_{25}-y)}{c_{5}-x}\psi_{y}\leq 0\qquad\text{in $\mathcal{R}(\Omega\cap\mathcal{D}^{5}_{\epsilon/2})$}\,.

This implies that

\psi_{x}\geq-\frac{\tan(\frac{\pi}{2}-\tilde{\omega}_{0})}{c_{5}-x}\psi_{y}\geq-Cx^{\frac{3}{2}}\qquad\text{in $\mathcal{R}(\Omega\cap\mathcal{D}^{5}_{\epsilon/2})$}\,,

by using property (
4.3
) and
the
a priori
estimates (
4.39
) in Lemma
4.17
.
Then
\partial_{x}\psi(x,y)\geq-Ax
after further reducing
\epsilon^{\prime}_{\rm sh}>0
.
Thus,
|\psi_{x}|\leq Ax
is proved, which indicates that

\mathcal{N}_{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}(\phi)=0
in Definition
4.10
(
vii
)
coincides with (
3.1
) in
\Omega\cap\mathcal{D}^{5}_{\epsilon/2}
.
Moreover, if
x_{P_{0}^{1}}\geq\frac{\epsilon}{10}
,
the same conclusion holds in

\Omega\cap\mathcal{D}^{5}_{\epsilon/2}\subseteq\Omega\setminus\mathcal{D}^{6}_{\epsilon/10}

by Lemma
4.12
(
iii
).

4.
Definition
2.11
(
iii
) follows directly
from Lemma
4.12
(
i
) and the fact proved in Step
3
.
Let the sound speed
c(|D\varphi|,\varphi)
be given by (
3.2
).
The strict ellipticity implies that

M\coloneqq\frac{|\partial_{\bm{\nu}}\varphi(\bm{\xi})|}{c(|D\varphi(\bm{\xi})|,\varphi(\bm{\xi}))}<1\qquad\text{on $\Gamma_{\rm shock}$}\,.

Define
M_{2}\coloneqq|\partial_{\bm{\nu}}\varphi_{2}(\bm{\xi})|
.
Similar to
[
2
, Eq.
(2.4.9)
]
, we have the following equality:

(1+\tfrac{\gamma-1}{2}M^{2})M^{-\tfrac{2(\gamma-1)}{\gamma+1}}=(1+\tfrac{\gamma-1}{2}M_{2}^{2})M_{2}^{-\tfrac{2(\gamma-1)}{\gamma+1}}\,.

Then
M_{2}>1
, and so
|D\varphi_{2}|>1=c(|D\varphi_{2}|,\varphi_{2})
holds
on
\Gamma_{\rm shock}
,

i.e.
,
\Gamma_{\rm shock}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\setminus\overline{B_{c_{2}}(O_{2})}
.
Finally, for the property:
\Gamma_{\rm shock}\subseteq\{\xi^{P_{3}}<\xi<\xi^{P_{2}}\}
,
we follow the same proof as in Lemma
3.2
by using all the properties above.
Therefore, all parts of Definition
2.11
have been verified,
which implies that
\varphi^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}
is an admissible solution
in the sense of Definition
2.11
.

\square

Proof of Theorem
2.1
.

We now complete the proof of Theorem
2.1
in five steps.

1.
We prove that the iteration map
\mathcal{I}:\overline{\mathcal{K}}\to C^{2,\alpha}_{(*)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})

is a compact map in the sense of Definition
B.5
.
Let
\{(u_{k},{\bm{\theta}}_{k})\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subseteq\mathcal{K}

converge to
(u,{\bm{\theta}})
in
C^{2,\alpha}_{(*)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})\times[0,\theta_{*}]^{2}
.
For each
k\in\mathbb{N}
, write
\Omega^{(k)}\coloneqq\Omega(u_{k},{\bm{\theta}}_{k})

and
\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(k)}\coloneqq\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(u_{k},{\bm{\theta}}_{k})}
,
as given by Definition
4.7
,
and
\Gamma_{\rm sonic}^{(k)}\coloneqq\Gamma^{5}_{\rm sonic}\cup\Gamma^{6}_{\rm sonic}

for the sonic arcs
related to parameters
{\bm{\theta}}_{k}
.
By Lemma
4.14
, there exists a unique solution

\hat{\phi}^{(k)}\in C^{2}(\Omega^{(k)})\cap C^{1}(\overline{\Omega^{(k)}}\setminus\Gamma_{\rm sonic}^{(k)})\cap C^{0}(\overline{\Omega^{(k)}})
of the iteration boundary value problem (
4.23
) related to
(u_{k},{\bm{\theta}}_{k})
.
For any
(s,t^{\prime})\in R_{\mathfrak{g}^{(k)}_{\rm sh}}
, define

\hat{w}^{(k)}(s,t^{\prime})\coloneqq\big(\hat{\phi}^{(k)}+\frac{1}{2}|\bm{\xi}|^{2}-\varphi^{*}_{{\bm{\theta}}_{k}}\big)\circ(\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}_{k}})^{-1}(s,t^{\prime})\,,

where
R_{\mathfrak{g}^{(k)}_{\rm sh}}
,
\varphi^{*}_{{\bm{\theta}}_{k}}
,
and
\mathcal{G}_{1}^{{\bm{\theta}}_{k}}
are given by (
4.42
),
Definition
4.4
, and (
4.7
), respectively.
Then

\hat{w}^{(k)}=\hat{u}^{(k)}\circ G_{2,\mathfrak{g}^{(k)}_{\rm sh}}

for
\hat{u}^{(k)}:\overline{\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}}\to\mathbb{R}
given by (
4.24
)
with
\hat{\phi}=\hat{\phi}^{(k)}
, and

G_{2,\mathfrak{g}^{(k)}_{\rm sh}}
given by (
4.13
).
Subsequently, we obtain functions
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{(k)}_{\rm sh}:[-1,1]\to\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}}
via Lemma
4.21
.

Since
({u}_{k},{\bm{\theta}}_{k})
converges to
(u,{\bm{\theta}})
in
C^{1,\alpha}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})\times[0,\theta_{*}]^{2}
,
we find that

\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(k)}\to\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}
in
C^{1,\alpha}([-1,1])

by Lemma
4.8
(
iii
).
Fix any compact subset
K\subseteq R_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}
.
From (
4.13
), Definition
4.10
(
iii
),
and the convergence of
\{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(k)}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
, we see that

G_{2,\mathfrak{g}^{(k)}_{\rm sh}}\to G_{2,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}
in
C^{1,\alpha}(K)
.
Then, by Corollary
4.16
and the convergence of
\{\hat{u}^{(k)}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
,
we obtain that

\hat{w}^{(k)}\to\hat{w}
in
C^{1,\alpha}(K)
.

By Lemma
4.17
, Proposition
4.20
(
iii
),
and the local convergence of
\{\hat{w}^{(k)}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
, for any
b_{1},b_{2}\in[-1,1]
chosen as in each case of Proposition
4.20
(
a
)–(
c
),

\{\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}^{(k)}}(\hat{w}^{(k)})\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
converges to
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}(\hat{w})
in the corresponding function space.
Combining the statement above with the convergence of
\{\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{(k)}_{\rm sh}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
from Lemma
4.21
, we conclude that

\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{g}^{(k)}_{\rm sh}}(\hat{w}^{(k)})\circ(G_{2,\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{(k)}_{\rm sh}})^{-1}\to\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm sh}}(\hat{w})\circ(G_{2,\hat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\rm sh}})^{-1}\qquad\,\,\text{in $C^{2,\alpha}_{(*)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})$}\,.

Since
C^{2,2\alpha}_{(*)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})
is compactly embedded into
C^{2,\alpha}_{(*)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})
,
we obtain from (
4.47
) that
\mathcal{I}(U)
is precompact
in
C^{2,\alpha}_{(*)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})
for any bounded
subset
U\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{K}}
so
that
the iteration map
\mathcal{I}
is compact.

2.
Next, we show that constants
N_{1},\delta_{2}>0
in Definition
4.10

can be chosen, depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{*})
,
such that, for any
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap[0,\theta_{*}]^{2}
, no fixed point of
\mathcal{I}(\cdot,{\bm{\theta}})

lies on boundary
\partial\mathcal{K}({\bm{\theta}})
,
where
\partial\mathcal{K}({\bm{\theta}})
is considered relative to

C^{2,\alpha}_{(*)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})
.

Suppose that
(u,{\bm{\theta}})\in\overline{\mathcal{K}}
satisfies
\mathcal{I}(u,{\bm{\theta}})=u
.
For
\varphi=\varphi^{(u,{\bm{\theta}})}
defined in
\Omega=\Omega(u,{\bm{\theta}})

by Definition
4.7
(
iii
), extend
\varphi
to
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}

via (
2.41
).
Then
\varphi
is an admissible solution corresponding to parameters
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap[0,\theta_{*}]^{2}
by Proposition
4.23
.

From
(
3.17
),
Propositions
3.4
,
3.8
,
and
4.3
(
iv
),
and Lemma
3.3
,
constants
(N_{2},\tilde{\mu},,\rho^{*}(\gamma),C_{\rm ub})
are fixed so that
any admissible solution satisfies the strict inequalities in
Definition
4.10
(
iii
) and
(
vi
)–(
v
).
Moreover, from Lemma
4.17
(
i
),

u
satisfies the strict inequality given in Definition
4.10
(
i
)
when

N_{1}\geq N_{1}^{({\rm adm})}
.

For condition (
iv
) of Definition
4.10
,
constants
(\mu_{0},\mu_{1},N_{4},N_{5},\sigma_{2})
are fixed such that any admissible solution
corresponding to
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap[0,\theta_{*}]^{2}
satisfies
the strict inequalities (
4.22
).
Next, we focus on the strict inequalities (
4.21
).
It is direct to see that the strict inequalities (
4.21
)
hold when

\max\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\}<\frac{\delta_{1}}{N_{1}^{2}}
,
since

\mathscr{K}_{2}(\max\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\})<0

and inequality (
2.42
)
in Definition
2.11
(
iv
)
and Lemma
3.1
(
ii
) hold
for any admissible solution corresponding to
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap[0,\theta_{*}]^{2}
.
When
\max\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\}\geq\frac{\delta_{1}}{N_{1}^{2}}
,
we similarly have

\displaystyle\varphi-\max\{\varphi_{5},\varphi_{6}\}>0\quad

\displaystyle\text{in}\;\overline{\Omega}\setminus(\mathcal{D}^{5}_{\epsilon/10}\cup\mathcal{D}^{6}_{\epsilon/10})\,,

\displaystyle\partial_{e_{S_{2j}}}(\varphi_{2}-\varphi)<0\quad

\displaystyle\text{in}\;\overline{\Omega}\setminus\mathcal{D}^{j}_{\epsilon/10}\;\text{for}\;j=5,6\,.

By Corollary
3.10
, and Propositions
3.12
–
3.14
and
3.16
,
we obtain that
\varphi\in C^{1,\alpha}(\Omega)
.
Combining this with Lemma
3.17
,
there exist both

\tau_{0}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{*})

and

\tau_{1}>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{*},\delta_{1},N_{1})

such that, for all
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap\{\frac{\delta_{1}}{N_{1}^{2}}\leq\max\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\}\leq\theta_{*}\}
,

\displaystyle\psi=\varphi-\max\{\varphi_{5},\varphi_{6}\}\geq\tau_{0}\quad

\displaystyle\text{in}\;\overline{\Omega}\setminus(\mathcal{D}^{5}_{\epsilon/10}\cup\mathcal{D}^{6}_{\epsilon/10})\,,

\displaystyle\partial_{e_{S_{2j}}}(\varphi_{2}-\varphi)\leq-\tau_{1}\quad

\displaystyle\text{in}\;\overline{\Omega}\setminus\mathcal{D}^{j}_{\epsilon/10}\;\text{for}\;j=5,6\,.

For any fixed
\delta_{1}>0
,
we can choose
\delta_{2}>0
small enough such that

\mathscr{K}_{2}(\max\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\})\leq\frac{\delta_{1}\delta_{2}}{N_{1}^{2}}<\min\{\tau_{0},\tau_{1}\}\qquad\,\,\text{for all
${\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap\{\frac{\delta_{1}}{N_{1}^{2}}\leq\max\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\}\leq\theta_{*}\}$}\,,

which implies that the strict inequalities (
4.21
) hold
when

\max\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\}\geq\frac{\delta_{1}}{N_{1}^{2}}
.

Finally, let
(\alpha,\epsilon,\delta_{1},\delta_{3},N_{1})
be chosen as
in Lemma
4.17
with
\delta_{2}
chosen as above.
Then the strict inequality (
4.25
) of
Definition
4.10
(
vii
) holds
by Proposition
4.18
.
Therefore,
u\in\mathcal{K}({\bm{\theta}})
for any
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\cap[0,\theta_{*}]^{2}
,
which completes this step.

3.
Now, we can fix constants
(\alpha,\epsilon,\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3},N_{1})
in Definition
4.10
as follows:

•

Choose constants
(\alpha,\epsilon,\delta_{1},\delta_{3},N_{1})
as in Lemma
4.17

and reduce
(\epsilon,\delta_{1})
as in Proposition
4.23
,
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{*})
;

•

Fix
\delta_{2}
as in Step
2
, depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{*},\delta_{1},N_{1})
;

•

Adjust
\delta_{3}>0
as in Definition
4.22
,
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{*},\delta_{2})
.

4.

Leray-Schauder degree theory.

When
{\bm{\theta}}=\mathbf{0}
,

\varphi_{5}=\varphi_{6}=\varphi_{0}
with
\varphi_{0}
defined in §
2.1.3
,
and the iteration boundary value problem
in the
(s,t^{\prime})
–coordinates is
a homogeneous problem.
From Lemma
4.14
, the iteration boundary value
problem (
4.23
) corresponding to any
(u,\mathbf{0})\in\overline{\mathcal{K}}

has a unique solution
\hat{\phi}=\frac{1}{2}|\bm{\xi}|^{2}+\varphi_{\mathbf{0}}^{*}=v_{2}\eta_{0}
,
where
\eta_{0}>0
is defined in §
2.1.3
.
It follows from Definition
4.10
that
the unique fixed point
u\equiv 0
of
\mathcal{I}(\cdot,\mathbf{0})
lies in
\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{0})
,
and
\textbf{Ind}(\mathcal{I}(\cdot,\mathbf{0}),\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{0}))=1
.
From the arguments above,
map
\mathcal{I}(\cdot,{\bm{\theta}}):\overline{\mathcal{K}({\bm{\theta}})}\to C^{2,\alpha}_{(*)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter})

satisfies
\mathcal{I}(\cdot,{\bm{\theta}})\in V(\mathcal{K}({\bm{\theta}}),C^{2,\alpha}_{(*)}(\mathcal{Q}^{\rm iter}))

from Definition
B.6
in Appendix
B
.
From Theorem
B.2
and for any
t\in[0,1]
, we have

\textbf{Ind}(\mathcal{I}(\cdot,t{\bm{\theta}}),\mathcal{K}(t{\bm{\theta}}))=\textbf{Ind}(\mathcal{I}(\cdot,\mathbf{0}),\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{0}))\qquad\text{for all ${\bm{\theta}}\in[0,\theta_{*}]^{2}$}\,.

We obtain the existence of a fixed point of map
\mathcal{I}(\cdot,{\bm{\theta}})
,
which is equivalent to the existence of an admissible solution corresponding
to
{\bm{\theta}}\in[0,\theta_{*}]^{2}
in the sense of Definition
2.11

by Proposition
4.23
.
The arbitrary choice of
\theta_{*}\in(0,\theta^{\rm d})
completes
the proof of the existence of admissible solutions for any
{\bm{\theta}}\in[0,\theta^{\rm d})^{2}
.

5.

Proof of the continuity at
{\bm{\theta}}=\mathbf{0}
.

Let
\varphi_{\rm norm}
be the admissible solution for
{\bm{\theta}}=\mathbf{0}
,
which is given by (
2.24
),
and let
\Omega^{(\bm{0})}
be the corresponding pseudo-subsonic domain.
For any sequence
\{{\bm{\theta}}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subseteq\Theta

with
{\bm{\theta}}_{k}\to\bm{0}
as
k\to\infty
, let
\varphi^{({\bm{\theta}}_{k})}

be an admissible solution corresponding to
{\bm{\theta}}_{k}
,
and let
\Omega^{(k)}
be the corresponding pseudo-subsonic domain.

Let
G\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
be any open set satisfying
\overline{G}\Subset\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
.
Choose any
compact subset
K\Subset G\setminus\partial\Omega^{(\bm{0})}
.
Then, by Lemma
3.17
(
ii
),
passing to a subsequence (still denoted)
k
,

K\cap\Omega^{(\bm{0})}\Subset\Omega^{(k)}
and
K\setminus\Omega^{(\bm{0})}\Subset G\setminus\Omega^{(k)}
.
Using Proposition
4.3
(
iv
),
Proposition
4.6
,
Lemma
4.8
(
v
),
and the continuity property of admissible solutions given in Step
1
of the proof of Lemma
4.17
, we have

\|\varphi^{({\bm{\theta}}_{k})}-\varphi_{\rm norm}\|_{1,\alpha,K\cap\Omega^{(\bm{0})}}\to 0\qquad\,\,\text{as $k\to\infty$}\,.

Similarly, using (
2.24
),
Proposition
2.6
(
i
),
and (
2.41
),
we have

\|\varphi^{({\bm{\theta}}_{k})}-\varphi_{\rm norm}\|_{W^{1,1}(K\setminus\Omega^{(\bm{0})})}\to 0\qquad\text{as $k\to\infty$}\,.

Finally, by (
2.41
) and the uniform bound in Lemma
3.3
,
there exists a constant
C>0
depending only on
(\gamma,v_{2},G)
such that

\|\varphi^{({\bm{\theta}}_{k})}-\varphi_{\rm norm}\|_{0,1,G}\leq C
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
which leads to

\|\varphi^{({\bm{\theta}}_{k})}-\varphi_{\rm norm}\|_{W^{1,1}(G)}\leq\|\varphi^{({\bm{\theta}}_{k})}-\varphi_{\rm norm}\|_{W^{1,1}(K)}+2C\,{\rm meas}(G\setminus K)\,.

This completes the proof of Theorem
2.1
.

\square

5.
Optimal Regularity of Solutions and Convexity of Free Boundaries

With the
a priori
estimates obtained in §
3
–§
4
,
we can now give the complete proofs
of Theorems
2.2
–
2.3
, respectively.

5.1.
Proof of Theorem
2.2
: Optimal regularity of solutions

This section is devoted to the complete proof of Theorem
2.2
.
Let
\varphi
be an admissible solution in the sense of Definition
2.11
.

Proof.

Fix
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta
.
By the symmetry of the problem, it suffices to prove the statement near
\Gamma_{\rm sonic}^{5}
.

1
.
Proof of
Theorem
2.2
(
i
)
.

First, it follows from Lemmas
3.6
and
3.9

that
\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}
is
C^{\infty}
in its
relative interior and
\varphi\in C^{\infty}(\overline{\Omega}\setminus\Gamma_{\rm{sonic}}^{5}\cup\Gamma_{\rm{sonic}}^{6})
.
By Definition
2.9
,
\Gamma_{\rm{sonic}}^{5}
is a closed arc of a circle
when
\theta_{1}\in[0,\theta^{\rm{s}})
, and becomes a point
P_{1}

when
\theta_{1}\in[\theta^{\rm{s}},\theta^{\rm{d}})
.
If
\theta_{1}\in[0,\theta^{\rm{s}})
, it follows from Propositions
3.12
–
3.13

that
\varphi
is
C^{1,1}
up to
\Gamma_{\rm{sonic}}^{5}
,
whilst, if
\theta_{1}\in[\theta^{\rm{s}},\theta^{\rm{d}})
,
then Propositions
3.14
and
3.16
imply that

\varphi
is
C^{1,\alpha}
up to
\Gamma_{\rm{sonic}}^{5}=\{P_{1}\}
for some
\alpha\in(0,1)
.
Furthermore, from Propositions
3.12
–
3.14

and
3.16
, we see that
\overline{\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cup S_{25}^{\rm seg}}
is a
C^{1,\alpha}
–curve,
including at
P_{2}
, for any
\theta_{1}\in[0,\theta^{\rm d})
.
It remains to show that
\overline{\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cup S_{25}^{\rm seg}}
is a
C^{2,\alpha}
–curve when
\theta_{1}\in[0,\theta^{\rm s})
.

For
\theta_{1}\in[0,\theta^{\rm s})
, let coordinates
(x,y)=\mathcal{R}(\bm{\xi})
be
given by (
3.24
).
Let
\bar{\varepsilon}>0
,

f_{5,0}
, and
f_{5,{\rm sh}}
be from Step
1
of the proof of Proposition
3.12
.
We extend

f_{5,\rm sh}
to

(-\bar{\varepsilon},\bar{\varepsilon})
by

(5.1)

f_{5,\rm sh}(x)\coloneqq{f}_{5,0}(x)\qquad\,\text{for $x\in(-\bar{\varepsilon},0]$}\,,

so that

(5.2)

(f_{5,\rm sh}-{f}_{5,0})(0)=(f_{5,\rm sh}-{f}_{5,0})^{\prime}(0)=0\,.

Define
\psi\coloneqq(\varphi-\varphi_{5})\circ\mathcal{R}^{-1}

and
\bar{\psi}_{25}\coloneqq(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{5})\circ\mathcal{R}^{-1}
.
Then
\bar{\psi}_{25}(x,{f}_{5,0}(x))=0
, which implies

\bar{\psi}_{25}(x,f_{5,\rm sh}(x))-\bar{\psi}_{25}(x,{f}_{5,0}(x))=\psi(x,f_{5,\rm sh}(x))\qquad\text{for $x\in(0,\bar{\varepsilon})$}\,.

Differentiating the above equality with respect to
x
twice,
we obtain the following expression:

(5.3)

(f_{5,{\rm sh}}-{f}_{5,0})^{\prime\prime}(x)=\frac{A_{1}(x)+A_{2}(x)+A_{3}(x)}{\partial_{y}\bar{\psi}_{25}(x,f_{5,\rm sh}(x))}\,,

where, for
(a_{0},a_{1},a_{2})\coloneqq(1,2,1)
,

\displaystyle A_{1}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum\limits_{k=0}^{2}a_{k}\big(({f}_{5,0}(x))^{k}\partial_{x}^{2-k}\partial_{y}^{2}\bar{\psi}_{25}(x,{f}_{5,0}(x))-(f_{5,{\rm sh}}(x))^{k}\partial_{x}^{2-k}\partial_{y}^{2}\bar{\psi}_{25}(x,f_{5,\rm sh}(x)\big)\,,

\displaystyle A_{2}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left(\partial_{y}\bar{\psi}_{25}(x,{f}_{5,0}(x))-\partial_{y}\bar{\psi}_{25}(x,f_{5,\rm sh}(x))\right){f}^{\prime\prime}_{5,0}(x)\,,

\displaystyle A_{3}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq-f_{5,\rm sh}^{\prime\prime}(x)\partial_{y}\psi(x,{f}_{5,\rm sh}(x))-\sum\limits_{k=0}^{2}a_{k}(f_{5,\rm sh}^{\prime}(x))^{k}\partial_{x}^{2-k}\partial_{y}^{k}\psi(x,f_{5,\rm sh}(x))\,.

By (
5.2
),
A_{1}(0)=A_{2}(0)=0
.
We differentiate the boundary condition in the tangential direction along
\Gamma_{\rm shock}

and apply the arguments in
Step
1

of the proof of Proposition
3.12
to obtain that there exists
a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle|\psi_{xx}|\leq C|(\psi,\psi_{x},\psi_{y},\psi_{xy},\psi_{yy})|\qquad\text{on $\mathcal{R}(\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap\partial\Omega^{5}_{\bar{\varepsilon}})$}\,,

where
\Omega^{5}_{\bar{\varepsilon}}
is from Step
1
of the proof of Proposition
3.12
.
From the above estimate and Proposition
3.12
, we obtain
that

\psi_{xx}(x,f_{5,\rm sh}(x))\to 0
as
x\to 0^{+}
, which implies that
A_{3}(x)\to 0
as
x\to 0^{+}
.
Hence,
\partial_{y}\bar{\psi}_{25}(x,f_{5,\rm sh}(x))\neq 0

on
\mathcal{R}(\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap\partial\Omega^{5}_{\bar{\varepsilon}})
.
Then we conclude from (
5.3
) that

(f_{5,\rm sh}-{f}_{5,0})^{\prime\prime}(0)=0
.
This implies that the extension of
f_{5,\rm sh}
given by (
5.1
) is
in
C^{2}([-\bar{\varepsilon},\bar{\varepsilon}])
.
Furthermore, we conclude from Proposition
3.12
that
the extension of
f_{5,\rm sh}
given by (
5.1
) is
in
C^{2,\alpha}((-\bar{\varepsilon},\bar{\varepsilon}))
for any
\alpha\in(0,1)
.
This implies that
\overline{\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cup S_{25}^{\rm seg}}
is
C^{2,\alpha}

for any
\alpha\in(0,1)
, including at point
P_{2}=\mathcal{R}^{-1}(0,{f}_{5,\rm sh}(0))
.
This completes the proof of statement (
i
).

2
.
Proof of
Theorem
2.2
(
ii
)–(
iii
)
.

It suffices to consider the case that
\theta_{1}\in[0,\theta^{\rm{s}})
.
We can apply Theorem
B.3
to obtain the regularity of
\psi

in the neighbourhood of
\mathcal{R}(\Gamma_{\rm{sonic}}^{5})
.
Then the admissible solution
\varphi
satisfies Theorem
2.2
(
ii
)–(
iii
).

3
.
Proof of
Theorem
2.2
(
iv
)
.

By Propositions
3.12
–
3.13
,

\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap\partial\Omega^{5}_{\bar{\varepsilon}}
can be represented as the graph of
y=f_{5,{\rm sh}}(x)

for
0\leq x\leq\bar{\varepsilon}
.

Let
\{y_{m}^{(1)}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence satisfying that

0<y_{m}^{(1)}<{f}_{5,\rm sh}(0)
for each

m\in\mathbb{N}
, and
y_{m}^{(1)}\to{f}_{5,\rm sh}(0)
as
{m\rightarrow\infty}
.
By Theorem
2.2
(
iii
),
we can choose a sequence
\{x_{m}^{(1)}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
such that

(x_{m}^{(1)},y_{m}^{(1)})\in\mathcal{R}(\Omega^{5}_{\bar{\varepsilon}})
,

0<x_{m}^{(1)}<\frac{1}{m}
, and

\char 12\relax{\psi_{xx}(x_{m}^{(1)},y_{m}^{(1)})-\frac{1}{\gamma+1}}\leq\frac{1}{m}\qquad\text{for each $m\in\mathbb{N}$}\,.

It follows from Step
1
of the proof of Proposition
3.12

that
0<y_{m}^{(1)}<{f}_{5,\rm sh}(0)<f_{5,\rm sh}(x_{m}^{(1)})
for each

m\in\mathbb{N}
so that

(5.4)

\lim\limits_{m\rightarrow\infty}(x_{m}^{(1)},y_{m}^{(1)})=(0,{f}_{5,\rm sh}(0))\,,\qquad\lim\limits_{m\rightarrow\infty}|\psi_{xx}(x_{m}^{(1)},y_{m}^{(1)})|=\frac{1}{\gamma+1}\,.

On the other hand, there exists a constant
\varepsilon\in(0,\bar{\varepsilon}]
such that the boundary condition
on
\Gamma_{\rm shock}\cap\partial\Omega^{5}_{\bar{\varepsilon}}
can be written as

\psi_{x}+b_{1}\psi_{y}+b_{0}\psi=0
with

(b_{0},b_{1})=(b_{0},b_{1})(\psi_{x},\psi_{y},\psi,x,f_{5,\rm sh}(x))
for any
x\in(0,\varepsilon)
.
Let
\omega>0
from Step
1
of the proof of Proposition
3.12
. Then

\{(x,f_{5,\rm sh}(x)-\tfrac{\omega}{10}x)\,:\,0<x<\varepsilon\}\subseteq\mathcal{R}(\Omega^{5}_{\bar{\varepsilon}})\,.

Denote
F(x)\coloneqq\psi_{x}(x,f_{5,\rm sh}(x)-\frac{\omega}{10}x)
.
By the geometric relation given above, we have

\displaystyle F(x)

\displaystyle=\psi_{x}(x,f_{5,\rm sh}(x))-\tfrac{\omega}{10}x\int_{0}^{1}\psi_{xy}(x,f_{5,\rm sh}(x)-\tfrac{\omega}{10}tx)\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=-(b_{1}\psi_{y}+b_{0}\psi)(x,f_{5,\rm sh}(x))-\tfrac{\omega}{10}x\int_{0}^{1}\psi_{xy}(x,f_{5,\rm sh}(x)-\tfrac{\omega}{10}tx)\,{\rm d}t\qquad\mbox{for $x\in(0,\varepsilon)$}\,.

Then Proposition
3.12
implies that

F(0)=0
,
F\in C([0,\varepsilon])\cap C^{1}((0,\varepsilon))
,

\lim\limits_{x\rightarrow 0+}\frac{F(x)}{x}=0

so that,
by the mean value theorem,
there exists a sequence
\{x_{m}^{(2)}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\subseteq(0,\varepsilon)
such that

(5.5)

\lim\limits_{m\rightarrow\infty}x_{m}^{(2)}=0\,,\qquad F^{\prime}(x_{m}^{(2)})=0\,.

For each
m\in\mathbb{N}
, define
y_{m}^{(2)}\coloneqq f_{5,\rm sh}(x_{m}^{(2)})-\frac{\omega}{10}x_{m}^{(2)}
,
so that
\{(x_{m}^{(2)},y_{m}^{(2)})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\subseteq\mathcal{R}(\Omega^{5}_{\bar{\varepsilon}})
.
By the definition of
F(\cdot)
and (
5.5
), we have

(5.6)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{m\rightarrow\infty}\psi_{xx}(x_{m}^{(2)},y_{m}^{(2)})

\displaystyle=\lim\limits_{m\rightarrow\infty}F^{\prime}(x_{m}^{(2)})-\lim\limits_{m\rightarrow\infty}(f_{5,\rm sh}(x_{m}^{(2)})-\tfrac{\omega}{10})\psi_{xy}(x_{m}^{(2)},y_{m}^{(2)})

\displaystyle=-\lim\limits_{m\rightarrow\infty}(f_{5,\rm sh}(x_{m}^{(2)})-\tfrac{\omega}{10})\psi_{xy}(x_{m}^{(2)},y_{m}^{(2)})\,.

Since
\lim\limits_{m\rightarrow\infty}(x_{m}^{(2)},y_{m}^{(2)})=(0,f_{5,\rm sh}(0))
,
we combine (
5.6
) with Proposition
3.12
to obtain

\lim\limits_{m\rightarrow\infty}\psi_{xx}(x_{m}^{(2)},y_{m}^{(2)})=0\,.

We have demonstrated that there are two sequences,

\{(x_{m}^{(1)},y_{m}^{(1)})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\{(x_{m}^{(2)},y_{m}^{(2)})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
,
taking values in
\mathcal{R}(\Omega^{5}_{\bar{\varepsilon}})
, such that the limits of both sequences are
(0,f_{5,\rm sh}(0))
, but

\lim\limits_{m\rightarrow\infty}\psi_{xx}(x_{m}^{(1)},y_{m}^{(1)})\neq\lim\limits_{m\rightarrow\infty}\psi_{xx}(x_{m}^{(2)},y_{m}^{(2)})\,.

This completes the proof of Theorem
2.2
(
iv
).

\square

5.2.
Proof of Theorem 2.3: Convexity of free boundaries and transonic shocks

We now discuss the geometric properties
of the transonic shock as a free boundary.
In Chen-Feldman-Xiang
[
12
]
, a general framework was developed under which the self-similar transonic shock, as a free boundary,
is proved to be uniformly convex for the potential flow equation;
see Framework (A) in Appendix
B.5
.
In this subsection, we apply this framework to prove the uniform convexity
of the transonic shock in the four-shock interaction problem, so it suffices to prove that
the admissible solutions satisfy the conditions in Theorems
B.4
–
B.5
.

Lemma 5.1
.

The following statements hold
:

(i)

Any admissible solution in the sense of
Definition
2.11

satisfies the conditions of
Theorems
B.4
–
B.5
.

(ii)

Any global weak solution of the four-shock interaction problem in the sense of

Definition
2.1
—satisfying also all
properties of
Definition
2.11

except (
2.43
) and with transonic shock
\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}
being
a strictly convex graph—satisfies condition (
2.43
) of
Definition
2.11
.

Proof.

We divide the proof into six steps. We start with
the proof of assertion (
i
).

1.

Claim
: Region
\Omega
satisfies the conditions in Framework (A).
Indeed, for
{\bm{\theta}}\in[0,\theta^{\rm s})^{2}
, the required piecewise regularity holds,
since
\Gamma_{\rm{sym}}
is a straight segment,
while
\Gamma_{\rm{sonic}}^{5}
and
\Gamma_{\rm{sonic}}^{6}

are the arcs of the sonic circles,
and
\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}
has the regularity stated in Theorem
2.2
(
i
).
For any incident angles
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta
, the fact that all angles of the corners of
\Omega

are less than
\pi
can be verified as follows:

If
\theta_{1}\in[0,\theta^{\rm s})
, since
\overline{S^{\rm seg}_{25}\cup\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}}
is a
C^{2,\alpha}
–curve for any
\alpha\in(0,1)
, including at points
P_{2}
,
it follows that
\Gamma_{\rm shock}
is tangential to
S^{\rm seg}_{25}
at
P_{2}
.
Meanwhile, for any
{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta
,
\partial B_{c_{5}}(O_{5})
and
S_{25}

always have two intersection points including
P_{2}
.
Therefore, the meeting angles at the two corners
\{P_{1},P_{2}\}
belong
to
[0,\pi)
when

\theta_{1}\in(0,\theta^{\rm s})
.
If
\theta_{1}\in[\theta^{\rm s},\theta^{\rm d})
, we see that
P_{2}=P_{1}=P_{0}^{1}
.
From (
2.38
),
the meeting angle at
P_{0}^{1}
is
\pi-\theta_{25}\in(0,\frac{\pi}{2})
, which satisfies

\pi-\theta_{25}\geq\delta_{5,6}^{(\theta^{\rm s})}>0\,.

Similarly, the meeting angles at the two corners
\{P_{3},P_{4}\}
also belong to
(0,\pi)

for any
\theta_{2}\in[0,\theta^{\rm d})
.

2.
The entropy condition (C-
1
) in Theorem
B.4
follows directly
from the properties of
Definition
2.11
, where state
(0)
in Theorem
B.4
is state
(2)
in our problem.
From the regularity of
\varphi
and
\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}
in Theorem
2.2
(
i
),
we see that conditions (C-
2
) and (C-
4
) of Theorem
B.4
hold.
Property (
iii
) of Definition
2.11
implies that condition
(C-
3
) of Theorem
B.4
holds.

3.
We use the notation for the endpoints of
\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}
from Framework (A),
that is,
A\coloneqq P_{2}
and
B\coloneqq P_{3}
,
where
P_{i+1}\equiv P_{0}^{i}
when
\theta_{i}\in[\theta^{\rm s},\theta^{\rm d})
for
i=1,2
.
Then, by the properties of Definition
2.11
, we see that

\bm{\tau}_{A}=\bm{e}_{S_{25}}
and

\bm{\tau}_{B}=\bm{e}_{S_{26}}
,
and it is clear that
\bm{\tau}_{A}\neq\pm\bm{\tau}_{B}
for all
{\bm{\theta}}\neq\bm{0}
.
Accordingly, let
{\rm Con}

be given by (
B.14
).
Combining property (
2.43
) with the fact that

\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}
is the level set:
\{\varphi-\varphi_{2}=0\}
,
we obtain that

\{P+{\rm Con}\}\cap\Omega=\varnothing
for all
P\in\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}
.
Thus, condition (C-
5
) of Theorem
B.4
is satisfied.

4.
For condition (C-
6
) of Theorem
B.4
,
we show that Case (C-
6c
) holds with
\phi=\varphi-\varphi_{2}
,
\bm{e}=(0,1)\in{\rm Con}
,

\Gamma_{1}\coloneqq\Gamma_{\rm sonic}^{6}\cup\Gamma_{\rm{sym}}\cup\Gamma_{\rm sonic}^{5}
,
and
\Gamma_{2}=\varnothing
.
For
j=5,6
, write
w\coloneqq\partial_{\bm{e}}(\varphi-\varphi_{j})
,
which satisfies a strictly elliptic equation in
\Omega
by taking the derivative of (
3.1
)
in direction
\bm{e}
, and using Definition
2.11
(
iii
).
By conditions (
iii
)–(
iv
) of Problem
2.10
,
w=0

on
\Gamma_{\rm sym}\cup\Gamma^{j}_{\rm sonic}
, which is a global maximum by Lemma
3.1
(
iii
).
Therefore, if
P\in\Gamma_{\rm sym}\cup\Gamma^{j}_{\rm sonic}
is a point of local minimum
for
w
, then
w=0
on
B_{r}(P)\cap\Omega
for some
r>0
, and so
w=0
in
\Omega
by the strong maximum principle,
which is a contradiction to Lemma
3.1
(
i
).
It follows that
\phi_{\bm{e}}\equiv w-v_{2}
cannot attain a local minimum on
\Gamma_{1}
.

5.
We now show that conditions (C-
7
)–(C-
10
)
are satisfied with
\hat{\Gamma}_{0}={\Gamma_{\rm{sonic}}^{5}}\setminus\{P_{2}\}

for
\theta_{1}\in[0,\theta^{\rm s})

(resp.
\hat{\Gamma}_{0}=\varnothing
for
\theta_{1}\in[\theta^{\rm s},\theta^{\rm d})
),

\hat{\Gamma}_{1}=\Gamma_{\rm{sym}}^{0}
,
\hat{\Gamma}_{2}=\varnothing
,
and
\hat{\Gamma}_{3}={\Gamma_{\rm{sonic}}^{6}}\setminus\{P_{3}\}
for
\theta_{2}\in[0,\theta^{\rm s})

(resp.
\hat{\Gamma}_{3}=\varnothing
for
\theta_{2}\in[\theta^{\rm s},\theta^{\rm d})
).

Indeed, (C-
7
) clearly holds.
Also, (C-
8
) holds since
D\varphi=D\varphi_{5}
on

\Gamma_{\rm{sonic}}^{5}
for
\theta_{1}\in[0,\theta^{\rm s})
so that

\phi_{\bm{e}}=\partial_{\bm{e}}(\varphi_{5}-\varphi_{2})
is a constant.
Similarly,
D\varphi=D\varphi_{6}
on

\Gamma_{\rm{sonic}}^{6}
for
\theta_{2}\in[0,\theta^{\rm s})
.
Condition (C-
9
) on
\hat{\Gamma}_{1}=\Gamma_{\rm{sym}}^{0}
can be checked as follows:
If
\bm{e}\neq(0,\pm 1)
,
then, as in Step
6
of the proof of
[
12
, Lemma 7.8]
,

\phi_{\bm{e}}
cannot attain its local minima or maxima on

\Gamma_{\rm{sym}}^{0}
.
In the other case, when
\bm{e}=(0,\pm 1)
,
the slip boundary condition
\partial_{\eta}\phi=0
on
\Gamma_{\rm{sym}}^{0}
implies that
\phi_{\bm{e}}
is constant
on
\Gamma_{\rm{sym}}^{0}
, which
verifies (C-
9
).
Case (C-10a) of (C-
10
) clearly holds here since
\hat{\Gamma}_{2}=\varnothing
.
This concludes the proof of assertion (
i
).

6.
It remains to prove assertion (
ii
):
Any solution satisfying all the properties of Definition
2.11

except (
2.43
),
and with transonic shock
\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}
being a strictly convex graph
in the sense of (
B.15
)–(
B.16
), satisfies condition (
2.43
) of Definition
2.11
.

We apply (
B.15
) in the present case with

(A,B)=(P_{2},P_{3})
and
\bm{e}=(0,1)
such that
\bm{\xi}(S,T)=(-T,S)
.
Then, using the properties of
Definition
2.11
(i),

\bm{e}_{S_{25}}=\frac{(-1,f^{\prime}(T_{A}))}{|(-1,f^{\prime}(T_{A}))|}\,,\qquad\,\bm{e}_{S_{26}}={-}\frac{(-1,f^{\prime}(T_{B}))}{|(-1,f^{\prime}(T_{B}))|}\,.

Also, from the strict concavity of
f
in the sense of (
B.16
),
we obtain that
f^{\prime}(T_{A})>f^{\prime}(T)>f^{\prime}(T_{B})

and
f(T)<f(T_{1})+f^{\prime}(T_{1})(T-T_{1})

for all
T,\,T_{1}\in(T_{A},T_{B})
.
From this, we see that
\{P+{\rm Con}\}\cap\Omega=\varnothing
for any

P\in\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}
.
Since
\varphi\leq\varphi_{2}
in
\Omega
from Definition
2.11

and
\varphi=\varphi_{2}
on
\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}
,
we obtain that
\partial_{\bm{e}}\varphi\geq\partial_{\bm{e}}\varphi_{2}

for any
\bm{e}\in{\rm Con}
, which implies (
2.43
).

\square

Appendix A
Proof of Lemma
2.5
and Related Properties of Solutions

A.1.
Proof of Lemma
2.5
: Monotonicity of critical angles for 2-D steady potential flow

The proof of Lemma
2.5
is given in four steps.

1.

The shock polar for 2-D steady potential flow connecting a constant supersonic
upstream state
U_{\infty}=(\rho_{\infty},u_{\infty},0)

to a constant downstream state
U=(\rho,u,v)
is given by

(A.1)

\displaystyle u=u_{\infty}-\frac{2\big(h(\rho)-h(\rho_{\infty})\big)}{\rho+\rho_{\infty}}\frac{\rho}{u_{\infty}}\,,\quad v^{2}=u_{\infty}^{2}-u^{2}-2\big(h(\rho)-h(\rho_{\infty})\big)\,,

where
h(\rho)\coloneqq\frac{1}{\gamma-1}(\rho^{\gamma-1}-1)
for
\gamma>1
, and
h(\rho)=\ln\rho
for
\gamma=1
;
see
e.g.

[
19
, §17.2]
for the derivation.
The downstream density
\rho\in(\rho_{\infty},\overline{\rho})

is a parameter along the shock polar, where the maximal density
\overline{\rho}>\rho_{\infty}

is determined uniquely by
v(\overline{\rho})=0
in (
A.1
), which
satisfies

\displaystyle M_{\infty}^{2}\Big(1-\big(\frac{\rho_{\infty}}{\overline{\rho}}\big)^{2}\Big)=\frac{2\big(h(\overline{\rho})-h(\rho_{\infty})\big)}{c_{\infty}^{2}}\,,

for
M_{\infty}\coloneqq\frac{u_{\infty}}{c_{\infty}}>1
,
the Mach number of the upstream state that is assumed to be supersonic, and
c_{\infty}
the sonic speed.
In the following, we denote by
w\coloneqq\tan{\theta_{\rm stdy}}
,
where
\theta_{\rm stdy}\in(0,\frac{\pi}{2})
is the angle between the velocities of the upstream
and the downstream flows, and by
\tau\coloneqq\frac{\rho}{\rho_{\infty}}\in(1,\overline{\tau})

the ratio between the downstream and upstream densities,
with
\overline{\tau}\coloneqq\frac{\overline{\rho}}{\rho_{\infty}}
.
Using the polytropic pressure law with
\gamma\geq 1
, we have

(A.2)

\displaystyle w

\displaystyle=\frac{v}{u}=\frac{\big(2h(\tau)\big)^{1/2}\big(M_{\infty}^{2}(1-\tau^{-2})-2h(\tau)\big)^{1/2}}{M_{\infty}^{2}(1+\tau^{-1})-2h(\tau)}\,.

From
[
10
, Lemma 7.3.2]
, there exist both a unique detachment
angle
\theta_{\rm stdy}^{\rm d}\in(0,\frac{\pi}{2})
and a unique sonic
angle
\theta_{\rm stdy}^{\rm s}\in(0,\theta_{\rm stdy}^{\rm d})
,
which are characterized by

\displaystyle w^{\rm d}\coloneqq\tan{\theta_{\rm stdy}^{\rm d}}=\sup{\{w(\tau)\,:\,\tau\in(1,\overline{\tau})\}}\,,\qquad\,\,w^{\rm s}\coloneqq\tan{\theta_{\rm stdy}^{\rm s}}=\frac{v}{u}\quad\text{when $u^{2}+v^{2}=\rho^{\gamma-1}$}\,.

By differentiation of (
A.2
) with respect
to
\tau\in(1,\overline{\tau})
, it follows that
w^{\rm d}=w(\tau_{\rm d})
,
where
\tau_{\rm d}\in(1,\overline{\tau})
satisfies

(A.3)

\displaystyle M_{\infty}^{2}\big(2h(\tau_{\rm d})+(\tau_{\rm d}^{2}-1)h^{\prime}(\tau_{\rm d})\big)-2h(\tau_{\rm d})\big(2h(\tau_{\rm d})+\tau_{\rm d}(\tau_{\rm d}+1)h^{\prime}(\tau_{\rm d})\big)=0\,.

Similarly, by routine calculation,
it follows that
w^{\rm s}=w(\tau_{\rm s})
,
where
\tau_{\rm s}\in(1,\overline{\tau})
satisfies

(A.4)

\displaystyle(\gamma+1)h(\tau_{\rm s})=M_{\infty}^{2}-1\,.

For the polytropic gas,
it is clear that the detachment angle and sonic angle

\theta_{\rm stdy}^{\rm d}
and
\theta_{\rm stdy}^{\rm s}
depend only on
\gamma
and
M_{\infty}
.
We also observe the following useful identities:

(A.5)

\displaystyle\tau\,h^{\prime}(\tau)=(\gamma-1)h(\tau)+1\,,\quad\frac{h^{\prime\prime}(\tau)}{h^{\prime}(\tau)}=\frac{\gamma-2}{\tau}\qquad\;\text{for all $\tau\in(0,\infty)$}\,.

2.

For the detachment angle, we combine (
A.2
)
with (
A.3
) to obtain a parametric description of
the detachment angle and the upstream Mach number in terms of
\tau_{\text{d}}
:

(A.6)

w^{\rm d}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\big(\tau_{\rm d}(\tau_{\rm d}^{2}-1)h^{\prime}-2h\big)^{1/2}\big((\tau_{\rm d}^{2}-1)h^{\prime}+2h\big)^{1/2}}{\tau_{\rm d}(\tau_{\rm d}+1)h^{\prime}+h}\,,\qquad M_{\infty}^{2}=\frac{2h\big(2h+\tau_{\rm d}(\tau_{\rm d}+1)h^{\prime}\big)}{2h+(\tau_{\rm d}^{2}-1)h^{\prime}}\,,

where
h,h^{\prime},
and
h^{\prime\prime}
are evaluated at
\tau=\tau_{\rm d}
.
A direct calculation shows that the signs of
(w^{\rm d})_{\tau_{\text{d}}}

and
(M_{\infty}^{2})_{\tau_{\text{d}}}
are equal to the sign of the quantity:

\displaystyle f_{\rm d}(\tau_{\text{d}})\coloneqq 2h^{2}\big(3+(\tau_{\rm d}+1)\frac{h^{\prime\prime}}{h^{\prime}}\big)+(3\tau_{\rm d}-5)(\tau_{\rm d}+1)hh^{\prime}+\tau_{\rm d}(\tau_{\rm d}+1)(\tau_{\rm d}^{2}-1)(h^{\prime})^{2}\,.

Applying (
A.5
) evaluated at
\tau=\tau_{\rm d}
,
along with
\gamma\geq 1
,
\tau_{\rm d}>1
, and
h>0
, we obtain

\displaystyle f_{\rm d}(\tau_{\text{d}})

\displaystyle=\big(3(\tau_{\rm d}-\tau_{\rm d}^{-1})+\big(3(\gamma-1)(\tau_{\rm d}-\tau_{\rm d}^{-1})+2(2-\tau_{\rm d}^{-1})\big)h\big)h+(1+\tau_{\rm d}^{-1})\big(\tau_{\rm d}^{2}(\tau_{\rm d}^{2}-1)(h^{\prime})^{2}-2h\big)

\displaystyle>(1+\tau_{\rm d}^{-1})\tilde{f}_{\rm d}(\tau_{\text{d}})\,,

where
\tilde{f}_{\rm d}(\tau_{\text{d}})\coloneqq\tau_{\rm d}^{2}(\tau_{\rm d}^{2}-1)(h^{\prime})^{2}-2h
.
We note that
\tilde{f}_{\rm d}(1)=0
and

\tilde{f}_{\rm d}^{\prime}(\tau)=2h^{\prime}(\tau)\big((\gamma-1)\tau(\tau^{2}-1)h^{\prime}(\tau)+\tau^{\gamma+1}-1\big)>0\qquad\,\,\mbox{for any $\tau>1$}\,.

It follows that
\tilde{f}_{\rm d}(\tau_{\text{d}})>0
and
f_{\rm d}(\tau_{\text{d}})>0

so that
(w^{\rm d})_{\tau_{\rm d}}>0
and
(M_{\infty}^{2})_{\tau_{\rm d}}>0

for all
\tau_{\text{d}}>1
.
Using the chain rule, we conclude that
w^{\rm d}
and

\theta^{\rm d}_{\rm stdy}

are strictly increasing with respect to
M_{\infty}>1
.

3.

For the sonic angle, we combine (
A.2
)
with (
A.4
) to give a parametric description
of the sonic angle and upstream Mach number in terms of
\tau_{\rm s}
:

(A.7)

w^{\rm s}=\frac{\big(2h\big)^{1/2}\big((\tau_{\rm s}^{2}-1)\tau_{\rm s}^{\gamma-1}-2h\big)^{1/2}}{(\tau_{\rm s}+1)\tau_{\rm s}^{\gamma-1}+2h}\,,\qquad M_{\infty}^{2}=1+(\gamma+1)h\,,

where
h
and
h^{\prime}
are evaluated at
\tau=\tau_{\rm s}
.
It is clear that
(M_{\infty}^{2})_{\tau_{\rm s}}>0
since
h^{\prime}>0
,
whilst a direct calculation shows that the sign of
(w^{\rm s})_{\tau_{\rm s}}
is equal to the sign of the quantity

\displaystyle f_{\rm s}(\tau_{\rm s})

\displaystyle\coloneqq 2h\big(1+(\gamma-1)h\big)\big(1+(\gamma+1)h\big)+(\tau_{\rm s}+1)\big((\tau_{\rm s}-1)\tau_{\rm s}^{\gamma-1}-2h\big)h^{\prime}\,.

Applying (
A.5
) evaluated at
\tau=\tau_{\rm s}
,
along with
\gamma\geq 1
,
\tau_{\rm s}>1
, and
h^{\prime}(\tau_{\rm s})=\tau_{\rm s}^{\gamma-2}>0
,
we obtain

\displaystyle f_{\rm s}(\tau_{\rm s})=\big(2(\gamma+1)\tau_{\rm s}h^{2}+\tau_{\rm s}(\tau_{\rm s}^{2}-1)h^{\prime}-2h\big)h^{\prime}>\tilde{f}_{\rm s}(\tau_{\rm s})h^{\prime}\,,

where
\tilde{f}_{\rm s}(\tau_{\rm s})\coloneqq\tau_{\rm s}(\tau_{\rm s}^{2}-1)h^{\prime}-2h
.
Notice that
\tilde{f}_{\rm s}(1)=0
and

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{\rm s}^{\prime}(\tau)=(\gamma+1)(\tau^{2}-1)h^{\prime}(\tau)>0\qquad\mbox{for any $\tau>1$}.

Then it follows that
\tilde{f}_{\rm s}(\tau_{\rm s})>0
and
f_{\rm s}(\tau_{\rm s})>0

so that
(w^{\rm s})_{\tau_{\rm s}}>0
.
We conclude via the chain rule that
w^{\rm s}
and hence
\theta_{\rm stdy}^{\rm s}

are strictly increasing with respect to
M_{\infty}>1
.

4.

The limiting values stated in the lemma can be checked directly
from (
A.6
)–(
A.7
),
now that the monotonicity of
(w^{\rm d},M_{\infty}^{2})
with respect to
\tau_{\rm d}

in (
A.6
) and the monotonicity of
(w^{\rm s},M_{\infty}^{2})

with respect to
\tau_{\rm s}
in (
A.7
) have been verified.

\square

A.2.
Monotonicity properties with respect to the incident angles

In Lemma
2.3
, we have shown that the pseudo-Mach number of state
(2)

at the reflection point
P_{0}^{1}
is a strictly decreasing function of
\theta_{1}\in(0,\theta^{\rm cr})
.
Subsequently, in Proposition
2.6
, we have used
Lemmas
2.3
and
2.5

to prove the existence of the unique detachment angle
\theta^{\rm d}

and sonic angle
\theta^{\rm s}
.
In Remark
2.1
(
i
), we have stated the relation
between
\theta^{\rm d},\,\theta^{\rm s}
, and
\theta^{\rm cr}
, which depends on the choice of
(\gamma,v_{2})
.
The following lemma provides the proof of this statement.

Lemma A.1
.

For any
\gamma>1,
there exist constants
v_{2}^{\rm d}
and
v_{2}^{\rm s},

with
v_{\min}<v_{2}^{\rm s}<v_{2}^{\rm d}<0,
uniquely determined by
\gamma

such that

(A.8)

\displaystyle\sgn\big(\hat{\theta}_{25}(\theta^{\rm cr};v_{2})-\theta_{\rm stdy}^{\rm d}(1,|D\varphi_{2}(P_{0,{\rm cr}}^{1})|)\big)

\displaystyle=\sgn(v_{2}-v_{2}^{\rm d})\,,

(A.9)

\displaystyle\sgn\big(\hat{\theta}_{25}(\theta^{\rm cr};v_{2})-\theta_{\rm stdy}^{\rm s}(1,|D\varphi_{2}(P_{0,{\rm cr}}^{1})|)\big)

\displaystyle=\sgn(v_{2}-v_{2}^{\rm s})\,,
