where

P_{0,{\rm cr}}^{1}\coloneqq P_{0}^{1}|_{\theta_{1}=\theta^{\rm cr}}
,
and
we define
\theta_{\rm stdy}^{\rm d}(1,M_{\infty})\equiv\theta_{\rm stdy}^{\rm s}(1,M_{\infty})\coloneqq 0

for any
0\leq M_{\infty}\leq 1
.
Furthermore, when
\gamma=1,

we define
v_{\min}=v_{2}^{\rm s}=v_{2}^{\rm d}=-\infty
.

Proof.
We divide the proof into two steps.

1.
For
\gamma>1
and
v_{2}\in(v_{\min},0)
, a direct calculation by using (
2.15
),
(
2.18
), and Lemma
2.3
gives

(A.10)

\displaystyle M_{2,\min}\coloneqq\inf_{\theta_{1}\in(0,\theta^{\rm cr})}|D\varphi_{2}(P_{0}^{1})|=|D\varphi_{2}(P_{0,{\rm cr}}^{1})|=\frac{v_{2}v_{\min}}{\sqrt{v_{\min}^{2}-v_{2}^{2}}}\,,

\displaystyle\hat{\theta}_{25}(\theta^{\rm cr};v_{2})=\arcsin{(\frac{-v_{2}}{M_{2,\min}})}=\arccos{(|{\frac{v_{2}}{v_{\min}}}|)}>0\,.

Then
\hat{\theta}_{25}(\theta^{\rm cr};v_{2})
is strictly increasing with respect to
v_{2}\in(v_{\min},0)
.
We also calculate

(A.11)

\lim_{v_{2}\to v_{\min}^{+}}M_{2,\min}=\infty\,,\quad\lim_{v_{2}\to 0^{-}}M_{2,\min}=0\,,\quad\frac{{\rm d}M_{2,\min}^{2}}{{\rm d}v_{2}}=\frac{2v_{\min}^{4}v_{2}}{(v_{\min}^{2}-v_{2}^{2})^{2}}<0\,,

so that
M_{2,\min}
is strictly decreasing with respect to
v_{2}\in(v_{\min},0)
.
Also observe that

M_{2,\min}>1

if and only if

v_{2}\in(v_{\min},v_{\rm mid})
,
for
v_{\rm mid}\coloneqq\frac{v_{\min}}{\sqrt{v_{\min}^{2}+1}}
.
Then, by Lemma
2.5
,

\theta_{\rm stdy}^{\rm d}(1,M_{2,\min})
is strictly decreasing with respect to

v_{2}\in(v_{\min},v_{\rm mid})
.
In particular, by (
A.10
)–(
A.11
)
and
[
10
, Lemma 7.3.3]
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (\hat{\theta}_{25}(\theta^{\rm cr};v_{2}),\theta_{\rm stdy}^{\rm d}(1,M_{2,\min}))&\to(\arctan{(|v_{\min}|)},0)\qquad&&\text{as}\;\;v_{2}&&\to v_{\rm mid}^{-}\,,\\
(\hat{\theta}_{25}(\theta^{\rm cr};v_{2}),\theta_{\rm stdy}^{\rm d}(1,M_{2,\min}))&\to(0,\tfrac{\pi}{2})\quad&&\text{as}\;\;v_{2}&&\to v_{\min}^{+}\,.\end{aligned}

Then we conclude from the monotonicity properties of

(\hat{\theta}_{25}(\theta^{\rm cr};v_{2})\,,\theta_{\rm stdy}^{\rm d}(1,M_{2,\min}))

with respect to
v_{2}\in(v_{\min},v_{\rm mid})
that there exists a unique value

v_{2}^{\rm d}\in(v_{\min},v_{\rm mid})
, depending only on
\gamma
,
such that (
A.8
) holds.

The proof of (
A.9
) follows via a similar argument
by using the fact that
\theta_{\rm stdy}^{\rm s}\in(0,\theta_{\rm stdy}^{\rm d})
and

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \theta_{\rm stdy}^{\rm s}(1,M_{2,\min})&\to 0\qquad&&\text{as}\;\;v_{2}&&\to v_{\rm mid}^{-}\,,\\
\theta_{\rm stdy}^{\rm s}(1,M_{2,\min})&\to\arctan\big(\tfrac{2}{\gamma-1}\big)^{1/2}\qquad&&\text{as}\;\;v_{2}&&\to v_{\min}^{+}\,.\end{aligned}

2.
For
\gamma=1
, from (
2.15
), we see that

v_{\min}=-\infty
and
\theta^{\rm cr}=\tfrac{\pi}{2}
.
Taking the limit:
v_{\min}\to-\infty
in Step
1
above, we obtain that

M_{2,\min}=|v_{2}|
and

\hat{\theta}_{25}(\tfrac{\pi}{2};v_{2})=\tfrac{\pi}{2}
.
From Lemma
2.5
, we know that the sonic and detachment angles
for 2-D steady potential flow
satisfy
0<\theta^{\rm s}(1,M_{\infty})<\theta^{\rm d}(1,M_{\infty})<\tfrac{\pi}{2}
for any
M_{\infty}>1
,
which implies that (
A.8
)–(
A.9
)
hold with
v_{2}^{\rm d}=v_{2}^{\rm s}=-\infty
.
\square

Lemma
2.3
is also useful to determine the monotonicity of other quantities
related to the four-shock interaction problem.
In the following lemma, we show that the reflected shock angles
\theta_{25}
and
\theta_{26}

are monotonic with respect to the incident shock angles
\theta_{1}
and
\theta_{2}
respectively
and, subsequently, that certain intersection points of the reflected shocks are also monotonic functions
of the incident angles.

Lemma A.2
.

Fix
\gamma\geq 1
and
v_{2}\in(v_{\min},0)
.

(i)

For any incident angle
\theta_{1}\in[0,\theta^{\rm d}),

let the reflected shock angle
\theta_{25}\in(\tfrac{\pi}{2},\pi]
be given
by (
2.34
).
Then
\theta_{25}
is a continuous, strictly decreasing function of
\theta_{1},

and the map
:

\theta_{1}\mapsto\theta_{25}
is
C^{\infty}
–smooth on
\theta_{1}\in[0,\theta^{\rm d})
.

(ii)

For the right-most intersection
P_{2}=(\xi^{P_{2}},\eta^{P_{2}})
of
\partial B_{c_{5}}(O_{5})
and
S_{25}

as given
in
Definition
2.9
,

and for the
\eta
–intercept of
S_{25}
which is denoted here by
a_{25}
:

\eta^{P_{2}}
is strictly decreasing with respect to
\theta_{1}\in(0,\theta^{\rm s})
,

and
a_{25}
is strictly increasing with
\theta_{1}\in(0,\theta^{\rm d})
.

Proof.

For (i), the continuity and smoothness of the map:
\theta_{1}\mapsto\theta_{25}

follows due to Proposition
2.6
and (
2.34
),
whilst the strict monotonicity follows directly from Lemma
2.3
and
[
2
, Lemma 2.17]
.
Indeed, suppose that
\theta_{1},\tilde{\theta}_{1}\in[0,\theta^{\rm d})
are such
that
\theta_{25}(\theta_{1})=\theta_{25}(\tilde{\theta}_{1})
.
Then, by
[
2
, Lemma 2.17]
, the uniform state
(5)
is uniquely determined
by
(v_{2},\gamma,\theta_{25})
so that the location of the reflected shock
S_{25}
must
coincide for both
\theta_{1}
and
\tilde{\theta}_{1}
.
In particular, for the pseudo-Mach number of state
(2)
at the reflection point
P_{0}^{1}
,
as defined by (
2.18
), we see that
M_{2}^{(\theta_{1})}=M_{2}^{(\tilde{\theta}_{1})}

so that
\theta_{1}=\tilde{\theta}_{1}
by Lemma
2.3
.

We have shown that the map:
\theta_{1}\mapsto\theta_{25}
is continuous and injective,
and therefore strictly monotonic.
In particular, by property (
i
) of
Proposition
2.6
and (
2.34
),

\theta_{25}\to\pi^{-}
as
\theta_{1}\to 0^{+}
;
whilst, by property (
ii
) of
Proposition
2.6
and (
2.34
),

\theta_{25}<\pi
for any
\theta_{1}\in(0,\theta^{\rm d}]
.
It follows that the map:
\theta_{1}\mapsto\theta_{25}
is monotonic decreasing.

For (ii), the first result follows directly from part (i) combined with
[
2
, Lemma 2.22]
,
whilst the second result follows directly from part (i) combined with
[
2
, Eqs. (2.4.14) and (2.4.42)]
.

\square

Finally, we give the proof that the intersection point
P_{I}
of the reflected shocks
S_{25}
and
S_{26}

can be extended to a
C(\overline{\Omega})
–function.

Lemma A.3
.

For
{\bm{\theta}}\in\overline{\Theta}\setminus\{\bm{0}\},
denote by
P_{I}=(\xi^{P_{I}},\eta^{P_{I}})
the unique
intersection point of the reflected shocks
S_{25}
and
S_{26},
as given by (
4.9
).
Then

(A.12)

\displaystyle\lim_{{\bm{\theta}}\to\bm{0},\,{\bm{\theta}}\in\overline{\Theta}\setminus\bm{0}}(\xi^{P_{I}},\eta^{P_{I}})=(0,\eta_{0})\,,

where
\eta_{0}
is given by (
2.23
).

Proof.

By Proposition
2.6
and
Lemma
A.2
(
i
),
limit (
A.12
) is equivalent to the limit:

\displaystyle\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\theta_{25},\theta_{26})\to(\pi,0)\\
(\theta_{25},\theta_{26})\in\overline{\Theta_{\rm refl}}\setminus\{(\pi,0)\}\end{subarray}}(\xi^{P_{I}},\eta^{P_{I}})=(0,\eta_{0})\,,

where
\Theta_{\rm refl}\coloneqq\{(\theta_{25},\theta_{26}):{\bm{\theta}}\in\Theta\}
.
The proof of the above limit consists of three steps.

1.
We first consider the unilateral normal reflection case:

{\bm{\theta}}\in\big(\{0\}\times(0,\theta^{\rm d})\big)\cup\big((0,\theta^{\rm d})\times\{0\}\big)
.

Without loss of generality, we restrict our attention to the case:
{\bm{\theta}}\in(0,\theta^{\rm d})\times\{0\}
,
and denote
(v_{\infty},\beta)\coloneqq(-v_{2},\pi-\theta_{25})
, where
\theta_{25}
depends only on
(v_{2},\gamma,\theta_{1})
.
We consider the Prandtl-Meyer reflection configuration associated with
parameters
(v_{\infty},\beta)
, as defined in
[
2
]
, and reflect this configuration in the vertical axis.
By
[
2
, Lemma 2.17]
, the location of the reflected shock
S_{25}
is uniquely
determined by
(v_{2},\gamma,\theta_{25})
, and thus coincides with the Prandtl-Meyer oblique shock
S_{\mathcal{O}}
.
Similarly, the (normal) reflected shock
S_{26}=S_{0}
coincides with the Prandtl-Meyer normal shock
S_{\mathcal{N}}
,
so that the intersection point
P_{I}
of
S_{25}
and
S_{26}
,
as given by (
4.9
), coincides with the intersection point of
S_{\mathcal{O}}
and
S_{\mathcal{N}}
, as given by
[
2
, Eq. (4.1.33)]
.
That is,

\displaystyle(\xi^{P_{I}}\,,\eta^{P_{I}})=\big(\frac{a_{25}-\eta_{0}}{\tan{\theta_{25}}}\,,\eta_{0}\big)\,,

where
a_{25}\coloneqq-\xi^{P_{0}^{1}}\tan{\theta_{25}}
and
\eta_{0}
are the
\eta
–intercepts of
S_{25}
and the normal reflection
S_{0}
, respectively, as given in §
2
.
By Proposition
2.6
(
i
) and Lemma
A.2
(
ii
),

a_{25}\to\eta_{0}
as
\theta_{25}\to\pi^{-}
. Then
we may apply L’Hôpital’s rule to give

\displaystyle\lim_{\theta_{25}\to\pi^{-}}\xi^{P_{I}}=-\lim_{\theta_{25}\to\pi^{-}}\frac{{\rm d}a_{25}}{{\rm d}\theta_{25}}\,.

From
[
2
, Eq. (2.4.14)]
,

a_{25}=v_{2}-q_{2}\sec{\theta_{25}}
,
for

q_{2}\coloneqq{\rm dist}(S_{25},O_{2})
.
By taking the derivative with respect to

\theta_{25}
,
we have

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}a_{25}}{{\rm d}\theta_{25}}=-\sec{\theta_{25}}\frac{{\rm d}q_{2}}{{\rm d}\theta_{25}}-q_{2}\sec{\theta_{25}}\tan{\theta_{25}}\,.

Using
[
10
, Lemma 6.1.2]
and (
2.23
),

\displaystyle{1<\lim_{\theta_{25}\to\pi^{-}}q_{2}=\eta_{0}-v_{2}<\infty}

so that

\displaystyle\lim_{\theta_{25}\to\pi^{-}}\frac{{\rm d}a_{25}}{{\rm d}\theta_{25}}=\lim_{\theta_{25}\to\pi^{-}}\frac{{\rm d}q_{2}}{{\rm d}\theta_{25}}\,.

By
[
2
, Eq. (2.4.12)]
, we have a relation between the pseudo-Mach numbers

(M_{2},M_{5})\coloneqq(\tfrac{q_{2}}{c_{2}},\tfrac{q_{5}}{c_{5}})

for
q_{5}\coloneqq{\rm dist}(S_{25},O_{5})
and the given parameters

(\gamma,v_{2},\theta_{25})
,
expressed as

\displaystyle M_{2}^{\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma+1}}(M_{2}^{\frac{2}{\gamma+1}}-M_{5}^{\frac{2}{\gamma+1}})=v_{2}\sec{\theta_{25}}\,.

Taking the derivative:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\lim_{\theta_{25}\to\pi^{-}}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}\theta_{25}}\Big(M_{2}^{\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma+1}}(M_{2}^{\frac{2}{\gamma+1}}-M_{5}^{\frac{2}{\gamma+1}})\Big)

\displaystyle=\lim_{\theta_{25}\to\pi^{-}}\frac{1}{\gamma+1}\Big((\gamma-1)M_{2}^{-1}v_{2}\sec{\theta_{25}}+2M_{2}^{\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma+1}}\big(M_{2}^{\frac{1-\gamma}{1+\gamma}}-M_{5}^{\frac{1-\gamma}{1+\gamma}}\frac{{\rm d}M_{5}}{{\rm d}M_{2}}\big)\Big)\frac{{\rm d}M_{2}}{{\rm d}\theta_{25}}\,.

By
[
2
, Eqs. (2.4.9)–(2.4.10)]
, we see that

\frac{{\rm d}M_{5}}{{\rm d}M_{2}}<0
for all
M_{2}\in(0,\infty)\setminus\{1\}
.
Therefore, the bracketed term above
is uniformly positive for

\theta_{25}\in(\tfrac{\pi}{2},\pi)

so that

\displaystyle\lim_{\theta_{25}\to\pi^{-}}\frac{{\rm d}q_{2}}{{\rm d}\theta_{25}}=\lim_{\theta_{25}\to\pi^{-}}\frac{{\rm d}M_{2}}{{\rm d}\theta_{25}}=0\,.

2.

We now consider the general case
{\bm{\theta}}\in\overline{\Theta}\setminus\{\bm{0}\}
.
By direct calculation, we have

\displaystyle\xi^{P_{I}}=\frac{a_{25}-a_{26}}{\tan{\theta_{26}}-\tan{\theta_{25}}}\,,

where
a_{2j}>0
represents the
\eta
–intercept of
S_{2j}
for
j=5,6
.
Note that
a_{25}
depends only on
(v_{2},\gamma,\theta_{25})
,
whilst
a_{26}
depends only on
(v_{2},\gamma,\theta_{26})
.
In particular, from Step
1
,

(A.13)

0=\lim_{\theta_{25}\to\pi^{-}}\frac{a_{25}-\eta_{0}}{\tan{\theta_{25}}}=\lim_{\theta_{26}\to 0^{+}}\frac{a_{26}-\eta_{0}}{\tan{\theta_{26}}}\,.

Let
\left\{{\bm{\theta}}^{(k)}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subseteq\overline{\Theta}\setminus\{\bm{0}\}
be any sequence
of parameters with
{\bm{\theta}}^{(k)}\to\bm{0}
as
k\to\infty
.
By moving to a subsequence, we may assume any of the following cases:
(i)
\theta_{1}^{(k)}=0
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
; (ii)
\theta_{2}^{(k)}=0
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
;
(iii) both
\theta_{1}^{(k)}>0
and
\theta_{2}^{(k)}>0
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
Denote by
\xi^{P_{I}}_{(k)}
the
\xi
–coordinates of the intersection points
P_{I}
associated
with parameters
{\bm{\theta}}^{(k)}
.
In cases (i) and (ii), it is clear from the previous step that
\xi^{P_{I}}_{(k)}\to 0
as
k\to\infty
,
so we focus on case (iii).
Using the triangle inequality, the fact that
\tan\theta_{25}
and
\tan\theta_{26}
have opposite signs,
and (
A.13
), we obtain

\displaystyle|\xi^{P_{I}}_{(k)}|=\char 12\relax{\frac{a_{25}^{(k)}-a_{26}^{(k)}}{\tan{\theta_{26}^{(k)}-\tan{\theta_{25}^{(k)}}}}}\leq\char 12\relax{\frac{a_{25}^{(k)}-\eta_{0}}{\tan{\theta_{25}^{(k)}}}}+\char 12\relax{\frac{a_{26}^{(k)}-\eta_{0}}{\tan{\theta_{26}^{(k)}}}}\to 0\qquad\,\,\text{as $k\to\infty$}\,.

3.

Finally, we show that
\eta^{P_{I}}\to\eta_{0}
as
{\bm{\theta}}\to\bm{0}

with
{\bm{\theta}}\in\overline{\Theta}\setminus\{\bm{0}\}
.
Indeed, it is clear from (
A.13
)
that
a_{25}\to\eta_{0}
as
\theta_{1}\to 0^{+}
, and
a_{26}\to\eta_{0}
as
\theta_{2}\to 0^{+}
.
Furthermore, it follows from Step
1
of the proof of Lemma
3.3
that

\min\{a_{25},a_{26}\}\leq\eta^{P_{I}}\leq\max\{a_{25},a_{26}\}
.

\square

Appendix B
Some Known Results Needed for the Proofs

In this appendix, we present some known results that are used in Sections 3–5
above.

B.1.
Well-posedness of the iteration boundary value problem

For a constant
h>0
and a function
f_{\rm bd}:[0,h]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{+}
,
fix a bounded domain
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{2}
as

(B.1)

\Omega\coloneqq\big\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\,:\,0<x_{1}<h,\,0<x_{2}<f_{\rm bd}(x_{1})\big\}\,,

where
f_{\rm bd}
satisfies that, for constants
t_{0}\geq 0
,
t_{h},\,t_{1},\,t_{2},\,t_{3},\,M\in(0,\infty)
,
and
\alpha\in(0,1)
,

(B.2)

\displaystyle f_{\rm bd}\in C^{1}([0,h])\,,\quad f_{\rm bd}(0)=t_{0}\,,\quad f_{\rm bd}(h)=t_{h}\,,

\displaystyle f_{\rm bd}(x_{1})\geq\min\{t_{0}+t_{1}x_{1},\,t_{2},\,t_{h}-t_{3}(x_{1}-h)\}\qquad\,\,\text{for all $x_{1}\in(0,h)$}\,,

\displaystyle\|f_{\rm bd}\|_{2,\alpha,(0,h)}^{(-1-\alpha),\{0,h\}}\leq M\,.

The boundary,
\partial\Omega\coloneqq\cup_{k=0}^{3}(\Gamma_{k}\cup\{P_{k+1}\})
, with vertices and segments:

(B.3)

\displaystyle P_{1}=(h,0)\,,\quad P_{2}=(h,f_{\rm bd}(h))\,,\quad P_{3}=(0,f_{\rm bd}(0))\,,\quad P_{4}=(0,0)\,,

\displaystyle\overline{\Gamma_{0}}=\partial\Omega\cap\{x_{1}=0\}\,,\quad\overline{\Gamma_{1}}=\partial\Omega\cap\{x_{2}=f_{\rm bd}(x_{1})\}\,,

\displaystyle\overline{\Gamma_{2}}=\partial\Omega\cap\{x_{1}=h\}\,,\quad\overline{\Gamma_{3}}=\partial\Omega\cap\{x_{2}=0\}\,,

and
\Gamma_{k}
,
k=0,1,2,3,
are the relative interiors of the segments defined above.

Let
g_{\rm so}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\{\frac{h}{3}<x_{1}<\frac{2h}{3}\})
be a piecewise smooth function
defined in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
such that

(B.4)

\displaystyle\|g_{\rm so}\|_{C^{3}(\overline{\Omega\setminus\{\frac{h}{3}<x_{1}<\frac{2h}{3}\}})}\leq C_{\rm so}\,,

\displaystyle g_{\rm so}(\cdot,x_{2})\;\;\;\text{is linear on $x_{1}$ in}\;\{x_{1}\leq\frac{h}{4}\}\cup\{x_{1}\geq\frac{3h}{4}\}\,,

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{2}}g_{\rm so}=0\quad\text{on $\Gamma_{3}$}\,.

We consider the following nonlinear problem:

(B.5)

\left\{\begin{aligned} \,&\!\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\tilde{A}_{ij}(Du,\bm{x})D_{ij}u+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\tilde{A}_{i}(Du,\bm{x})D_{i}u=0\quad&&\text{in}\;\Omega\,,\\
&\tilde{B}(Du,u,\bm{x})=0\quad&&\text{on}\;\Gamma_{1}\,,\\
&u=g_{\rm so}(\bm{x})\quad&&\text{on}\;\Gamma_{2}\cup\Gamma_{0}\,,\\
&\bm{b}^{(\rm w)}(\bm{x})\cdot Du=0\quad&&\text{on}\;\Gamma_{3}\,.\end{aligned}\right.

For constants
\lambda\in(0,1)
,
M<\infty
,
\alpha\in(0,1)
,
\beta\in[\frac{1}{2},1)
,

\delta\in(0,1)
,
\sigma\in(0,1)
, and
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{h}{10})
,
assume that the following conditions are satisfied:

(i)

For any
\bm{x}\in\Omega
and
\bm{p},\bm{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,

\lambda\,{\rm dist}(\bm{x},\Gamma_{2}\cup\Gamma_{0})|\bm{\mu}|^{2}\leq\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\tilde{A}_{ij}(\bm{p},\bm{x})\mu_{i}\mu_{j}\leq\lambda^{-1}|\bm{\mu}|^{2}
.
Moreover, for any
\bm{x}\in\Omega\setminus\{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\leq x_{1}\leq h-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\}
,

\lambda|\bm{\mu}|^{2}\leq\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\frac{\tilde{A}_{ij}(\bm{p},\bm{x})\mu_{i}\mu_{j}}{\left(\min\{x_{1},h-x_{1},\delta\}\right)^{2-\frac{i+j}{2}}}\leq\lambda^{-1}|\bm{\mu}|^{2}\,.

(ii)

(\tilde{A}_{ij},\tilde{A})(\bm{p},\bm{x})
are independent of
\bm{p}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
on
\Omega\cap\{\varepsilon\leq x_{1}\leq h-\varepsilon\}
with

\|\tilde{A}_{ij}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega\cap\{\varepsilon\leq x_{1}\leq h-\varepsilon\})}\leq\lambda^{-1}\,,\qquad\|(\tilde{A}_{ij},\tilde{A})\|_{1,\alpha,\Omega\cap\{\varepsilon\leq x_{1}\leq h-\varepsilon\}}\leq M\,.

(iii)

For any
\bm{p}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,

\|(\tilde{A}_{ij},\tilde{A}_{i})(\bm{p},\cdot)\|_{C^{\beta}(\overline{\Omega\setminus\{2\varepsilon\leq x_{1}\leq h-2\varepsilon\}})}+\|D_{\bm{p}}(\tilde{A}_{ij},\tilde{A}_{i})(\bm{p},\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega\setminus\{2\varepsilon\leq x_{1}\leq h-2\varepsilon\})}\leq M\,.

(iv)

(\tilde{A}_{ij},\tilde{A}_{i})\in C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\overline{\Omega}\setminus(\overline{\Gamma}_{2}\cup\overline{\Gamma_{0}})))
and, for any
s\in(0,\frac{h}{4})
,

\|(\tilde{A}_{ij},\tilde{A}_{i})\|_{1,\alpha,\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\overline{\Omega}\cap\{s\leq x_{1}\leq h-s\})}\leq M\Big(\frac{h}{s}\Big)^{M}.

(v)

For each
(\bm{p},\bm{x})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\overline{\Omega}\setminus\{\varepsilon<x_{1}<h-\varepsilon\})

and
i,j=1,2
, define

\hat{\bm{p}}\coloneqq\bm{p}-Dg_{\rm so}(\bm{x})\,,\qquad(\tilde{a}_{ij},\tilde{a}_{i})(\hat{\bm{p}},\bm{x})\coloneqq(\tilde{A}_{ij},\tilde{A}_{i})(\hat{\bm{p}}+Dg_{\rm so}(\bm{x}),\bm{x})\,.

For any
(\bm{p},(x_{1},0))\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\Gamma_{3}\setminus\{\varepsilon\leq x_{1}\leq h-\varepsilon\})
,

(\tilde{a}_{11},\tilde{a}_{22},\tilde{a}_{1})((\hat{p}_{1},-\hat{p}_{2}),(x_{1},0))=(\tilde{a}_{11},\tilde{a}_{22},\tilde{a}_{1})((\hat{p}_{1},\hat{p}_{2}),(x_{1},0))\,.

For any
(\bm{p},\bm{x})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\Omega\setminus\{\varepsilon\leq x_{1}\leq h-\varepsilon\})

and
i=1,2
,

|\tilde{a}_{ii}(\hat{\bm{p}},\bm{x})-\tilde{a}_{ii}(Dg_{\rm so}(\bm{x}_{\rm e}),\bm{x}_{\rm e})|\leq M|\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{\rm e}|^{\beta}\,,\qquad(\tilde{A}_{12},\tilde{A}_{21})(\bm{p},\bm{x}_{\rm e})=0\,,

either for point
\bm{x}_{\rm e}\coloneqq(0,x_{2})
or point
\bm{x}_{\rm e}\coloneqq(h,x_{2})
.

(vi)

For any
\bm{p}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and

\bm{x}\in\Omega\setminus\{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\leq x_{1}\leq h-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\}
,

\,\tilde{A}_{1}(\bm{p},\bm{x})\leq-\lambda
.

(vii)

The nonlinear boundary condition
\eqref{vonN-Sec4-5-2:NonlinearProblem}_{2}
satisfies the following conditions:

(
vii
–a)

For any
(\bm{p},z,\bm{x})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}\times\Gamma_{1}
,

D_{\bm{p}}\tilde{B}(\bm{p},z,\bm{x})\cdot\bm{\nu}^{(1)}(\bm{x})\geq\lambda
,
where
\bm{\nu}^{(1)}
is the unit normal vector on
\Gamma_{1}
pointing to
\Omega
.

(
vii
–b)

For any
(\bm{p},z)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}
and
k=1,2,3
,

\displaystyle\|\tilde{B}(Dg_{\rm so},g_{\rm so},\cdot)\|_{C^{3}(\overline{\Omega}\setminus\{\frac{h}{3}\leq x_{1}\leq\frac{2h}{3}\})}+\|D^{k}_{(\bm{p},z)}\tilde{B}(\bm{p},z,\cdot)\|_{C^{3}(\overline{\Omega})}\leq M\,,

\displaystyle\|D_{\bm{p}}\tilde{B}(\bm{p},z,\cdot)\|_{C^{0}(\overline{\Omega})}\leq\lambda^{-1}\,,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &D_{z}\tilde{B}(\bm{p},z,\bm{x})\leq-\lambda\quad&&\text{for all}\;\bm{x}\in\Gamma_{1}\,,\\
&D_{p_{1}}\tilde{B}(\bm{p},z,\bm{x})\leq-\lambda\quad&&\text{for all}\;\bm{x}\in\Gamma_{1}\setminus\{\varepsilon\leq x_{1}\leq h-\varepsilon\}\,.\end{aligned}

(
vii
–c)

There exist
v\in C^{3}(\overline{\Gamma_{1}})
and a nonhomogeneous linear operator:

L(\bm{p},z,\bm{x})=\bm{b}^{(1)}(\bm{x})\cdot\bm{p}+b^{(1)}_{0}(\bm{x})z+g_{1}(\bm{x})

defined for
\bm{x}\in\Gamma_{1}
and
(\bm{p},z)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}
, satisfying

\|v\|_{C^{3}(\Omega)}+\|(\bm{b}^{(1)},b^{(1)}_{0},g_{1})\|_{C^{3}(\overline{\Gamma_{1}})}\leq M\,,

such that, for any
(\bm{p},z,\bm{x})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}\times\Gamma_{1}
,

\displaystyle\big|\tilde{B}(\bm{p},z,\bm{x})-L(\bm{p},z,\bm{x})\big|\leq\sigma\big(|\bm{p}-Dv(\bm{x})|+|z-v(\bm{x})|\big)\;,

\displaystyle\big|D_{\bm{p}}\tilde{B}(\bm{p},z,\bm{x})-\bm{b}^{(1)}(\bm{x})\big|+\big|D_{z}\tilde{B}(\bm{p},z,\bm{x})-b_{0}^{(1)}(\bm{x})\big|\leq\sigma\,.

(
vii
–d)

Obliqueness requirements: For the interior unit normal vector
\bm{\nu}^{\rm(w)}
on
\Gamma_{3}
to
\Omega
,

\displaystyle\bm{b}^{\rm(w)}\cdot\bm{\nu}^{\rm(w)}\geq\lambda\,,\quad b^{\rm(w)}_{1}\leq 0\qquad\text{on $\Gamma_{3}$}\,,

\displaystyle\bm{b}^{\rm(w)}=(0,1)\;\;\text{on $\Gamma_{3}\setminus\{\varepsilon\leq x_{1}\leq h-\varepsilon\}$}\,,\qquad\|\bm{b}^{\rm(w)}\|_{C^{3}(\overline{\Gamma_{3}})}\leq M\,.

(
vii
–e)

\,\,\tilde{B}(\bm{0},0,\cdot)\equiv 0\quad
on
\Gamma_{1}\setminus\{\varepsilon\leq x_{1}\leq h-\varepsilon\}\,
.

Proposition B.1

(
[
10
, Proposition 4.7.2]
)
.

Fix constants
h,t_{1},t_{2},t_{3}\in(0,\infty)
and
t_{0},t_{h}\in[0,\infty)
.
For constants
\lambda>0,

M<\infty,

\alpha\in(0,1),
and
C_{\rm so}>0,

domain
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{2}
satisfies
conditions (
B.1
)–(
B.4
).
For
\beta\in[\frac{1}{2},1),

\delta\in(0,1),

\sigma\in(0,1),
and
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{h}{10}),

the nonlinear boundary value problem (
B.5
) satisfies
conditions (
i
)–(
vii
).
Then there exist

\alpha_{1}\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
depending only on
\lambda,

and constants
\delta_{0},\sigma\in(0,1)
depending only on
(\lambda,M,C_{\rm so},\alpha,\beta,\varepsilon)
such that,
under further requirement
\delta\in[0,\delta_{0}),

the boundary value problem (
B.5
) has a unique solution

u\in C(\overline{\Omega})\cap C^{1}(\overline{\Omega}\setminus(\overline{\Gamma_{2}}\cup\overline{\Gamma_{0}}))\cap C^{2}(\Omega)
.
Moreover,
u
satisfies

(B.6)

\|u\|_{0,\overline{\Omega}}\leq C\,,\qquad\,|u(\bm{x})-g_{\rm so}(\bm{x})|\leq C\min\{x_{1},h-x_{1}\}\quad\text{in $\Omega$}\,,

where
C>0
depends only on
(\lambda,M,C_{\rm so},\varepsilon)
.
Furthermore,
u\in C(\overline{\Omega})\cap C^{2,\alpha_{1}}(\overline{\Omega}\setminus\overline{\Gamma_{2}}\cup\overline{\Gamma_{0}})
satisfies

(B.7)

\|u\|_{2,\alpha_{1},\overline{\Omega\cap\{s<x_{1}<h-s\}}}\leq C_{s}

for each
s\in(0,\frac{h}{10}),
where
C_{s}>0
depends only
on
(\lambda,M,C_{\rm so},\alpha,\beta,\varepsilon,s)
.

Proposition B.2

(
[
10
, Proposition 4.8.7]
)
.

Fix constants
h,t_{1},t_{2},t_{3}\in(0,\infty),

t_{0}=0,
and
t_{h}\geq 0
.
For constants
\lambda>0,

M<\infty,

\alpha\in(0,1),
and
C_{\rm so}>0,

domain
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{2}
satisfies
conditions (
B.1
)–(
B.4
) with changes
:

\,P_{3}=P_{4}=(0,0)
and
\overline{\Gamma_{0}}=\{(0,0)\}
.
For
\beta\in[\frac{1}{2},1),

\delta\in(0,1),

\sigma\in(0,1),
and
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{h}{10}),

the nonlinear boundary value problem (
B.5
) satisfies
conditions (
ii
)
,
(
iv
)
,

and (
vii
) above,
and
(
i
*),

(
iii
*),

(
v
*),
and
(
vi
*)
below
:

(i*)

For any
\bm{x}\in\Omega
and
\bm{p},\bm{\kappa}=(\kappa_{1}\kappa_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2},

\displaystyle\min\big\{\lambda\,{\rm dist}(\bm{x},\overline{\Gamma_{0}})+\delta,\lambda\,{\rm dist}(x,\Gamma_{2})\big\}|\bm{\kappa}|^{2}\leq\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\tilde{A}_{ij}(\bm{p},\bm{x})\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}\leq\lambda^{-1}|\bm{\kappa}|^{2}\,,

\displaystyle\big\|((\tilde{A}_{ij},\tilde{A}_{i})(Dg_{\rm so},\cdot),D^{m}_{\bm{p}}(\tilde{A}_{ij},\tilde{A}_{i})(\bm{p},\cdot))\big\|^{(-\alpha),\{P_{4}\}}_{1,\alpha,\Omega\cap\{x_{1}<2\varepsilon\}}\leq M\qquad\text{for $m=1,2$}\,.

Moreover, for any
\bm{x}\in\Omega\cap\{h-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}<x_{1}<h\},

\lambda|\bm{\mu}|^{2}\leq\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\frac{\tilde{A}_{ij}(\bm{p},\bm{x})\mu_{i}\mu_{j}}{\left(\min\{h-x_{1},\delta\}\right)^{2-\frac{i+j}{2}}}\leq\lambda^{-1}|\bm{\mu}|^{2}\,.

(iii*)

For any
\bm{p}\in\mathbb{R}^{2},

\|(\tilde{A}_{ij},\tilde{A}_{i})(\bm{p},\cdot)\|_{C^{\beta}(\overline{\Omega\cap\{h-2\varepsilon<x_{1}<h\}})}+\|D_{\bm{p}}(\tilde{A}_{ij},\tilde{A}_{i})(\bm{p},\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega\cap\{h-2\varepsilon<x_{1}<h\})}\leq M\,.

(v*)

For each
(\bm{p},\bm{x})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\overline{\Omega}\cap\{h-\varepsilon<x_{1}<h\})
and
i,j=1,2,

define

\hat{\bm{p}}\coloneqq\bm{p}-Dg_{\rm so}(\bm{x})\,,\qquad(\tilde{a}_{ij},\tilde{a}_{i})(\hat{\bm{p}},\bm{x})\coloneqq(\tilde{A}_{ij},\tilde{A}_{i})(\hat{\bm{p}}+Dg_{\rm so}(\bm{x}),\bm{x})\,.

For any
(\bm{p},(x_{1},0))\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\Gamma_{3}\cap\{h-\varepsilon<x_{1}<h\}),

(\tilde{a}_{11},\tilde{a}_{22},\tilde{a}_{1})((\hat{p}_{1},-\hat{p}_{2}),(x_{1},0))=(\tilde{a}_{11},\tilde{a}_{22},\tilde{a}_{1})((\hat{p}_{1},\hat{p}_{2}),(x_{1},0))\,.

For any
(\bm{p},\bm{x})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(\Omega\cap\{h-\varepsilon<x_{1}<h\})
and
i=1,2,

\quad|\tilde{a}_{ii}(\hat{\bm{p}},\bm{x})-\tilde{a}_{ii}(Dg_{\rm so}(\bm{x}_{\rm e}),\bm{x}_{\rm e})|\leq M|\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{\rm e}|^{\beta},\quad(\tilde{A}_{12},\tilde{A}_{21})(\bm{p},\bm{x}_{\rm e})=0\qquad\mbox{for $\bm{x}_{\rm e}\coloneqq(h,x_{2})$}\,.

(vi*)

For any
\bm{p}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and

\bm{x}\in\Omega\cap\{h-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}<x_{1}<h\},

\,\,\tilde{A}_{1}(\bm{p},\bm{x})\leq-\lambda
.

Then there exist both
\alpha_{1}\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
depending only on
(\lambda,\delta)

and
\sigma\in(0,1)
depending only on
(\lambda,\delta,M,C_{\rm so},\alpha,\beta,\varepsilon)
such that,
under the further requirement
\delta\in[0,\delta_{0}),

the boundary value problem (
B.5
) has a unique
solution
u\in C(\overline{\Omega})\cap C^{1}(\overline{\Omega}\setminus(\overline{\Gamma_{2}}\cup\overline{\Gamma_{0}}))\cap C^{2}(\Omega)
.
Moreover,
u
satisfies

(B.8)

\|u\|_{0,\overline{\Omega}}\leq C\,,\qquad\,|u(\bm{x})-g_{\rm so}(\bm{x})|\leq C\min\{x_{1},h-x_{1}\}\quad\text{in $\Omega$}\,,

where
C>0
depends only on
(\lambda,\delta,M,C_{\rm so},\varepsilon)
.
Furthermore,

u\in C(\overline{\Omega})\cap C^{2,\alpha_{1}}(\overline{\Omega}\setminus\overline{\Gamma_{2}}\cup\overline{\Gamma_{0}})

satisfies

(B.9)

\|u\|_{2,\alpha_{1},\overline{\Omega\cap\{s<x_{1}<h-s\}}}\leq C_{s}

for each
s\in(0,\frac{h}{10}),
where
C_{s}>0
depends only
on
(\lambda,\delta,M,C_{\rm so},\alpha,\beta,\varepsilon,s)
.
In addition,
u
satisfies

\|u\|^{(-1-\alpha_{1}),\{P_{4}\}}_{2,\alpha_{1},\Omega\cap\{x_{1}<\frac{h}{4}\}}<\hat{C}\,,

for constant
\hat{C}>0
depending only on
(\lambda,\delta,M,\alpha,\varepsilon)
.

Proposition B.3
.

Fix constants
h,t_{1},t_{2},t_{3}\in(0,\infty)
and
t_{0}=t_{h}=0
. For constants
\lambda>0,

M<\infty,

\alpha\in(0,1),
and
C_{\rm so}>0,
domain
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{2}
satisfies
conditions (
B.1
)–(
B.4
) with changes
:

\displaystyle P_{3}=P_{4}=(0,0)\,,\quad\overline{\Gamma_{0}}=\{(0,0)\}\,;\qquad\,P_{2}=P_{1}=(h,0)\,,\quad\overline{\Gamma_{2}}=\{(h,0)\}\,.

For
\beta\in[\frac{1}{2},1),

\delta\in(0,1),

\sigma\in(0,1),
and
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{h}{10}),

the nonlinear boundary value problem (
B.5
) satisfies
conditions (
ii
)
,
(
iv
)
,

and (
vii
) above, and
(
i
*)
below
:

(i*)

For any
\bm{x}\in\Omega
and
\bm{p},\bm{\kappa}=(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2},

\displaystyle\min\big\{\lambda\,{\rm dist}(\bm{x},\overline{\Gamma_{0}})+\delta,\lambda\,{\rm dist}(x,\Gamma_{2})+\delta\big\}|\bm{\kappa}|^{2}\leq\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\tilde{A}_{ij}(\bm{p},\bm{x})\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}\leq\lambda^{-1}|\bm{\kappa}|^{2}\,,

\displaystyle\big\|((\tilde{A}_{ij},\tilde{A}_{i})(Dg_{\rm so},\cdot),D^{m}_{\bm{p}}(\tilde{A}_{ij},\tilde{A}_{i})(\bm{p},\cdot))\big\|^{(-\alpha),\{P_{1},P_{4}\}}_{1,\alpha,\Omega\setminus\{2\varepsilon<x_{1}<h-2\varepsilon\}}\leq M\qquad\text{for $m=1,2$}\,.

Then there exist constants
\alpha_{1}\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
depending only on
(\lambda,\delta),

and
\sigma\in(0,1)
depending only on
(\lambda,\delta,M,C_{\rm so},\alpha,\beta,\varepsilon)

such that, under the further requirement
\delta\in[0,\delta_{0}),

the boundary value problem (
B.5
) has a unique solution

u\in C(\overline{\Omega})\cap C^{1}(\overline{\Omega}\setminus(\overline{\Gamma_{2}}\cup\overline{\Gamma_{0}}))\cap C^{2}(\Omega)
.
Moreover,
u
satisfies

(B.10)

\|u\|_{0,\overline{\Omega}}\leq C\,,\qquad|u(\bm{x})-g_{\rm so}(\bm{x})|\leq C\min\{x_{1},h-x_{1}\}\quad\text{in $\Omega$}\,,

where

C>0
depends only on
(\lambda,\delta,M,C_{\rm so},\varepsilon)
.
Furthermore,

u\in C(\overline{\Omega})\cap C^{2,\alpha_{1}}(\overline{\Omega}\setminus\overline{\Gamma_{2}}\cup\overline{\Gamma_{0}})
satisfies

(B.11)

\|u\|_{2,\alpha_{1},\overline{\Omega\cap\{s<x_{1}<h-s\}}}\leq C_{s}

for each
s\in(0,\frac{h}{10}),
where
C_{s}>0
depends only
on
(\lambda,\delta,M,C_{\rm so},\alpha,\beta,\varepsilon,s)
. In addition,
u
satisfies

\|u\|^{(-1-\alpha_{1}),\{P_{1},P_{4}\}}_{2,\alpha_{1},\Omega\setminus\{\frac{h}{4}\leq x_{1}\leq\frac{3h}{4}\}}<\hat{C}\,,

for constant
\hat{C}>0
depending only on
(\lambda,\delta,M,\alpha,\varepsilon)
.

B.2.
Regularized distance function

Lemma B.4

(
[
10
, Lemmas 13.9.1]
)
.

For any
g(\cdot)\in C^{0,1}([-1,1])
that is positive on
(-1,1),

there exists a function
\delta_{g}\in C^{\infty}(\overline{R_{\infty}}\setminus\overline{R_{g}}),

the regularized distance, such that

(i)

|\delta_{g}(s,t^{\prime})-{\rm dist}((s,t^{\prime}),\Sigma_{g})|\leq\frac{1}{2}{\rm dist}(\mathbf{x},\Sigma_{g})\,\,

for all
(s,t^{\prime})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\overline{\Sigma_{g}}
;

(ii)

|D^{m}\delta_{g}(s,t^{\prime})|\leq C(m)\big({\rm dist}((s,t^{\prime}),\Sigma_{g})\big)^{1-m}

for all
(s,t^{\prime})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\overline{\Sigma_{g}}
and
m=1,2,\cdots,

where
C(m)>0
depends only on
m
;

(iii)

\delta_{g}(s,t^{\prime})\geq C_{\rm rd}(t^{\prime}-g(s))
for all
(s,t^{\prime})\in\overline{R_{g}}\setminus\overline{\Sigma_{g}},

where
C_{\rm rd}>0
depends only on
{\rm Lip}[g]
;

(iv)

Let
g_{k},g\in C^{0,1}([-1,1])
be uniformly bounded Lipschitz functions for all
k\in\mathbb{N},

with
g_{k}(s)
converging to
g(s)
uniformly on
[-1,1]
.
Then

\,\,\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}\|\delta_{g_{k}}(s,t^{\prime})-\delta_{g}(s,t^{\prime})\|_{C^{m}(K)}=0

for any
m=0,1,2,\cdots
,
and any compact set
K\subseteq\overline{R_{\infty}}\setminus\overline{R_{g}}
.

B.3.
Leray-Schauder degree theorem

Definition B.5

(Compact map)
.

Let
X
and
Y
be Banach spaces.
Fix any open subset
G\subseteq X
.
A map
\mathbf{f}:\overline{G}\to Y
is called compact if

(i)

\mathbf{f}
is continuous
;

(ii)

\mathbf{f}(U)
is precompact in
Y
for any bounded subset
U\subseteq\overline{G}
.

Definition B.6
.

Let
X
be a Banach space, and let
G
be a bounded open subset of
X
.
Write
V(G,X)
to denote the set of all maps
\mathbf{f}:\overline{G}\to X
satisfying

(i)

\mathbf{f}
is a compact map in the sense of
Definition
B.5
;

(ii)

\mathbf{f}
has no fixed point on the boundary of
\partial G
.

Definition B.7
.

Let
X
be a Banach space, and let
G
be a bounded open subset of
X
.
Two maps
\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}\in V(G,X)
are called compactly homotopic
on
\partial G
if there exists a map
\mathbf{H}
with the following properties
:

(i)

\mathbf{H}:\overline{G}\times[0,1]\to X
is a compact map
;

(ii)

\mathbf{H}(\mathbf{x},\tau)\neq\mathbf{x}
for all
(\mathbf{x},\tau)\in\partial G\times[0,1]
;

(iii)

\mathbf{H}(\mathbf{x},0)=\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})
and
\mathbf{H}(\mathbf{x},1)=\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{x})

for all
\mathbf{x}\in\overline{G}
.

We write
\partial G:\mathbf{f}\cong\mathbf{g}
if
\mathbf{f}
and
\mathbf{g}

are compactly homotopic on
\partial G
in the sense of
Definition
B.7
, and we call
\mathbf{H}
a compact homotopy.

Theorem B.1

(Leray-Schauder degree theorem)
.

Let
X
be a Banach space, and let
G
be a bounded open subset of
X
.
Then, for each map
\mathbf{f}\in V(G,X),
a unique integer
\mathbf{Ind}(\mathbf{f},G)

can be assigned with the following properties
:

(i)

If
\mathbf{f}(\cdot)\equiv\mathbf{x}_{0}
on
\overline{G}

for some fixed
\mathbf{x}_{0}\in G,
then
\mathbf{Ind}(\mathbf{f},G)=1
;

(ii)

If
\mathbf{Ind}(\mathbf{f},G)\neq 0,

then there exists a fixed point
\mathbf{x}\in G
of map
\mathbf{f},
that is,

\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})=\mathbf{x}
;

(iii)

\mathbf{Ind}(\mathbf{f},G)=\Sigma_{j=1}^{n}\mathbf{Ind}(\mathbf{f},G_{j}),

whenever
\mathbf{f}\in V(G,X)\cap(\cap_{j=1}^{n}V(G_{j},X)),

where
G_{i}\cap G_{j}=\varnothing
for
i\neq j
and
\overline{G}=\cup_{j=1}^{n}\overline{G_{j}}
;

(iv)

If
\partial G:\mathbf{f}\cong\mathbf{g},

then
\mathbf{Ind}(\mathbf{f},G)=\mathbf{Ind}(\mathbf{g},G)
.

Such a number
\mathbf{Ind}(\mathbf{g},G)
is called the fixed point index of
\mathbf{f}
over
G
.

A generalized homotopy invariance of the fixed point index is given by the following theorem.

Theorem B.2

(
[
48
, §13.6, A4*]
)
.

Let
X
be a Banach space, and let
t_{2}>t_{1}
.
Let
U\subseteq X\times[t_{1},t_{2}],
and let
U_{t}=\{\mathbf{x}\,:\,(\mathbf{x},t)\in U\}
.
Then, for any compact map
\mathbf{h}:\overline{U}\to X

with
\mathbf{h}(\mathbf{x},t)\neq\mathbf{x}
on
\partial U,

\mathbf{Ind}(\mathbf{h}(\cdot,t),U_{t})={\rm const}.\qquad\text{for all}\;t\in[t_{1},t_{2}]\,,

provided that
U
is a bounded open set in
X\times[t_{1},t_{2}]
.

B.4.
Regularity theorem

Theorem B.3

(
[
1
, Theorem 3.1]
)
.

For constants

r,\,R>0,
define
Q_{r,R}^{+}
by

Q^{+}_{r,R}\coloneqq\big\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\,:\,0<x<r,\,|y|<R\big\}\,.

For positive constants
a,\,b,\,M,\,N,
and
\kappa\in(0,\frac{1}{4}),

suppose that
\psi\in C(\overline{Q^{+}_{r,R}})\cap C^{2}(Q^{+}_{r,R})

satisfies
\psi>0,

-Mx\leq\psi_{x}\leq\frac{2-\kappa}{a}x,
and

(2x-a\psi_{x}+O_{1})\psi_{xx}+O_{2}\psi_{xy}+(b+O_{3})\psi_{yy}-(1+O_{4})\psi_{x}+O_{5}\psi_{y}=0\qquad\,\mbox{in $Q^{+}_{r,R}$}\,,

and
\psi=0
on
\partial Q^{+}_{r,R}\cap\{x=0\},

where
O_{k}(x,y),

k=1,\cdots,5,
are continuously differentiable and

\frac{|O_{1}(x,y)|}{x^{2}}+\frac{|D_{(x,y)}O_{1}(x,y)|}{x}+\sum\limits_{k=2}^{5}\Big(\frac{|O_{k}(x,y)|}{x}+|D_{(x,y)}O_{k}(x,y)|\Big)\leq N\qquad\mbox{in $Q^{+}_{r,R}$}\,.

Then
\psi\in C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{Q^{+}_{{r}/{2},{R}/{2}}})

for any
\alpha\in(0,1)
and

\displaystyle\psi_{xx}(0,y)=a^{-1}\,,\quad\,\,\psi_{xy}(0,y)=\psi_{yy}(0,y)=0\qquad\mbox{for all $y\in(-\frac{R}{2},\frac{R}{2})$}\,.

B.5.
General framework for the convexity of transonic shocks

We state a general framework for the transonic shock as a free boundary, developed in Chen-Feldman-Xiang
[
12
]
.

Let
\Omega
be an open, bounded, and connected set,
and
\partial\Omega=\Gamma_{{\rm shock}}\cup\Gamma_{1}\cup\Gamma_{2}
,
where the closed curve segment
\Gamma_{{\rm shock}}
is a transonic shock that separates
a pseudo-supersonic constant state denoted as state
(2)
outside
\Omega

from a pseudo-subsonic (non-constant) state inside
\Omega
, and

\Gamma_{1}\cup\Gamma_{2}
is a fixed boundary.

We now present a more structural framework for domain
\Omega
under consideration.

Framework
(A). The structural framework for domain
\Omega
:

(i)

Domain
\Omega
is bounded, with boundary
\partial\Omega
that is a continuous closed curve without self-intersections.
Furthermore,
\partial\Omega
is piecewise
C^{1,\alpha}
up to the endpoints of each smooth part for some
\alpha\in(0,1)
,
and the number of smooth parts is finite.

(ii)

At each corner point of
\partial\Omega
, angle
\theta
between the arcs meeting at that point
from the interior of
\Omega
satisfies
\theta\in(0,\pi)
.

(iii)

\partial\Omega=\Gamma_{{\rm shock}}\cup\Gamma_{1}\cup\Gamma_{2}
,
where
\Gamma_{{\rm shock}}
,
\Gamma_{1}
, and
\Gamma_{2}
are connected and disjoint,
and both
\Gamma_{{\rm shock}}^{0}
and
\Gamma_{1}\cup\Gamma_{2}
are non-empty.
Moreover, if
\Gamma_{i}\neq\varnothing
for some
i\in\{1,2\}
,
then its relative interior is nonempty,
i.e.
,
\Gamma_{i}^{0}\neq\varnothing
.

(iv)

\Gamma_{{\rm shock}}
includes its endpoints
A
and
B
with corresponding unit tangent vectors

\bm{\tau}_{A}
and
\bm{\tau}_{B}
pointing to the interior of
\Gamma_{{\rm shock}}
, respectively.
If
\Gamma_{1}\neq\varnothing
, then
A
is a common endpoint of
\Gamma_{{\rm shock}}

and
\Gamma_{1}
. If
\Gamma_{2}\neq\varnothing
,
then
B
is a common endpoint of
\Gamma_{{\rm shock}}
and
\Gamma_{2}
.

Let
\phi=\varphi-\varphi_{2}
.
Then
\phi
satisfies the following equation:

(B.12)

(c^{2}-\varphi_{\xi_{1}}^{2})\phi_{\xi_{1}\xi_{1}}-2\varphi_{\xi_{1}}\varphi_{\xi_{2}}\phi_{\xi_{1}\xi_{2}}+(c^{2}-\varphi_{\xi_{2}}^{2})\phi_{\xi_{2}\xi_{2}}=0\qquad\text{in $\Omega$}\,,

with the boundary conditions:

(B.13)

\phi=0\,,\quad\rho(|D\phi+D\varphi_{2}|^{2},\phi+\varphi_{2})D(\phi+\varphi_{2})\cdot\bm{\nu}=\rho_{2}D\varphi_{2}\cdot\bm{\nu}\qquad\,\,\text{on $\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}$}\,.

If
\bm{\tau}_{A}\neq\pm\bm{\tau}_{B}
, define the cone:

(B.14)

{\rm Con}\coloneqq\{r\bm{\tau}_{A}+s\bm{\tau}_{B}\,:\,r,\,s>0\}\,.

Theorem B.4

(
[
12
, Theorem 2.1]
)
.

Assume that domain
\Omega
satisfies
Framework (A)
.
Assume that
\phi\in C^{1}(\overline{\Omega})\cap C^{2}(\Omega\cup\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}^{0})\cap C^{3}(\Omega)

is a solution of (
B.12
)–(
B.13
)
,
which is not a constant state in
\Omega
.
Moreover, let
\phi
satisfy the following conditions
:

(C-1)

The entropy condition holds
across
\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}
:

\rho(|D\varphi|^{2},\varphi)>\rho_{2}

and
\phi_{\bm{\nu}}<0
along
\Gamma_{\rm{shock}},
where
\bm{\nu}
is the unit normal vector on
\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}
pointing to
\Omega
;

(C-2)

There exist constants
C_{1}>0
and
\alpha_{1}\in(0,1)

such that
\|\phi\|_{1+\alpha_{1},\overline{\Omega}}\leq C_{1}
;

(C-3)

Equation (
B.12
) is strictly elliptic in
\Omega\cup\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}^{0}
:

c^{2}-|D\varphi|^{2}>0
in
\Omega\cup\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}^{0}
;

(C-4)

\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}
is
C^{2}
in its relative interior
;

(C-5)

\bm{\tau}_{A}\neq\pm\bm{\tau}_{B},
and
\{P+{\rm Con}\}\cap\Omega=\varnothing
for any point
P\in\overline{\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}}
;

(C-6)

There exists a vector
\bm{e}\in{\rm Con}
such that one of the following conditions holds
:

(C-
6
a)

\Gamma_{1}\neq\varnothing,
and the directional derivative
\phi_{\bm{e}}
cannot have
a local maximum point on
\Gamma_{1}^{0}\cup\{A\}
and a local minimum point on
\Gamma_{2}^{0}
;

(C-
6
b)

\Gamma_{2}\neq\varnothing,
and
\phi_{\bm{e}}
cannot have a local minimum point
on
\Gamma_{1}^{0}
and a local maximum point on
\Gamma_{2}^{0}\cup\{B\}
;

(C-
6
c)

\phi_{\bm{e}}
cannot have a local minimum point on
\Gamma_{1}\cup\Gamma_{2}
;

where all the local maximum or minimum points are relative to
\overline{\Omega}
.

Then the free boundary
\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}
is a convex graph in each direction
\bm{e}\in{\rm Con}
.
That is, there exists a concave function
f\in C^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{R})
in the orthogonal coordinate system
\bm{\xi}\eqqcolon S\bm{e}+T\bm{e}^{\perp}

such that

(B.15)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}=\big\{\bm{\xi}(S,T)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\,:\,S=f(T),\,T_{A}<T<T_{B}\big\}\,,

\displaystyle\Omega\cap\big\{\bm{\xi}\,:\,T_{A}<T<T_{B}\big\}\subseteq\big\{\bm{\xi}\,:\,S<f(T)\big\}\,.

Moreover, the free boundary
\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}
is strictly convex in its relative interior in the sense that,
if
P=\bm{\xi}(S,T)\in\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}^{0}
and
f^{\prime\prime}(T)=0,

then there exists an integer
m>1,
independent of the choice of
\bm{e}\in{\rm Con}
such that,
for any
n=2,\cdots,2m-1,

(B.16)

f^{(n)}(T)=0\,,\qquad f^{(2m)}(T)<0\,.

The number of the points at which
f^{\prime\prime}(T)=0
is at most finite on each compact subset of
\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}^{0}
.
In particular, the free boundary
\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}
cannot contain any straight segment.

Furthermore, under some additional assumptions, we can show that the shock curve is uniformly convex
in its relative interior in the sense defined in the following theorem:

Theorem B.5

(
[
12
, Theorem 2.3]
)
.

Let
\Omega
and
\phi
be as in
Theorem
B.4
.
Furthermore, assume that, for any unit vector
\bm{e}\in\mathbb{R}^{2},

the boundary part
\Gamma_{1}\cup\Gamma_{2}
can be further decomposed so that

(C-7)

\Gamma_{1}\cup\Gamma_{2}=\hat{\Gamma}_{0}\cup\hat{\Gamma}_{1}\cup\hat{\Gamma}_{2}\cup\hat{\Gamma}_{3},

where some
\hat{\Gamma}_{i}
may be empty,
\hat{\Gamma}_{i}
is connected for each
i=0,1,2,3,

and all curves
\hat{\Gamma}_{i}
are located along
\partial\Omega
in the order of their indices,
i.e., non-empty sets
\hat{\Gamma}_{j}
and

\hat{\Gamma}_{k},

k>j,
have a common endpoint if and only
if either
k=j+1
or
\hat{\Gamma}_{i}=\varnothing
for all
i=j+1,\cdots,k-1
.
Also, the non-empty set
\hat{\Gamma}_{i}
with the smallest
(
resp. largest
)
index has
the common endpoint
A

(
resp.
B
)
with
\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}
.
Moreover, if
\hat{\Gamma}_{i}\neq\varnothing
for some
i=0,1,2,3,

then its relative interior is nonempty
:

\hat{\Gamma}_{i}^{0}\neq\varnothing
;

(C-8)

\phi_{\bm{e}}
is constant along
\hat{\Gamma}_{0}
and
\hat{\Gamma}_{3}
;

(C-9)

For
i=1,2,
if
\phi_{\bm{e}}
attains its local minimum or maximum relative
to
\overline{\Omega}
on
\hat{\Gamma}_{i}^{0},
then
\phi_{\bm{e}}
is constant along
\hat{\Gamma}_{i}
;

(C-10)

One of the following two conditions holds
:

(C-
10
a)

Either
\hat{\Gamma}_{1}=\varnothing
or
\hat{\Gamma}_{2}=\varnothing
;

(C-
10
b)

Both
\hat{\Gamma}_{1}
and
\hat{\Gamma}_{2}
are non-empty, and
\hat{\Gamma}_{3}=\varnothing,

so that
\hat{\Gamma}_{2}
has the common endpoint
B
with
\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}
.
At point
B,
the following conditions hold
:

(1*)

If
\bm{\nu}_{\rm{sh}}(B)\cdot\bm{e}<0,
then
\phi_{\bm{e}}
cannot attain
its local maximum relative to
\overline{\Omega}
at
B
;

(2*)

If
\bm{\nu}_{\rm{sh}}(B)\cdot\bm{e}=0,
then
\phi_{\bm{e}}(B)=\phi_{\bm{e}}(Q^{*})

for the common endpoint
Q^{*}
of
\hat{\Gamma}_{1}
and
\hat{\Gamma}_{2}
;

where
\bm{\nu}_{\rm{sh}}(B)\coloneqq\lim\limits_{\Gamma^{0}_{\rm{shock}}\ni P\rightarrow B}\bm{\nu}(P),

which exists since
\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}
is
C^{1}
up to
B
.

Then the shock function
f(T)
in (
B.15
) satisfies
f^{\prime\prime}(T)<0
for all

T\in(T_{A},T_{B}),
that is,
\Gamma_{\rm{shock}}
is uniformly convex on closed subsets of
its relative interior.
